The site cites this row BY NAME rather than by line number | Port-Doctrine (the port's identity is not DFU's); recorded AUDIT 58, records lane |
| **THE MUSIC REPLACEMENT FOLDER IS A USER PICK, AND ITS EXTENSIONS ARE A SET (AUDIT 58, seams lane)** - `src/systems/musicReplacement.js`. `SoundReplacement.TryImportSong` (SoundReplacement.cs:59-62) asks `TryImportAudioClip(song.ToString(), ".ogg", true, ...)` and that helper builds ONE path, `Path.Combine(soundPath, name + extension)` (:87), under `StreamingAssets/Sound`. Two halves of that are unportable as written. (a) THERE IS NO STREAMINGASSETS in a browser, so the "folder" is a pick the user makes, held the way ARENA2 is, and the lookup runs over that SET rather than over a path - the shape the callers depend on (name in, bytes or nothing out) is untouched. (b) EXTENSIONS ARE A SET: `MUSIC_EXTENSIONS` is ogg, mp3, m4a, flac, wav, tried in that fixed PREFERENCE order so a folder carrying one song twice still answers deterministically. DFU's single extension is a Unity WWW-loader constraint, not a law about the format; restricting to `.ogg` would reject most real music packs for no gain, and a browser decodes whatever its codec set covers. THE GATE STAYS DFU'S OWN - `Settings.AssetInjection`, both import paths' first line - including that the built-in song still plays when the answer is no: a replacement is an override, never a requirement | OURS in the two places the host forces, DFU's everywhere else. The site cites this row BY NAME rather than by line number | Port-Doctrine (no filesystem; the host's codec set is the host's); recorded AUDIT 58, seams lane |
| **THE FUZZY FIND COMPUTES IN DOUBLES AND COLLATES ORDINALLY (AUDIT 58, seams lane)** - `src/systems/editDistance.js`. EditDistance.cs/Distance.cs/DaggerfallDistance.cs (MIT, Daggerfall Workshop) are ported verbatim in every law that decides a match - the cost table, the four paddings, the row/future_row DP with its upperBound early-out (:83-146), relevance = `exp(-0.1 * distance)` (:213-217) - and depart in two arithmetic seams the host owns. (a) C# COMPUTES IN FLOAT32; JS has one number type, and the house convention is no `Math.fround` anywhere in `src/` (the same call the FaceUVTool row above makes), so a tie float32 rounding would have broken can land the other way. Nothing the window shows depends on it: the relevance cutoff is a `>=` test and both sides of an exact tie are kept. (b) TWO COLLATIONS ARE ORDINAL where C# is culture-sensitive - `SetDictionary`'s `SortedDictionary<string,string>` iterates in the CURRENT CULTURE's order, observable only once the heap starts dropping (which it does: the window passes ntop 1000 and Daggerfall alone holds 1331 locations), and `CompareTo`'s tie-break `string.Compare(other, this, InvariantCulture)` (:37-46), which IS the output order, so a group of equal-relevance names differing only by an apostrophe or a hyphen can reach the list picker in a different order than DFU's | OURS, and said out loud at the site rather than implied by a 'verbatim' header. The site cites this row BY NAME | Port-Doctrine (one number type; the port does not carry a culture); recorded AUDIT 58, seams lane |

| **THE KEYBINDING STANDARD (KB1, 2026-09-23, Mac: "We have a lot of mods, a lot of keybinds. I really want to formalize a solid solution")** - `src/systems/inputActions.js` (`ACTIONS`, `DEFAULT_BINDINGS`, `MOD_ACTIONS`, `ACTION_GROUPS`, `HIDDEN_ACTIONS`, `actionLive`, `migrateKeyBinds`), `src/ui/input.js`, the three controls windows, `bible/10-UI/Controls.md`. ONE REGISTRY owns every world key: DFU's actions, the port's (the F-menu, the quickslots, the plaque, the mouse toggle, `Interact`, the pixel dial as `QuickDial`, the hotbar's slots 5-10 - slots 1-4 ARE the diamond's four), and every VENDORED MOD'S keys, which each mod read off its own TextKey where no binding table could see them (Handheld Torches' three, Eye Of The Beholder's two, Travel Options' follow key, Horse Cart and Cargo's two). DEPARTURES FROM DFU'S TABLE, Mac's calls: `ToggleConsole` (no console here) and `Slide` (DFU reads it nowhere) ship UNBOUND and off the Controls page, freeing `` ` `` and Left Ctrl; `AbortSpell` moves off `E` onto `` ` ``; `CenterView` (Home) and `PrintScreen` (F8), which DFU binds and reads nowhere, are BUILT - Home levels the view through the look filter, F8 saves the canvas as a PNG; online, `Enter` (ActivateCursor) opens the chat and does not free the mouse, which is `Y` (FreeMouse); offline Enter frees it as DFU's does; the dungeon's diagnostics readout, a raw F8 that answered only while F8 was unbound, is the `DebugOverlay` action, unbound. LAWS: one key, one action (no default twice); a key another action holds is ASKED for in every controls window (the replace prompt), never silently taken; every world key is read through the registry, live; a mod switched off answers nothing on its keys (`actionLive`, at the one reader every key goes through) and they stay bound for when it is on; a window's own keys (Escape, Enter, arrows, its letters) are the window's while it is up; the enhanced page's Defaults is STAGED as its own sentence says (DFU's SetDefaults saved on the spot). A saved file is carried forward ONCE (`version` 2): the three moved DFU defaults let go where they still stood, the hidden two let go of any key, the standard's defaults land, and only then is a mod key the player SAVED carried into its action, onto a key still free - what could not be carried is told to the player on the HUD. | **ENHANCED WATER (WATER1, 2026-09-08)** - `render/waterSurface.js`, `drawWaterSurface` in `render/renderer.js`, the slot in both exterior hosts, `world/cityNavigation.js`. WHAT DFU DRAWS: water is record 0 of the terrain tileset and `Daggerfall/TilemapTextureArray` draws it as it draws dirt - one opaque, unmoving, Lambert-lit layer (the port's `TERRAIN_FS`, verbatim); classic palette-cycled the tile. Mac: "develop proper water shader for the oceans/rivers/ponds of daggerfall." THE DEPARTURE, enhanced skin only, switch `enhancedWater` (on by default, `?water=off` the kill switch): a second pass over the pixel's OWN terrain grid, lifted a hand's breadth and alpha-blended over the ground, whose fragment shader discards every texel that is not water and shades the rest as a surface - three wave trains on the eased wind (the vector the cloud deck and the mills take), the sky reflected by Schlick's Fresnel (the dome's own zenith and horizon this frame), the sun's and the moon's glints, the cloud deck's shadow (VC4's recorded gap on the water, closed), rain pocking it with the front's intensity, the classic water texel underneath, tinted and lit by the ground's own light law, and the shore feathered along the marching squares' diagonal off a 256-entry water-corner table that inverts `createLookupTable` (the river painter's records and their water-grass / water-stone twins included). The classic lane and the terrain pass are untouched; nothing asserts a water surface height to the game (DFU has none outdoors, `player/exteriorSurface.js`). FOUND ON THE WAY, a parity defect fixed at its source: `generateTileData` compared the double product fround(27.2 / 1539) * 1539 = 27.20000077 against the double 27.2, where C#'s float32 rounds the product back to 27.2f - so through this port's job an open ocean was 0 water corners in 16641 - and since the clamped float is the least value the sampler can store, NO corner in any pixel, land or sea, was ever water: every sea tile drawn was the beach band's dirt, every lake and shoreline transition unreachable, the only exterior water a town's own sentinel tiles; the compare is float32's now, the beach jitter and the nature compare with it, and the shared constant is the float (`test/terrain.test.js`). AUDITED the same day (WATER-AUDIT, two adversarial lanes, on the arc page): the wave trains by rotation, the point lights on the water, the water's own index set, the fixed city's real extent, the corner table pinned through the producer. Seen in the water lab (`water.html`, 10/10 in `tools/waterProbe.mjs`), not on a real GPU or with ARENA2. WATER-NPC (2026-09-15, Mac from live play: "NPCs arent water aware and will walk into it"): the same switch now widens the TOWNSFOLK NAVGRID. DFU's `CityNavigation.GetTileWeight` gives weight 0 to seven records because its `TileTypes` enum knows Water and TWO edges per shore family (`WaterDirtEdge1/2`, `WaterGrassEdge1/2`, `WaterStoneEdge1/2`) - but each family has FOUR members, the three-corner and the saddle among them, and the shallow-whole records belong to none of them; DFU contradicts itself about this, since its own `PlayerMotor.OnShallowWaterTile` (:551-563) wades the player through records 8, 23, 33-36 and 49 that its townsfolk walk dry-shod. Twelve records - 7, 8, 22, 23, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36, 48, 49, 50 - were water to the draw and dry land to the pathing. The enhanced arm asks the port's OWN water table (`world/waterCorners.js`, the one the surface draws from and MAC2 swims the player by), so the picture, the physics and now the pathing cannot disagree by construction: wherever the enhanced pass draws water, a wandering NPC will not walk into it. The classic lane keeps DFU's seven exactly, the switch is `waterSwitchOn()` in both exterior hosts, and the family is DERIVED from the corner table rather than enumerated - pinned through the producer in `test/waternpc.test.js`. WATER-PUDDLE (2026-09-25, Mac: "fixing any and all issues (especially with ingame puddles and tiles in towns that are one square)"): the records the corner table calls whole without knowing their shape - DFU's shallow-water records 8, 23, 33-36 (the docks, moats and puddles) and WATER-DRAW1's record 9 - were drawn as a full 6.4 m square of water over a pool painted in the art, the lone squares in the towns (a census of every RMB block found only two lone record-0 tiles, DFU's own). The pass now draws them where their OWN TEXELS are water - WATER5's colour rule (within 24 of a water-tile colour) for these seven records alone, cleaned into shapes and carried in the alpha of the record's layer of the ground tile array (`world/puddleMask.js`), read through the tile's turn; the grass placer asks the same mask; the feet keep DFU's whole tile (WATER-DRAW1's split). WATER-DRAW1's "record 9 is water art" was read off a screenshot and is wrong for most climates: on the art it has no water at all in the desert, the woods or any winter set, a strip in the mountains, a fifth of the tile in the swamp - pinned on ARENA2 in `test/waterpuddle.test.js`. Recorded in `07-Rendering/Water-Arc.md`. |
| **ENHANCED LIGHTING (EL1, 2026-09-17)** - `render/enhancedLighting.js`, `setLightingLane` / `_buildWorldSet` / `_installWorldSet` in `render/renderer.js`, the lane in `render/farRing.js`, the one call in the four scene hosts. WHAT DFU DRAWS: Unity's forward lighting in gamma space with no tonemap - the port's classic lane, verbatim in shape (Lambert, sixteen point lights on a squared-linear falloff, ambient-plus-half-sun flats, emission cancelling other light). Mac, on Better Ambience's dungeon fog: "enhance our lighting system tenfold" - tiers 1, 2 and 3. THE DEPARTURE, enhanced skin only, switch `enhancedLighting` (on by default, `?lighting=classic` the kill switch, `?exposure=` the tuning door): the renderer's mesh, billboard, terrain and character fragment shaders and the far ring's REPLACED as a set by the lane's - every texel and every scene colour decoded from sRGB so light adds in linear, lanterns on a windowed inverse square (`2 / (1 + 16 x^2) (1 - x^4)^2`, zero at the range) and forty-eight of them, the city's and dungeon's white lanterns given a ~1900 K flame at the host's intensity, exposure 1.4 then an extended Reinhard at white 4 (0.12 -> 0.145, 0.5 -> 0.52, 1.0 -> 0.82 on the classic scale), the analytic single scattering of each lantern along the view ray windowed to its range and scaled by the fog's own density, the fog colour blended decoded and re-encoded so a fogged fragment is bit-for-bit the sky's. The classic shaders are untouched to the byte and a classic page never compiles the lane; the cap is the installed set's, read by every host. Seams left to the later tiers: the water surface and both skies still light in display space beside the lane. Pinned in `test/el1_enhancedlighting.test.js`; the uniform sweep reads the lane's file. EL2 (same day): `render/shadowPass.js` - the world pass's draws recorded and replayed depth-only from the light at the next frame's start, a two-cascade sun map outdoors and a cube map from the nearest lantern indoors, read by the lane's shaders with PCF (DFU draws Unity's shadows, which the classic lane never had; the lane's are the departure's own, not a port of Unity's cascades); casters the frame did not draw, the character rigs, the water and the moon are recorded limits (`test/el2_shadows.test.js`). EL3 (same day): `render/airPass.js` - a depth image from the camera off the same records, ambient occlusion on the lane's ambient term, bloom sourced from the emitters and a glare per lantern the depth does not hide, the sun's shafts, composited by the first screen draw; the scene forward-tonemapped (`test/el3_air.test.js`). EL4 (same day, Mac: "Proper darker dungeons"): the frame-target law in `render/renderTarget.js`, the world drawn into a frame image and resolved once with bloom from its bright pass, a vignette and contrast; eye adaptation off the frame's mean luminance, opening slowly into the dark and closing fast into the light, clamped so the dark stays dark; the dungeon ambient scaled to 0.35 under the lane (DFU's 0.12 flat and the mod's trilight alike - the departure's own darkness, the Dungeon Brightness setting still on top); a glint from every lantern (`test/el4_frame.test.js`). `?air=off`. AUDIT-EL (same day): twenty findings fixed and pinned (`test/audit_el.test.js`) - the terrain lost under `?air=off`, the travel map never resolved, the light cap never reaching the shader, the flats edge-on in the depth image, the shade black, the fog wall bright, the biases in the wrong space, the eye measuring itself, and twelve more; the castle's darkening a recorded judgement. NOT SEEN ON A GPU. The arc's four tiers are shipped and audited on `07-Rendering/Enhanced-Lighting-Arc.md`. EL5 THE FIELD (2026-09-17, the first report from the game: a town gate at night, lanterns glaring through the walls, the framerate): the glare's occlusion in world units (`AIR_GLARE_SLACK`), every replay culled by bounding spheres (`render/bounds.js`: `boundsOf` at `createMesh` per mesh and sub-mesh, per terrain surface, per billboard batch; `spherePlanes` over frustum.js), four lantern casters (`SHADOW_POINT_CASTERS`) in one depth array with the face selected by hand (`faceBasis`, `shadowOfLight`), the resolve's contrast in display space; `tools/enhancedLightingProbe.mjs` on a real WebGL2 context. test/el5_field.test.js, tools/mutants/el5.json. EL6 (2026-09-17, Mac's four: campfire shadows, dark textures, lights through walls, performance): `SHADOW_LIGHT_FLATS` (a flame casts from the sun, never from a lantern), the AO's ordered rotation and `DITHER_GLSL` at both encodes, `AIR_EMIT_SLACK` (emitters occluded by the frame's depth), `AirPass.prepare` + `_images` at the resolve off the frame's depth texture (no camera replay; the AO the resolve's, `AIR_AO_RESOLVE`), the scatter loop's early-out, six casters. EL7 (2026-09-17, #7 and the ball): `AIR_GLARE_SLACK` as presence (a flame under the glare) and `AIR_GLARE_MIN_DISTANCE` (none in the hand), `SHADOW_CASCADES` 12/48/240 with `uSunTexel`, `recordCharacter` (the rigs cast, never from the sprite or studio passes), `waterSurfaceProgramLane` (the water receives), the depth-aware AO blur, `glslFloat`. EL8 (2026-09-17, contact and cadence): `AIR_CONTACT_GLSL` / `contactShadow` (every lantern without a caster slot, off the previous frame's depth - two depth textures on the frame, `uPrevVP`), `SHADOW_CASTER_TABLE` / `uCasterOf`, `SHADOW_FAR_CASCADE_EVERY` / `SHADOW_FAR_CASTER_EVERY` / `SHADOW_NEAR_CASTERS` (the cadence, immediate when a slot's light changes), `render/perfMeter.js` (`?perf`). BUGS-5 (2026-09-17, the field's five): `SHADOW_CASTER_MIN_DISTANCE` 1.5 (the hand's light, shared with the lit block's march gate), the self check in `AIR_CONTACT_GLSL`, `SHADOW_NO_CAST_ARCHIVES` / `SHADOW_FLAT_MIN_HEIGHT` / a batch's `noShadow`, `AIR_GLARE_SLACK` 0.25, `SHADOW_CASCADE_MIN_RADIUS_TEXELS` / `replay`'s `minRadius`, `AIR_CONTACT_STEPS` 4 / `AIR_CONTACT_RANGE_FRACTION`. |

The collider reads the SAMPLES and the carve never touches them: the player swims where DFU swims. Enhanced skin only, under WATER1's switch and kill door; the classic lane draws the flat tile. `test/water2.test.js`; `07-Rendering/Water-Arc.md` WATER2. WATER3 (the same day): THE SWELL AND THE FOAM - the vertex rides the height whose gradient the fragment's three trains already were (A / k, the same phase and fade, scaled down over a bed shallower than `SWELL_DEPTH` so the sheet never lifts off its shoreline), and the fragment foams the shore (the last `FOAM_DEPTH` of bed, wider on a wind, a value-noise lace drawn along the wind) and the crests a strong wind breaks, lit white by the ground's light and opaque. `test/water3.test.js`. |
| **THE LOOK'S FLOOR (MAC1, 2026-09-10 - Mac's play report)** - `src/player/lookFilter.js` (`PITCH_FLOOR`). Mac: "Looking straight down and moving is jarring. Add a stopper so the player's camera can not go down past a certain threshold." DFU's PlayerMouseLook clamps the pitch at 90 degrees both ways (PitchMinLimit / PitchMaxLimit); the port keeps the reference CEILING (`PITCH_LIMIT`, MW-D30's +/-(PI/2 - e)) and takes the owner's FLOOR at 75 degrees below the horizon, applied where the reference applies its clamp - to the look filter's TARGET - so a pitch restored from a save below the floor glides up to it. The number is Mac's to tune. The same report's other calls are presentation, not law, and carry no row: the far-flat rule (`world/flatDistance.js` - the two nearest rings draw every flat, the far rings the trees and the flats that move), the render eye's step smoothing (`PlayerMotor._smoothEyeFeet`, the simulation eye untouched), the raw pointer deltas (`requestLook`'s unadjustedMovement) and the resume gesture's relock (`ui/pauseDoor.js`). | OURS, by the owner's call; the ceiling stays the reference's | Mac, 2026-09-10 (MAC1); pinned in `test/mac1_playreport.test.js` |
| THE FOUR PLAYERWEAPON RIGS never share state at a transition (AUDIT 65 SL-2) | DFU has ONE WeaponManager for every WorldContext, and SerializablePlayer.cs:175-176/:420-421 read and write sheath and hand off it wherever the save is taken. The port mints FOUR independent rigs - world.js's, worldModes' `interiorWeapon` (worldModes.js:682), dungeonContext's and exterior.js's - and nothing hands the pair between them at a mode transition: drawing a weapon in the street and walking into a shop still finds the interior rig sheathed and right-handed, with no save involved. AUDIT 65 SL-2 closed the SAVE half only (world.js's composer asks the mode host for the rig actually in the player's hands, and the restore lands the pair in every rig). The transition half is open by decision; the one-manager shape is the next slice through worldModes.js | Structural (AUDIT 65, the saveload refuters' condition on SL-2) |
| THE CORPSE'S INFO ARM IS UNPORTED, so a too-far body in Info mode speaks the refusal (AUDIT 65 MC-2) | `PlayerActivate.cs:928-934` speaks "You see a dead X" (`youSeeADead`) in Info mode and returns BEFORE the distance gate; the port has no `youSeeADead`, so since MC-2 handed the corpse family to the one ray, a body past 3.75 units in Info mode speaks "You are too far away." where DFU names the body - silence replaced by the wrong line, with the click's CONSUMPTION newly correct. Stands until `youSeeADead` is ported (the activation lane did not widen into it) | Structural (AUDIT 65 MC-2, its refuters' Ledger condition) |

| ONLINE (ONLINE1, 2026-09-12) | Daggerfall Unity has no multiplayer. The port's own: every player runs their own world from their own save and PRESENCE is shared (and, since WORLD1, a world room's memory) - a Cloudflare relay (`server/`, one Durable Object per room, storing nothing past the connection until WORLD1) fans each player's pose to the peers in range (`src/net/online.js` the session, `src/net/remotePlayers.js` the others drawn); the streaming world sharded into 16-pixel cells with a 3-pixel range, towns, dungeons and interiors rooms by location; every peer drawn as their PAPERDOLL (the look travels once: race, gender, face, the equipped items' doll fields; the pose its move bit, its drawn flag and its swing - MAC7) at their feet with their name over the head; the front door's ONLINE brings the most recent save in. MWBODY1 (2026-09-12) put every peer in the Morrowind body on the enhanced skin (`src/net/peerBodies.js`, one rig per peer); CHAT1 (2026-09-12) added the live chat - `src/net/chat.js` the log, `src/ui/chatPanel.js` the enhanced skin's panel, one World tab on a `chat:world` channel of the same relay (a channel keeps no roster and fans every line to everyone), audited before its merge (AUDIT CHAT: the whitelist, the room budget, the sanitizer's classes, the cursor key, the pointer lock). WORLD1 (2026-09-12, Mac: "The world is the server and every player should inhabit that world ... True persistance") began the persistent shared world: the relay names a HOST per room (the socket in it longest, said in the welcome and in a host frame) and keeps a dungeon's world - the layout's foes, actions and locks, never the quest owner's foes and (since WORLD4) no untouched container's contents - as the host's snapshot in the Durable Object's storage, chunked, handed raw to every joiner in the welcome and outliving an empty room; the host publishes every 15 s and on every farewell. No player-versus-player, no shared clock or weather; until WORLD2 the live simulation was still each player's own (the memory a snapshot, not the moment). AUDIT WORLD (2026-09-12) audited MAC6, MAC7, OD1 and WORLD1 before their merge - 36 findings fixed: the relay's unmetered world door, the prefix's cap bypass, the parked worlds (a map-id key, an alarm that forgets an empty room's memory after WORLD_TTL_MS), the reconnect's lost seat; the host's own memory back on a reconnect, the layout's run, the drops and the species in the shared snapshot, the dungeon's silent death; the arm read off the wrong rig underground, the sheathed cast, the refused strike, the bow's double loose; the pins that could not fail and the record's stale sentences. WORLD2 (2026-09-12, Mac: "Lets continue on with the next phase") made the layout's foes ONE simulation per room: the host steps them and streams every changed one a few times a second ({t:'foes'}, its own bucket at the relay), a joiner's layout foes are PUPPETS that mirror the stream (eased feet, the walk, the attack clip once per count, the hurt, death through the one kill door), a joiner's blows go to the host as hits ({t:'hit'}, to the host's socket alone) through the host's own damage door, and the seat's handover makes the puppets live (the motor's resumeLive). Until WORLD3 the host's foes targeted the host alone. AUDIT WORLD2 (2026-09-12) audited it: the seat and the stream (a handover between two others froze a third, a dead socket froze a joiner for ever, no watchdog on a silent host, the room hold on a dungeon's edge), the relay's budgets (the fan by bytes times listeners, the hit funnel, the type's bucket, the free ingress sink), the puppets' senses for the rest gate, the species on the stream, the striker's HUD and the host's for a peer's blow, a foe's spell as the player's, the corpse where the foe fell. WORLD3 (2026-09-12, Mac: "Begin") made the live world events: a dungeon's doors, levers and platforms move for everyone ({t:'act'} - the action records an entry changed, from whoever touched them, landed and heard on every other graph), the host's foes hunt every player in the room (the peers ride the target machine as candidates off the pose stream; the stream says whom each foe hunts and what it cast), a puppet resolves the host's foe's blows against its own player (the melee frame, the arrow, the spell - its own reach and stats), the roster is the room's (a foe whose species the memory or the stream disagrees with is rebuilt at its index), and the hit carries the striker's feet and the blow's direction. Loot is slice 4. AUDIT WORLD3 (2026-09-13) audited it - six opus lenses, sixteen findings confirmed and six more upheld: ONE ROOT above all (WORLD3 made a peer count as a player so it would pass every targeting gate, and four guards elsewhere spelled "mine" the same way - so a foe that saw only the joiner held the host's alert, refused its rest and killed it by exhaustion, aimed its sight ray at the host's capsule, was blinded by the host's invisibility, spent the host's language check and rolled the host's Stealth); the relay's act fan given the byte budget the foes fan has and its records projected at the receiving end (a non-numeric tween bricked a door for the room, permanently, from one 146-byte frame); the picker's retry latch kept off the wire; a refused act healed; the peers kept off the candidate list of foes the stream does not carry; a puppet un-blinded by the host's word (a joiner was invulnerable to a passive-marker foe); the seat's off direction; the roster's rebuild taken by the save path, re-landing its record, guarded by the build chain's own test and by the teardown latch; the striker riding every kind of blow, and its direction normalised (an unbounded one froze the host's tab from 85 bytes). WORLD4 (2026-09-13, Mac: "Lets start on slice 4") made a dungeon's LOOT the room's: a container nobody has opened is each client's own roll and the memory carries nothing for it, and the moment anyone opens one it becomes the room's - claimed on the open, said again on the close, landed in place (except on a container you have open, which is yours until you close it - AUDIT WORLD4 C1), its flat freed for whoever is standing beside it, and carried by the memory from then on. It rides WORLD3's act frame as a second half beside the doors, so it needed no relay change, no frame of its own and no budget of its own; a container's list off the wire is clamped by `src/systems/loot.js` the way an action record is. A dropped pile is still the dropper's. AUDIT WORLD4 (2026-09-13) audited it - four opus lenses, fifteen findings: ONE ROOT, that both of the slice's caps (64 items in a container's word, the wire's frame bytes) were enforced at the far end only, so an over-full container was sent and dropped in silence, then refused by the sender for its size and fed to the refused-act heal, which re-read and re-refused it every frame and pinned every later door behind it, and then wiped by the next reader's claim - `actFrameFits` is the wire's law with one home and the record's mint is the one home of what may be said; an `enchantments` string surviving the clamp and throwing out of the frame body through three readers, which froze the tab for good; the room's word landing under an open loot window, where the pack binds each row to the item object and never repaints, making two of one item; a claim un-emptying a chest for everyone inside the memory's publish window, and the memory now falling due the moment a container is claimed; a refilled pile with no batch, invisible and un-openable for ever; an uncanonical container key, by which one peer could grow the room's memory until it could not be published; a claim for a container a transformed lycanthrope never opened; the foes half of the memory carrying every layout corpse's items (the same container vocabulary, carried twice and unprojected); and a `piles` field still applied though no longer sent. The recovered-arrows cost of per-close takes is recorded, not paid. AUDIT WORLD34 (2026-09-13, Mac: "enemies, doors, and everything else doesnt persist between connected players") audited slices 3 and 4 together against that live report - five opus lenses; ONE ROOT: the wire's world-room law admitted eight digits of map id and a real MapTableData.MapId is nine or ten (Privateer's Hold 187853213), so every real dungeon was joined as a presence-only room at both ends, the poses relayed and nothing else, with no word said, and a pin had enshrined the bound; ten digits now, the id unsigned, the relay to be redeployed (`/health` names its version). Chat LINES are not filtered - a name is permanent and a line scrolls away - and that is a decision. A notice is a `system` line on EVERY tab of `src/net/chat.js`'s log, drawn with no name and no `#tag` (`src/ui/chatPanel.js`), because a name is forgeable and a flag that never travels on the wire is not. DICE1 (the same deploy): the relay rolls - `src/net/dice.js`, the dice's law for both ends, rolled from the relay's own CSPRNG and said as a frame of its own type. INSPECT1 (the same deploy): the profile of a player standing in front of you - the F-menu's Inspect row opens `src/ui/profileWindow.js`, the relay's badge beside the card their own game hands over when asked (`src/net/profileCard.js`, what their sheet shows and what they wear now, a directed `card` frame through the cast arm's per-sender funnel). | ~~THE WATER IS THE ART'S (WATER4, 2026-09-11)~~ **REVERTED (2026-09-12, Mac: "revert to our original implementation before the depth" - Water-Arc.md, THE REVERT; `world/waterArt.js` DELETED, the corner table draws again).** | Classic and DFU draw a terrain record as its 64x64 texel art and nothing about it says "water". WATER1's enhanced surface named the water by a hand-made CORNER table (`world/waterCorners.js`), so a shore was a straight diagonal and a record whose water reaches no corner (a puddle, a pond's inner bank) drew none. The port reads each ground archive's own bitmaps once (`world/waterArt.js`): the texels painted in record 0's palette indices are the water, box-averaged into a 16x16 grid per record that the shader samples at TERRAIN_FS's own transformed uv - so the surface's outline is the art's, a dither is a fraction, and every record with water in its art draws it. The same art hands the basin its corners and the feet their coverage (MAC2's law rides it: the player swims in the puddle the art paints). The corner table is the fallback where no archive is read; the classic lane is untouched. The shallows go from 0.30 to 0.80 opaque. WATER5 (the same day, Mac: "It's still not a perfect trace"): PER TEXEL - a 64x64 mask per record and its signed distance field on a texture array of the tile array's own shape, read at the terrain's own uv; the edge is the field's zero crossing over one screen pixel, the foam band the same distance, no ramp of its own and no bed ramp; the palette widened by colour (`WATER_COLOUR_TOLERANCE`); `?water=mask` paints the mask for the eye that judges it | Mac's call, 2026-09-11 ("its still not taking into account all the water textures that are on land, and its not traced well, just square. | **THE ENTER THAT CLOSES A WINDOW IS THE WINDOW'S (PL2, 2026-09-12)** - `player/pointerLock.js`. Mac: "Pointer can detach from the game and you're unable to click back in and use your pointer." The hosts' keydown ladders are bubble listeners registered before `bindCursorToggle`'s, and a window that closes on Enter (a message box's Enter button, a conversation's, the rest and level-up prompts) closes inside them - so by the time the toggle read `isWindowUp()` the window was gone, the same press flipped `cursorActive`, released the lock, and `requestLook`'s precedence line (U45: a deliberately freed cursor is taken back only by the toggle) refused every click after. DFU never sees that press: `InputManager.Update` withholds every action for `inputWaitTotal` (0.0833 s) after a pause ends (InputManager.cs:49, :511-515) and `ActionStarted` is what PlayerMouseLook.cs:190 reads. The port's copy of that skip is the CAPTURE phase - the guard is read before any bubble listener can pop the window - plus a typed DOM field keeps its Enter (CG2's `isTextEntryTarget`). Pinned: `test/cursortoggle.test.js` PL2. |

The compositor's buffer is a UI image, row 0 at the top, while the billboard shader samples GL's bottom-up texel order (render/renderer.js's header: every other billboard arrives from TextureFile.getColor32 already bottom-up). The doll's crop was the only top-down buffer ever handed to `createBillboardBatch`, so the peers stood on their heads. `cropRgba(..., { bottomUp: true })` writes the rows in reverse for the upload; the classic composite and the Morrowind bodies (MWBODY1) are untouched. Pinned: `test/online.test.js` OD1. |
| **TRAVEL OPTIONS, 1:1 (TO1, 2026-09-17)** - `vendor/travel-options/`, `systems/travelPaths.js`, `systems/travelAutopilot.js`, `systems/travelOptions.js`, `systems/travelPorts.js`, `systems/travelOptionsText.js`, `systems/timeScale.js`, `ui/travelControlUI.js`, `ui/travelJunctionMap.js`, `ui/travelPathsOverlay.js`, `ui/travelMapOptions.js`, `ui/enhancedTravelControl.js`, the additions to `ui/travelMapWindow.js` and `ui/travelPopUp.js`, `player/motor.js`'s scaled step, `world/terrainGenClient.js`'s `source`, and the mod's construction, fork, update, draw, key arm and click router in `scenes/world.js`. Hazelnut's Travel Options 1.11 for DFU (MIT), with the autopilot Jedidia wrote for Tedious Travel - ported off the author's own sources at ajrb/dfunity-mods AFTER checking them against the shipped 1.11 DLL, because a mod page's source tree and a mod page's binary are not the same artifact: 78 of the 80 method names in those sources resolve in the DLL's string table, and the settings keys the source reads are exactly the keys `modsettings.json` declares, neither side carrying one the other does not. PORTED 1:1: `IsPlayerControlledTravel`'s three-way fork (the whole difference between a walked trip and DFU's fast travel), the autopilot's Update in its own order (the arrival test first and only inside the destination pixel, the re-aim only when the pixel under the player CHANGED, the orientation and the forward force every frame) with `CalculateYaw`'s reversed atan2 and `+ 180` kept as written, the five-degree overshoot that counts as arrival, the 800-unit arrival buffer grown only for a location destination, `GetDirection`'s eight arms and the N arm's `<= 360`, `GetTargetPixel`, path following's junction halt and its recovery walk, the four circumnavigation corner rects, `DrawPath`'s two texels a direction (the cardinals straight out, the diagonals one beside and one in the corner) at five texels a map pixel, `DrawLocation`'s 5x5 and 3x3 dots with the middle-click ring, `DrawMapSection`'s tracks-then-roads-then-dot order and its half-pixel circular crop, the junction map's `herePt` expression and `GetDirectionIndex`'s eight offsets, the 417 port location ids, the Mages Guild teleport's rank-scaled charge, and the mod's 44 CSV strings. THE TIME SCALE IS UNITY'S, AND IT IS READ INSIDE THE MOTOR: `Time.timeScale` with `fixedDeltaTime = timeScale * base` means a x50 journey is still ONE physics step a frame covering fifty times the ground, and `maximumDeltaTime` is UNSCALED - so `player/motor.js` multiplies its own frame and its own step by `timeScale()` and every caller keeps handing it the real frame, which is what kept the twelve host-shape pins literal. BASIC ROADS COMPATIBILITY, MAC'S EXPLICIT ASK, IS NOW EXPLICIT AT THE SEAM: this port draws roads either from Hazelnut's vendored byte arrays or from its OWN generated network when Basic Roads is off, and `terrainGenClient` did not say which - it does now (`source`, set at all three assembly sites), and path following takes the vendored arrays ALONE, because the generated network has no junction data to follow and a mini-map of it would be a picture of a road the mod cannot walk. THE ENHANCED PANEL IS THE PORT'S OWN, NOT HIS (Mac: "an enhanced version of the UI for enhanced mode") - the same five controls in the enhanced skin's language with the hours still to go, the distance out, what the journey follows and the junction map as a real canvas; it lives on the HUD layer and NOT in the overlay slot, because an overlay holds the motor and the world clock (`_overlayHeld`) and townTalk's keydown swallows every key, so a journey behind one could neither run nor be steered. NINE DEPARTURES RECORDED, the load-bearing one being that only the traveller and the calendar are scaled - the encounter pump, the foe pools, the weather and the animations stay on real time, since a scaled pump at x50 would roll fifty times the ambushes a real second and the terrain queue cannot raise fifty map pixels in the time it raises one. THE THREE UI TEXTURES are this tree's re-encode of the bundle's Texture2D objects (reproducible by `tools/travelOptionsAssets.mjs`), not the author's file bytes, and `vendor/travel-options/README.md` says so; the permission line is open. `test/to1_travelOptions.test.js` (41), `tools/mutants/to1.json` (81: 79 dead, 2 equivalent). Not seen in a browser. AUDIT-TO1 (2026-09-18): the audit found the journey DEAD in the shipping host - the host's `isPlayerOnHUD` was `!gamePaused()`, so every accelerated journey interrupted itself on its first unpaused frame while every pin supplied the flag by hand; three of the mod's eight classes unread (MagesGuildTO: paid teleport unreachable below the rank it is free at; CastWhenHeldTO: a walked journey billed a held enchantment every four game minutes); the default skin unable to start a journey or refuse a ship; the strip and the ports art never uploaded (white bars); the junction disc gated on the panel it outlives; the location rects off a method MapsFile never had; four of five Start subscriptions unwired; the ship restriction inverted at the popup; the follow key on SocialInteract's F; rain and the stride through the journey; a load keeping the destination; NO on the resume prompt closing the map. Fixed, 55 pins, 113 mutants, departures 6/8/9 corrected and 10-14 added - `06-Systems/Travel-Options.md` AUDIT-TO1. ROAD-CRASH (2026-09-23, Discord: "crashes while traveling on roads with travel options"): the ring walk kept a stale destination name, so its rects refreshed mid-walk, went null at a neighbour pixel and OnArrival read `.zMax` off null - a dead frame loop; the ring arm forgets the name, the walk guards its rects, the recovery walk's give-up is a junction (departures 16-17, 4 pins, 4 mutants). TRAVEL-NAV (2026-09-25, Mac: "Improving travel options navigation to properly route around objects and stopping before running into buildings"): the port's own steering between the autopilot and the motor (`systems/travelSteer.js`) - feelers through the host's own collider, a fan with a committed side, a stand-off cap at every acceleration, and a stop in the mod's voice when there is no way round, no movement or (TRAVEL-NAV2, the review before the merge) no headway; a named destination's arrival buffer made an arrival across a pixel edge; the port's own switch `GeneralOptions.AvoidObstacles` on the mod's pane, on, and off the mod's beeline exactly (departures 18-19, `test/travelnav.test.js` (22), `tools/mutants/travelnav.json` and `travelnav2.json`, 54 dead). `06-Systems/Travel-Options.md` | Mac, 2026-09-17 ("This is the next daggerfall mod we are to implement 1:1... ensure the compatibility is sound with basic roads"); permission line in `vendor/travel-options/README.md` | TO1 |

The pixel was not: ES1e's retro pass lived inside the dome's fragment shader alone, so PS1's switch reached the port's dome and nothing else - the clouds composited SMOOTH over a pixelated dome, and the mod's skybox never saw it. cubeSnap and bayer4 are one GLSL string now (`RETRO_GLSL`, moved out of the dome verbatim) and three passes carry it with the same two uniforms: the dome, the clouds' composite (the direction snapped before the map is read, colour AND transmittance posterised by the cell before the blend) and the mod's skybox (snapped before anything is computed - its sun, moons, stars and sheets land on the grid; the encoded colour posterised last; 0/0 is the mod's shader as written, its own NEAREST sheets and REDUCE_COLOR untouched). | ~~**THE SQUARE FRAME GOES - THE GRID IS RINGS (ES1g, 2026-09-12)**~~ REMOVED (FT3, 2026-09-14, Mac: "Remove our version of pixelated sky"); `ringSnap` outlives it in `render/orderedDither.js` for PS3's dither - was: `render/retroPixel.js`, `render/enhancedSky.js`. Mac: "the returning pixelated sky look exposes the frame of a square skybox." It did, and the frame was REAL. ES1f cast the retro grid on an equi-angular CUBE to escape the lat-long pole; a cube has twelve edges. The geometry tiled across them continuously (measured: 44.5605, 44.7363, 44.9121 | 45.0879, 45.2637, 45.4395, the boundary exactly at 45) but the CELL NUMBERING did not - a face's axes are its own, so approaching the +Y/+Z edge the Bayer phase ran 3,0,1 and leaving it 3,2,1, the index counting up to the edge on one face and down from it on the other, with `face * 977.0` shifting the phase again on top. Rendered as a map the break is unmistakable: above 45 degrees every row of the dither shears (the top face's axes are x and z, so azimuth crosses it diagonally), below it every row is identical (a side face's axes are azimuth and elevation). That hard line, four of them meeting at the corners, IS the frame, and no cube can avoid it - adjacent faces' axes differ by 90 degrees whatever you index. So the grid is RINGS: rows of constant elevation one `step` tall, each holding `round(2*PI*cos(el)/step)` cells so every cell is one `step` wide too - the count FALLS toward the pole, which is the one thing the lat-long grid got wrong (it kept `2*PI/step` on every ring, so the cells narrowed to slivers and pinwheeled at the zenith). Each ring's count is ROUNDED TO A MULTIPLE OF FOUR, because the dither is a 4x4 tile indexed by the cell and a ring whose count is not would step its Bayer phase across the azimuth wrap - a dislocation up the az=0 meridian, the same class of defect as the cube's edges, on 370 of 512 rings before the rounding and 0 after. Measured: cell width 0.3465-0.3572 degrees below 85 degrees of elevation against a height of exactly 0.3516 everywhere (width:height 0.986 to 1.016), the largest neighbour jump under one cell's diagonal (0.4972) anywhere - quantization, and NO seam, through both poles and across the wrap, in the geometry AND in the cell numbering the dither reads. What it costs is the last degree or so around the zenith and the nadir, where the rounded counts run 4, 8, 16, 20, 28 coming down: the rows stop lining up and the cells there go 0.785 to 1.178 wide for one tall, so the grid reads as a rosette rather than a checkerboard. The cube spent a hard edge across the whole sky to buy that. THREE defects the adversarial pass then found and this row carries: the ring count is ROUNDED (PI/step lands exactly on an integer and GLSL ES allows a divide 2.5 ULP out, so a truncating cap answered 512 on one GPU and 511 on another - the zenith a whole pixel different between a phone and a desktop); atan(0,0) is UNDEFINED in GLSL and the exact zenith reaches it, so the poles are guarded; and the parameter is `stepRad`, because `step` is a GLSL built-in and shadowing it in three shaders is a compile risk nothing here can run. And it is the painted sky's own shape - SKY??.DAT is a panorama STRIP, horizontal rows wrapped around the horizon. **AND THE PIXEL IS ITS TRUE SIZE AGAIN**: ES1f passed `n = (PI/2)/step` as "cells per face", but the face coordinate spans [-1,1], so `floor(uv * n)` cut 2n of them - 512 a face, 1024 across 180 degrees against SKY??.DAT's 512, every retro pixel HALF its documented width, and the pin guarded `n` rather than the angle it produced. The ring snap takes `step` itself. The dome writes the SHARED snap lines now, so the three passes cannot drift. The star field keeps its own cube coordinate (EE2 F2): a sparse random field has no grid to break, and the face offset is what stops a mirrored id drawing the same star either side of an edge. The star field is untouched and the pixel doubling costs it little: the dome samples stars() on the snapped direction, so the sampling pitch doubled, but a lit cell is four times the area and the measured mean light over a patch of night sky falls 4.7% (0.00256 to 0.00244) with the lit fraction flat at 0.79% - fewer, chunkier stars rather than a dimmer sky, recorded here so a future step change cannot move it unnoticed. Pinned: `test/enhancedSky.test.js` ES1g - every line of the snap by text, the cell's angle and its HEIGHT measured off the snap (the first cut pushed a constant and compared it to itself, an assertion no shader could fail), a seam sweep to 89.99 degrees plus every ring boundary, and the wrap's dither phase on all 512 rings; all mutation-run, four mutants killed. Also `test/macfive.test.js` PS2, `test/audit39_render.test.js` F53. |
| **THE PROGRESSING CIRCLES GO (PS3, 2026-09-12)** - `render/dynamicSkiesRenderer.js`, `scenes/shared.js`, `src/tools/skyLab.js`. Mac: "with the dynamic skies mod there's these progressing circles in the sky when I want it to be a smooth sky transition." The circles are the mod's own REDUCE_COLOR: it quantizes each channel with a bare `ceil()` and NO dither, in linear light, at a step the sun's height drives (`_stepSize - lerpScale^5 * _stepSize + 0.001`). The sky's iso-luminance contours around the sun are concentric rings, so a hard quantizer draws them as bands - and because the step changes as the sun climbs, they WALK, which is the "progressing". Measured on the shipped Sunny preset (`stepSize` 0.015) with the sun high: FIFTY flat plateaus through the halo, the worst a 0.033 sRGB edge, some eight levels of 255 and several times the threshold at which a smooth gradient shows a contour; the mod's other presets run 0.001-0.015, so every one of them bands. The port's answer is the device ES1e already applies to its own dome - an ORDERED dither, the same Bayer cell, half a step either way, so the quantizer's threshold moves per cell and the contours dissolve into a stipple. The palette is untouched and so is the mod's upward `ceil()` bias - the offset is ZERO-MEAN, `bayer4 - 0.46875` rather than `- 0.5`, because bayer4 averages 7.5/16 and the naive form would have raised the bias by 1/32 of a band. Over the same sweep a four-row average resolves 577 distinct values instead of 50. The index is WORLD-FIXED either way: the sky's own cell while it is pixelated, and a cell a third that size when it is smooth. Indexed by `gl_FragCoord` it would be locked to the display while the sky slides beneath it, so it would crawl as the camera turned - and half of the mod's band is 7/255 in the darks, not the half-LSB the dome's smooth pass dithers with and calls never itself visible. A PORT-SIDE departure from 1:1, and the mod's raw threshold is one door away: `?bands=raw` (uniform `uBandDither` 0). Not a compatibility switch between mods (MM1) - it is this mod's own posterise. Pinned: `test/dynamicSkies.test.js` - the step formula still the mod's to the character, the dither's form and its zero mean, the uniform fetched AND uploaded, the default, the door reachable in both hosts, no `gl_FragCoord` anywhere in the block, and the plateau count measured raw against dithered. (The door and the upload are pinned because they are what make the departure legal: delete the upload and the uniform sits at its default 0, the mod's raw ceil, and the slice silently does nothing - the `_CloudTopColorBoost` failure the renderer's own comment cites.) |
| **LAND VIEW DISTANCE, ENHANCED (LV1, 2026-09-12)** - `world/landView.js`, `systems/uiPrefs.js`, `scenes/world.js`, `ui/enhancedMenu.js`. Mac: "I wanna push the draw distance as far as we can push it while keeping performance perfect." StreamingWorld.TerrainDistance (StreamingWorld.cs:55-56, [Range(1,4)], default 3) is the streamed grid's Chebyshev radius in map pixels; the 1:1 lane keeps DFU's setting and its 1..4 (Experimental/TerrainDistance, untouched). The enhanced lane pays less for a far pixel than DFU does - the third ring out builds strided 4x (EV4), its flats draw only tall or moving (MAC1), the grass grows in the near pixels alone (PERF2), the fog end scales with the radius (EV4) - so it takes its own: the Enhanced pane's Land view distance (uiPrefs `landViewDistance`, tiers 3/4/5/6, 5 by default: 121 pixels, 72 of them the cheap kind, the haze at 4000 units). Six is the ceiling - past it the far province ring (EV8, radius 48) and the build queue on a pixel crossing (13 pixels an edge at 6) are the binding costs, not the draw. ONE pure function decides (`landViewDistance({enhanced, pref, setting})`) and the world host reads it once for both the fog scale and the grid. Not measured on a GPU in this session (no ARENA2 here; `npm run perf` is the probe). Pinned: `test/macfive.test.js` LV1, `test/streaming.test.js`. |
| **THE POINTER COMES BACK (PL3, 2026-09-12)** - `player/pointerLock.js`, `scenes/world.js` (the chat's open), `ui/pixelDial.js`, `main.js`. Mac: "The mouse pointer can still get stuck outside of the game, not allowing you to interact with the game unless refreshing. I'm not sure if this is an issue with interacting with UI elements itself." It was, three ways past PL2. (1) `cursorActive` is a module global nothing reset: a host that booted after it latched true had every relock arm refused from its first frame - the bind resets it (a host boot is a fresh PlayerMouseLook). (2) The toggle read only the host's window predicate, and two surfaces that own Enter were invisible to it: the pixel dial (Tab; its Enter is its commit) and the chat (opened by the SAME Enter, CHAT_OPEN_ACTION) - the toggle, a window capture listener bound at boot, fired first, flipped the flag and released the lock, and the dial's close never relocked while the chat's relock was refused by the precedence line. The toggle now declines while an enhanced overlay stands (`overlayOpen`), the chat's open reclaims its Enter (`setCursorActive(false)`), and the dial gives the lock back to the element that held it. (3) A door that relocks inside its closing gesture (ui/pauseDoor.js, MAC1's rule) is a frame ahead of the host's overlay slot, so the look gate released the lock the gesture had just won and re-asked with no gesture, which the browser refuses after an Escape exit: a request honoured within RELOCK_GRACE_MS (150) now holds. Plus the net: a click on the canvas or the body with nothing up, no cursor activated and no lock held takes the lock inside that gesture whatever swallowed the canvas arm; and the crash report (`#crash`, the bottom 45% of the viewport) is pointer-events none - it ate every click over it. Pinned: `test/macfive.test.js` PL3 x2, `test/cursortoggle.test.js`, `test/chat1.test.js`. |
| **A TABLE IS A WALL (SH1, 2026-09-12)** - `player/collider.js`. Mac: "3D geometry has this issue where while it has collision, you can immediately walk over things (like interior tables, tree trunks, etc)." The step-up ladder lifted the capsule up to stepOffset, moved it forward and KEPT the raised height for the ground snap to settle - so beside a tabletop's edge it hovered at +0.5, the next frame's ladder lifted it +0.5 again from the hover, the edge contact (a normal inside slopeLimit 70) grounded each slice, and a 0.7 box was mounted in five frames; `test/motorStairs.test.js`'s own comment blessed it ("crests via its ~45-deg edge contact, as Unity" - it does not). CharacterController's law: a step is a surface at most stepOffset ABOVE THE FEET. Three moves in `_moveStep`: the ladder's stand ceiling (`entryY + STEP_OFFSET`) - a contact above it with an upward-leaning normal is a WALL in `_resolveSphere`, pushed out sideways by its whole penetration and never ground; a rung whose resolve lifted past the ceiling is refused; and the DOWN leg, Unity's third move - from the raised, advanced position the capsule comes down to the first height at which it stands on ground under the ceiling with the forward gain kept, and a rung with nothing to stand on is refused, the plain move's block standing. Every caller but the ladder passes Infinity and resolves as before (the stairs, the ramps, the 78-degree wall, the wall ladder, the MAC3 floor snap - all 35 pins hold). Measured against the old collider walking into boxes: 0.55/0.6/0.7 mounted (0.38/0.6/0.51 high) before, blocked flat now; 0.3 and 0.4 stepped both times. Wilderness trees are billboards with no collider at all; a trunk that blocks is an RMB model and now blocks like the table. Pinned: `test/macfive.test.js` SH1. |
| **DAGGERFALL ENHANCED (BR1, 2026-09-13)** - `index.html`, `play/index.html`, `public/manifest.webmanifest`, `app/package.json`, `app/main.cjs`, `ui/enhancedMenu.js`, `scenes/dataSource.js`, `main.js`, `README.md`. Mac: "I want to rebrand across the board as Daggerfall Enhanced... Daggerfall Enhanced / An open-source reimplementation of The Elder Scrolls II: Daggerfall. We need to tackle any javascript mention across the website, game, and note this in the bible." The public name was DAGGERFALL JAVASCRIPT, U60's choice, and the wordmark rendered it as **Daggerfall** over a tracked sub-line reading JAVASCRIPT - the implementation language standing where the product's name goes. THIRTEEN SURFACES carried it and are one name now: the landing page's `<title>`, its `og:title`, its wordmark and its footer, plus the cards and answers that called the project "this port" where it now has a name; the game page's title; the PWA manifest; the Electron shell's `productName` twice, its window title and its up-to-date dialog; the About panel; the two `document.title` writers (the loader and the second-tab refusal); the IN-GAME wordmark on the front door; the two prototype pages' wordmarks; and the PAUSE SCREEN's version line, which drew the WORKING name `project-dagger <sha>` - this Ledger's own row above calls that line the whole of the branding surface a player reads in game, and no sweep for the old PUBLIC name could ever have found it. The TAGLINE also had to be put somewhere a human reads: before this it existed only in `<meta>`, while the page's own standfirst still opened "The 1996 game with its rules intact". And the word ENHANCED collided with itself - it is this project's long-standing name for one of its two UI skins - so the website says "modern screens" where it meant the skin, while the code keeps `enhanced`, which is settled and internal. The TAGLINE is one sentence in all four places that answer "what is this" - the page description, the manifest, the About panel and the README - where there were four different answers before, one of them "a 1:1 JavaScript port of Daggerfall". The download's filename follows (`DaggerfallEnhanced-<version>-<os>-<arch>`), which the update check never reads: it keys on the release TAG and its html_url (Desktop-App.md). WHAT DID NOT MOVE, and why, each pinned so a careless rename fails loudly instead of breaking something live: the GitHub repository (`Lattymoy/daggerfall-js-source` - every bible link and every past release URL is written against it), the live domain (`daggerfalljs.dev` - it moves with DNS, not a commit), the Electron `appId` (`dev.daggerfalljs.app` - an install IDENTITY: change it and every installed copy stops seeing updates and an install-over leaves two apps), the npm package name, which follows the repository, and `dfjs-mod-settings`, the localStorage key the Mods pane writes - rename it and every player silently loses the mods they had turned on. Those four are moves OUTSIDE this tree. Two mentions of the old spelling survive on purpose and are pinned as survivors: a comment explaining a C# integer overflow "a JS port gets wrong by default" (a true statement about the language - the sweep is scoped to the product NAME, because rewriting technical prose to avoid a word would make the source lie about what it is), and TI2's header, which QUOTES Mac asking to "enhance the mobile element of DFJS" - his words, at the time he said them. THE REBRAND'S OWN WORST BUG WAS NOT A NAME. `productName` is what Electron derives `app.getName()` from, and `app.getPath('userData')` from that - so renaming the product moved `<appData>/Daggerfall JavaScript` out from under every installed desktop copy on its next launch: saves, Prefs, and `config.json` with the ARENA2 path in it, all silently unreachable, and the first-run folder prompt back. Nothing in the tree accounted for it. The storage root is PINNED now to the folder the shipped versions created (`app/main.cjs`, after the `DAGGER_USER_DATA` door), and stays there whatever the product is called: a rebrand is a name, not a migration. THE WORDMARK IS THE OTHER LESSON. It splits the name across two elements - `Daggerfall` and a tracked sub-line - so `git grep 'Daggerfall JavaScript'` can never see it whole, and the in-game one went on rendering DAGGERFALL over JAVASCRIPT on the front door, the first thing a player sees every launch, while a sweep for the old name reported zero hits and the suite ran green. An adversarial discovery pass found it; the adjacency sweep never would have. So every wordmark is pinned STRUCTURALLY, by the sub-line's own text wherever the halves live, beside the sweep rather than behind it. THE NAME HAD NO PIN AT ALL BEFORE THIS: the whole rebrand landed and all 7247 tests stayed green, which is what an unguarded law looks like. `test/brand.test.js` is its one home now - the name and the tagline as constants, every surface asserted against them, a tracked-tree sweep for the old spelling, and the four identifiers pinned as deliberately unchanged with the consequence written beside each. Mutation-run: reverting one surface fails two tests. |
| **THE LANDING PAGE, HOUSEKEPT (HK1, 2026-09-13)** - `index.html`, `test/landing.test.js`. Mac: "I think now is a good time to do some housekeeping. Starting with the website... improve organization... reduce text bloat and overexplanation." MEASURED FIRST: 973 words over six sections, and four ideas each said six to eight times - the phone's touch controls EIGHT times, needing your own copy of Daggerfall SEVEN, that nothing is uploaded SEVEN, classic versus modern screens SIX. ORGANIZATION: six sections became FOUR, in the order a visitor actually asks them - what is this, can I play it, what is in it, who made it. Two of the six were `What it is` and `What's in it`, indistinguishable while scanning a nav; the step that GATES a first visit (you need your own copy) sat third, behind the 274-word feature grid, so `How to play` moved up in front of it. `Good to know` was four-sixths answers to questions the page had already answered, so the two that were not - the controls and the browsers - joined the practical tail of `How to play` along with saves, the bug door, and the desktop app, which had held a whole section, a nav slot and one of the two hero buttons on a page whose own pitch is that the browser needs no install. The two scattered expectation-setters (the `In progress` card and the `Not yet` line buried at the foot of the feature grid) are one paragraph now. BLOAT: every repeated idea was cut to one home - the hero carries the your-files-stay-yours promise, the steps carry the folder, the feature grid carries classic-versus-modern - and the three-clause sentences that stated one thing three ways went with them (a save "survives a cleared browser, you can back it up by copying it, and a save you copy in shows up on the load screen" is "real files on disk"). The feature grid's nine entries averaged 28 words of sub-features, manual content rather than capability; `Ninety of the game's ninety-one spell effects` invited the question it did not answer and went, while `265 quests` stayed, being the opposite. 973 words to 682, with nothing dropped that the page actually said. The `.notyet` rule went with its markup. NOT TOUCHED and worth knowing: the two build stamps, in the rail foot and at the page end, are deliberate and pinned (U63's three zones), so they are not duplication to tidy; and the hero keeps both plaques, because removing a download call to action is a decision about the product, not about the prose. Pinned: `test/landing.test.js` HK1 - the four sections in reading order with the nav exactly those four and no broken anchor or orphan, and a word ceiling with a per-idea mention ceiling, because this is the kind of page that grows back one well-meant sentence at a time. |
| ~~**THE WORDMARK IS THE LOGO (BR2, 2026-09-13)**~~ **REVERTED (BR3, 2026-09-14, Mac: "On the website revert the logo to the original Daggerfall Enhanced text instead of using the uploaded logo")** - `index.html`'s wordmark is the typed `Daggerfall` over `ENHANCED` again, over its rule; `public/brand/` is gone, the doctrine allow-list row with it, and `test/landing.test.js` holds U60's whole no-image law once more (no `<img>`, no image file, `public/brand/` untracked). The ruby gem BR2 set in the dividers stays - the colour was asked for, the picture was not. Original record: - `index.html`, `public/brand/daggerfall-enhanced.webp`, `ui/enhancedStyle.js`, `test/landing.test.js`, `test/brand.test.js`, `README.md`. Mac, with the mark attached: "I want to implement this into the website and adjust the colour scheme to follow along with the style without being overbearing." THE PAGE'S OWN LAW SAID NO. U60 pinned the landing page as a DOCUMENT that "carries no image" and `test/landing.test.js` forbade every `<img>`, every raster filename and every `url()`, with the Ko-fi cup drawn in nine box-shadow pixels rather than be the page's only picture. That law is NOT what it says on its face, and BR2 is where the difference had to be settled: the pictures it was written against were the U60c menu SCREENSHOTS under `public/site/`, renders of ARENA2 content, and the doctrine it enforces is `a render of game data is game data`. A WORDMARK RENDERS NOTHING. It is the product's own mark, so the law NARROWS rather than lifts - spelled now as a whitelist of ONE: exactly one `<img>` on the page, exactly one image filename anywhere in it, both this file, and `public/site/` still asserted EMPTY beside them so the screenshots cannot return under the mark's exemption. THE ASSET: the source is 1536x1024 with real alpha (border alpha 0-7, so no keying and no box on the dark page), cropped to its own art bounds and resized to 800x507, WebP q85 - 151 KB against 407 KB for the same art as PNG. It declares `width`/`height` in the markup so the door does not reflow as it loads, and it carries the NAME IN `alt`, which is the pin that matters: an `<img>` with no alt takes the product's name off the front door for every reader who cannot see it, and no adjacency sweep would ever notice - the same blindness BR1's split wordmark had, one layer along. IN GAME THE WORDMARK IS STILL TYPE, and that is not an oversight: `ui/enhancedStyle.js`'s BRAND note is that the classic PICK03I0 paints its labels into a 320x200 bitmap, art that cannot reflow, cannot scale and cannot be read on a phone, which is the exact thing this overhaul exists to stop doing. A landing page is a fixed banner on a document - one heading, no state, sized off the viewport, with its text in `alt`; a MENU is none of those, so the menu is pinned as staying type. THE COLOUR, and the restraint Mac asked for: the mark's art is a red (`#b91309`) with gold bevels over a gold divider bar with a red stone set in its centre. The page already had that divider - `.rule` with a `.gem`, the menu's own idiom, five of them down the page - and it was ALL brass. The gem takes the mark's own build instead: a RUBY core keeping its four gold tips. That is the whole of the colour change. `--ruby` is declared in the skin's token block (nothing in-game consumes it yet, the precedent being `--brand`, declared there for the same reason) because the landing page owns no colour of its own and every one of them must be a var() the skin declares. Red appears exactly where the logo puts it, in the stone at the centre of a gold bar, and nowhere else on the page - one deliberate accent repeated as a motif is what following a style looks like; tinting the headings would have been wearing it. `README.md`'s "carries no game data or imagery" was true and is now half true, so it says what it means: no game data, one image, and it is the wordmark. AND THE DOCTRINE GATE CAUGHT IT, which is the part worth recording: `test/doctrine.test.js` refuses any tracked raster that is not on its allow-list, and the list's question is "did the pixels come from ARENA2". The honest answer for a Daggerfall wordmark is NOT "ours" - the classic logo is in ARENA2 too (the title screen draws it), so lettering that looks like it cannot be cleared by inspection the way a generated head turnaround can. What IS settled is the other half: a MARK is not a render of game content - no dungeon, no sprite, no frame of play, nothing a player could use in place of owning the game, which is what the doctrine keeps out of a public tree and why the U60c screenshots may not follow it back in. So the row on that list records the QUESTION and flags it for Mac, in the shape the Dynamic Skies textures already set, rather than claiming an ownership nobody here can prove; the credits already carry the not-affiliated-with-Bethesda line, and a ruling the other way is one file and one `<img>`. |
| **BASIC ROADS HAD NO SWITCH (BR3, 2026-09-13)** - `systems/modSettings.js`, `scenes/world.js`, `world/terrainGenClient.js`, `test/roadsReal.test.js`, `test/modsettings.test.js`. Mac: "can you do an audit on basic roads. I dont think its working." THE PIPELINE WAS NOT THE PROBLEM, and the audit says so with numbers rather than a reading: his four arrays are 500,000 bytes each (21,554 road, 30,472 track, 973 river, 2,203 stream pixels), the bit convention is his (N=128..NW=1), the index is his (`y*1000+x`), all 21,554 road pixels paint (mean 305 tiles of 16,384), 250 of 250 carry road records through `assignTiles` and `convertTilemap` into the bytes the shader reads, and the built site serves each asset at 500,000 bytes. WHAT WAS ACTUALLY WRONG WAS THE SWITCH. Basic Roads was the ONLY one of the six vendored mods with no `Enabled` key and no gate: MO1 ("All mods should be enabled by default") gave every other mod the switch and defaulted it true, and this one was missed - so Basic Roads was not enabled-by-default, it was UNCONDITIONAL. `world.js` called `loadModRoads()` with nothing to ask, its card in the Mods pane was the only one with no on/off row, and a player could neither confirm it was on nor turn it off. It reads like Dynamic Skies' now: ON is his network 1:1; OFF is the PORT'S OWN network (ROADS 3), the same fallback a map his arrays cannot load already takes - the switch can never cost a player their roads, which is what keeps it compatible across the board (Mac's rule). AND THE PIN THAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT DID NOT EXIST, twice over. MO1's law was asserted mod by mod, so the one mod left out of the sweep was the one with the gap; it is a LOOP over `MOD_SETTINGS` now, and a seventh mod cannot land without the switch. And EVERY ROADS TEST USED A SYNTHETIC MASK - `roads.test.js` hands the painter a `DIR.N | DIR.S` it wrote itself, and `roadsParity.test.js` replays 907 oracle cases whose `roadCorners` is an INPUT, so `pathCorners` - the function that COMPUTES that byte from the vendored arrays - was pinned against the port's own expectation and never against a byte the mod would produce. Nothing anywhere asserted that the files the game actually ships put ONE road tile on the ground: a wrong convention, a transposed index, a truncated asset or a painter that answers for every invented mask and none the map holds would all have been green. `test/roadsReal.test.js` runs the REAL arrays through the REAL kernel and counts what lands. Mutation-run, all four: removing the `Enabled` key fails 2, ungating `loadModRoads` fails 1, flipping `pathCorners`' two halves fails 2, truncating `roadData.bytes` fails 1. ONE MORE, latent: `setRoadsData` never set `_switches` while `setRoads` did, so the three rebuilds that read it would have dropped SmoothRoads and RiversAndStreams on his path - AUDIT 58 F3 exactly, in the one place that fix did not reach. Inert only because `_roads` is set inline; closed here rather than left to be found a third time. |

| **EYE OF THE BEHOLDER, PARTIAL (EOTB0-EOTB7, 2026-09-15; AUDIT-EOTB the same day found it substantially non-functional and the word 1:1 false)** - `vendor/eye-of-the-beholder/`, `player/eotbCamera.js`, `player/eotbBillboard.js`, `player/eotbSprite.js`, `player/eotbBody.js`, `player/mwView.js` (the seam), `systems/mathf.js`, `vite.config.js`. RedRoryOTheGlen's third-person camera and player sprite, read off the shipped assembly's IL with dncil/dnfile (`monodis` segfaults on it). Mac handed the archive over and, asked whether it should replace the port's Morrowind camera: "This is moreso for those who opt out of using morrowind." So the two coexist and the seam asks which body can answer - the Morrowind rig where `fpArm` has built one, this mod's sprite where it has not. **FOUR DEPARTURES, each marked where it lands.** (1) `CameraScrolling.ScrollableZOffset` ships OFF and the port ships it ON - the MODS-ON class's THIRD departure, and the decision behind it is Mac's own ("instead of numpad being used to change views, I want it scrollable like how we handle morrowind"): it is the arm the whole view seam is built on, and with it off there is no way into third person at all. (2) `Camera.TogglePerspective` (KeypadEnter) is INERT - the wheel supersedes it - and is kept in the pane rather than deleted, because a key quietly removed is a key nobody can ask about, the shape HT's `Throwing.ShowTrajectory` is kept in. (3) `Camera.SwitchShoulder` ships `Tab` and the port binds `B`: HT4's finding again, same author, same key - Tab is free in Daggerfall Unity and SPENT here, PX15 gave it to the pixel dial. (4) THE ART IS RE-ENCODED. The bundle's 3035 sprites are palettised colour stored as truecolor; measured over all 3035 rather than sampled, every pixel's alpha is 0 or 255 (the port's own cutout law) and no sprite holds more than 166 distinct colours, so each is written as an indexed PNG - 19.72 MB becomes 8.39 MB, verified per sprite. That is lossless for everything that reaches a screen and NOT byte-lossless, which is the distinction this row exists to make: the source carried up to 59 colours UNDER transparent pixels, ghost colour from the author's export that no renderer has ever shown, and those collapse to one index.  **AUDIT-EOTB, SIX FINDINGS, and every one of them had passed a green suite.** F1 the sprite's clock had no caller, so the body never animated; F2 `loadSettings` had no caller, so every dial on the pane was inert; F3 no host passed `dt`, so MoveTowards stepped nothing and the camera parked at the player's FEET; F4 no host passed `weaponReady`, so all three CameraOverride arms were unreachable code; F5 the up vector was `right x forward`, inverted; F6 four exports called by nothing. The cause is one cause: every pin drove the UNIT and none drove the WIRING, which is the F-SING lesson for the fifth time in this port. F1-F5 are fixed and pinned through `mwViewFrame` - the only path a player has - and F6 is gated by a DERIVED dead-export ledger rather than a list. **AND THE CLAIM.** The arc said 1:1 in six places; the assembly carries 62 authored methods and the port implements thirteen. `test/eotb_scope.test.js` holds all 62 rows, each with a verdict and, where there is no port, which kind of gap it is - so the next slice either ports one and moves its row or leaves it and says so, and the claim is checkable instead of asserted. **EOTB-IL (2026-09-16): THE ASSEMBLY READ.** Mac handed the shipped archive over ("it needs to be 1:1 with the uploaded file. No exceptions") and the port's own UnityFS reader took `Eye Of The Beholder.dll` out of the `.dfmod`; `tools/ilDump.py` (dnfile + dncil) printed every method body and both are vendored beside the art. Every `[SETTINGS]` law is gone - each is `[IL]` with its offset, and where a reading was wrong the offset that corrects it is cited. Mac's two reports were both in the IL: the FRAME CHOPPING was one five-frame clock over tables of one, two, four, six and eight frames (the counts are now the mod's own, the one-shots run on their own ticks - `animTime * 5 / frames` for a swing, 0.125 for a bow, a spell and claws, 0.5 for a death - and the three-frame delayed write is kept); the SPRITE TURNING THE WRONG WAY was `SignedAngle`'s two arguments swapped, which negates the angle and runs the wheel backwards. Beyond those: the table chooser has no death arm and gallops on a SPEED; Mirror flips only the front and back records while the count is odd; PingPong turns at `n/2 + offset`; Mixed rolls every fourth; the hold draws down to frame 0; the death freezes; the orientation is throttled at 0.1 s; the auto-toggle is three independent blocks and a fan-out and `autoPOVSwitch` is DERIVED from the rows (the bundle ships it disarmed - the port had armed it); the bounds seed at 2.0; the ladder tests the Z captured before the notch; the mirrored base arm scales Z alone; the shoulder's revert re-probes from the target's height; the keys fire on RELEASE; `GlobalOffsetScale` is inert because its getter has no caller; and the CART is ported (model 41239 through the host's pipeline, 2.49 m behind, the ground probe, LookAt, the wobble, the 3.2 activation raced with the torch and the door). The ledger is corrected to 61 authored methods (AUDIT-EOTB's 62 listed one that does not exist), 45 ported and 16 with no twin, and checked against the dump. EOTB-WALL (2026-09-17, Mac: "3rd person clips through walls and ceilings"): a fourth departure - after the mod's per-axis CheckBounds the port casts ONE line-of-sight ray from the head to the smoothed eye and pulls in with the mod's own clearance, the Morrowind camera's law (camera.cpp:200-206), because three axis casts never measure the diagonal the camera stands on and the smoothing lags the pulled-in target; `06-Systems/Eye-Of-The-Beholder.md` EOTB-WALL. | Mac, 2026-09-15 ("Alright next mod I want to add 1:1"; "This is moreso for those who opt out of using morrowind"), recorded in `06-Systems/Eye-Of-The-Beholder.md` | MODS-ON, HT4, PX15, MW-D25 |

| **THE WISPS' COUNT, THE SKY'S DIRECTION, THE GRASS AT NIGHT (WIND4, 2026-09-15)** - `render/windWisps.js`, `render/volumetricClouds.js`, `render/labGrass.js`, `grass-proto.html`, `scenes/world.js`. Mac, three in one message on the wind and weather work. (1) `WISP_MAX` 2400 -> 650 and the calm floor 0.12 -> 0.08: one wisp per three cubic metres of the box reads as fog, not as wind, and the field's job is the DIRECTION. (2) THE DRIFT'S SIGN: the cloud field is sampled at an absolute position, so the recenter is added - but the drift is how far the AIR has moved, and adding it too showed the cloud that was at `p + d` standing at `p`, so the whole sky crept UPWIND at the wind's own speed. Subtracted now, in both reads of the field. (3) THE SWARD'S LIGHT: every other surface lights as ambient plus the sun's colour times its SCALE plus the moon's the same way; the grass used the colour, dropped the scale - the term that goes to zero at sunset - and had no moon, so at midnight the ground went dark and the grass glowed under a sun that had set. Both terms ride it now, and the host hands five instead of three. GR1's byte-for-byte law with `grass-proto.html` is kept: the lab carries the same text and sets the new uniforms itself. WIND5 (2026-09-23, Mac: "reduce the amount of wind streaks and change their design to be more swirly like the image"): 240 at a gale, and each wisp a FLOURISH - a ribbon along an arched or S stroke that ends in a tightening curl, leaning its own way, drawn on and off along its path; the sandstorm keeps its straight streak (`render/windWisps.js`, Rendering.md WIND5). WISPS-RETURN (2026-09-25, Mac: "I want to return to the original wind wisps before our current design"): the flourish is retired and each wisp is WIND3's straight streak along the wind again; DISC17-A's count (120 at a gale) and doubled alpha stand, as do AUDIT-VC7's clock and AUDIT 68's wrap (Rendering.md WISPS-RETURN). | Mac, 2026-09-15, recorded in `07-Rendering/Rendering.md` WIND4; the return, 2026-09-25, in WISPS-RETURN | WIND2, WIND3, WEATHER2, GR1, VC4 |
| **THE HELD SPRITE WAS UPSIDE DOWN (HT3, 2026-09-15)** - `formats/color32Order.js`, `systems/handheldTorches.js`, `combat/weaponWidgetAssets.js`. Mac: "The player held torch mod shows the sprite upside down when held" - the flame under the hand. The port has TWO right answers and the door took the wrong one: `toColor32` is a FLIP, right for a Unity texture (Unity stores bottom-up, so flipped its row 0 is the picture's top) and wrong for a decoded PNG whose row 0 already is. Which is right depends on WHERE IT IS DRAWN - a world billboard samples v with 0 at the bottom; a SCREEN quad does not, because `drawScreenQuad` gives the rect's TOP the source rect's `v0` and `UNPACK_FLIP_Y_WEBGL` is false at every upload. `toScreenOrder` is the same shape without the flip; the held torch and the widget's loose-PNG arm (the identical latent fault, unseen because in play the widget takes its Unity bundle arm) take it, and the world doors still flip. TEX1 did not catch this because TEX1 was about the SHAPE, and the only door where the orientation was visible had never drawn a frame until MODS-ON turned `Modules.Sprite` on. | Mac, 2026-09-15, recorded in `06-Systems/Handheld-Torches.md` HT3 | TEX1, HT1, MODS-ON |
