| **ONLINE, A REST WAITS FOR NOTHING (RESTX1, 2026-09-15)** - `systems/restSession.js`, and nothing else. Mac: "for online I want to change the rest mechanic to not use any time. Basically rest just becomes the way to regain". THE THING THAT WAS WRONG IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE: online a rest has never been able to MOVE the clock - the world's time is a function of wall time and `setWorldMinutes` refuses every local write while the shared clock stands (WORLD5's own law, and the right one) - so WORLD5 made the rest honest by PACING it off that clock, "an hour of rest is an hour of the shared world's time". Exactly right, and the cost is the whole of the complaint: an hour is five real minutes at DFU's TimeScale, so eight hours of rest was FORTY REAL MINUTES of watching a counter, which nobody does - they close the window and stay hurt, and the mechanic quietly stops existing online. So the waiting goes and nothing else does: a REST (`full`/`timed`) online resolves inside the one frame that asks and passes NO minutes, because none were ever available to pass. The hourly ladder is untouched - the same hours, the same per-hour `restVitals`, the same enemy check, the same prevented-rest poll, the same CheckRent - only the waiting between the rungs is gone. LOITER IS NOT REST AND KEEPS WAITING: it recovers nothing by design and passing time IS its purpose, so a loiter that resolved at once would do literally nothing; online it rides the shared clock exactly as WORLD5 left it, and AUDIT WORLD5 C7/C8 are re-aimed onto loiter where their law still holds unchanged. WHAT A FREE REST DOES NOT DO, and it follows from "no time passes" rather than being an omission: no world minutes, so no quest ticks, no magic rounds, no disease or poison progress, no encounter catch-up - `advanceMinutes` is simply never called, together with the quest tick that rides the same sub-tick. OL1's SHAPE: the session asks `sharedMinutes`, the dep WORLD5 already gave it, so THE FOUR HOSTS are untouched and the pin holds that none of them may name the predicate. `FREE_REST_HOUR_CAP` (99, DFU's own prompt cap) is a per-frame CHUNK and not a stop - `full` has no counter of its own - and it always converges, because a free rest passes no minutes for a disease to drain through and all three recovery rates clamp above zero; driven in the pins, because an untested guard is a guess. THREE JUDGMENT CALLS, each small and reversible, taken because Mac said "we can dive deeper at a later time": the hours dial still means hours of regain (a free 99-hour rest reaches the same place, so "Rest until healed" is the path in practice), skills still tally as offline, and loiter was left alone. 7 pins, 8 mutants, 8 dead. `06-Systems/Online-Arc.md` RESTX1 | Mac, 2026-09-15 ("for online I want to change the rest mechanic to not use any time") | WORLD5, OL1 |

Please audit the online quest system." The rest change read back with the WHOLE PATH DRIVEN (a real RestWindow under a shared clock, healing a real entity through real restVitals), and the online quest system mapped and mutation-sized beside it. ONE FINDING, PAID. **RESTX F1: the rate limit time was providing.** `restVitals` tallies Medical every hour and in DFU that tally is rate-limited by the hour COSTING TIME; RESTX1 removed the cost and the limit went with it. Measured: `rest 99 hours` at full health online was 99 Medical tallies in ONE frame on ONE click, repeatable for ever, where offline the same ninety-nine hours costs 74 real seconds of watching a counter - which is the limit DFU was relying on. The free lane restores it with the only thing it has left: AN HOUR IS PAID FOR WHEN IT DOES SOMETHING, which is also what the switch says it is ("rest just becomes the way to regain" - resting when there is nothing to regain is not resting). The PACED lanes, offline and any loiter, keep DFU's unconditional tally because there the hour really was spent. Now 0 tallies at full health, 99 for ninety-nine healing hours, 9 for a paced offline rest; 3 mutants, 3 dead. **THREE RECORDS NARROWED**, none a defect - each a sentence true of rests and now true only of loiter or of the ordinary clock: OL2's rest counter is LOITER's decoration now (a rest never draws that page); the enchantment reroll no longer rides the sleep, and nothing is lost because the world's clock is wall time and a stale item rerolls on its own hour clock through the ordinary tick; and QUEST DELAYS can no longer be slept forward - they still advance, because WORLD7's clocks charge played time, but the player cannot accelerate one by sleeping, which is the one thing about RESTX1 that will surprise someone. **AUDIT OQ: NO DEFECT FOUND**, and what the system IS, recorded so nobody re-derives it: quests are ENTIRELY PER-PLAYER (each client its own QuestMachine, journal and resources), the system's only online awareness is WORLD7's two (`questClockStepMax` and CreateFoe's interval), the boundary that makes it safe is `_layoutFoes` - every foe past it is this player's own, so a quest foe is minted above it and is never streamed, puppet-ised or touched by a joiner's stream - and quest ITEMS ride site links rather than loot piles, so WORLD4's shared containers cannot reach one. Sized by mutation rather than read: erasing the boundary reddens WORLD1's host pin, stopping the world host handing the played step reddens four suites, and WORLD7's machine pin is EXECUTED over the real quest tables, machine and Clock. THE OPEN QUESTION IS MAC'S: a per-player quest foe means two players in one dungeon do not see each other's quest targets - correct for a per-player quest system and what the code has always done, but whether co-op SHOULD share a quest is a design decision nobody has made. `06-Systems/Online-Arc.md` AUDIT RESTX + AUDIT OQ | Mac, 2026-09-15 ("Do an audit on this...") | RESTX1, WORLD5, WORLD7, OL2 (RESTX2, 2026-09-17: the free lane and `test/restx1_online_rest.test.js` are RETIRED; `test/restx2_online_rest.test.js` holds the law that replaced it.) |
| **THE QUICKSLOT DIAMOND (QS1-QS3, 2026-09-17)** - `systems/quickslots.js`, `ui/enhancedHud.js` (the diamond), `ui/quickslotTags.js`, `ui/padGlyphs.js`, `ui/itemIconUrl.js`, `ui/enhancedInventory.js` (the tooltip's slot buttons and the row chips), `ui/enhancedControls.js`, `systems/inputActions.js`, `ui/input.js`, `ui/gamepadInput.js`, `combat/weaponRig.js` (`refreshWorn`), and the four hosts' `quickUse`/`quickSwap` doors. (2) Three port actions past DFU's enum, appended never inserted as SocialInteract was - `QuickUse1`, `QuickUse2`, `QuickSwap` on Digit1-3, unspent by DFU, by the port and by every vendored mod (the HT4 pin class) - yielded by the classic windows through PORT_ACTIONS and drawn by the enhanced pane under their own Quickslots heading. (6) The hotkey swap BILLS the inventory window's equip pause (`billEquipDelayOnClose`, the window's own sum for the same table transition - the hotkey's transition is deliberately not the window's, because it empties the main hand first where `GetEquipSlot`'s either-hand arm would put the second weapon in the free OFF hand and swap nothing) around its one `equipItem`, because a swap for free is the exploit the pause exists to stop; out of empty hands the slot points at BARE HANDS and the next press puts the weapon away (AUDIT QS F2). The use is the window's own `useItem` ladder with the host's own hooks and refusals. The tags read the digit for the digit row (DFU's GetButtonText says A1) and OUR OWN pixel pad glyphs - Xbox or PlayStation by the pad's id - while the pad is the live device; Handheld Torches' toggle is a mod TextKey, so the torch cell's tag is keyboard only. A features row (`quickslot-diamond`) hides the diamond alone. Audited the same day (AUDIT QS, ten findings, eight fixed, two recorded). **QS4 + QS5 (the same day, Mac playing):** a FOURTH port action, `QuickOffHand` on Digit4, because the off-hand corner named no key at all - it is the mod's own toggle arm through one door (`toggleLightPress`, so the free-hand guard has one home), the cell names the port's rebindable action rather than the mod's TextKey, and the four hosts finally hand `drawHud` the three doors the phone's cells were built to press. And the durability strip under the art became the cell's own lower EDGES - an SVG gauge stroked inside the rhombus, draining from the bottom point outward - so the sprite gets the whole cell back. **QS6 (the same day, before the merge):** the THIRD digit is a SPELL slot, and a HELD key fills a slot. A fifth port action, `QuickSpell`, takes Digit3; `QuickSwap` keeps its index, its pane row and its rebind and ships UNBOUND, because the cell the swap is DRAWN in is the off hand's and Digit4 already presses that cell (`offHandOffersSwap` is the VIEW's own answer, so there is one ladder and not two). The spell slot is keyed by the spell INDEX - the key `systems/save.js` and the engine's `setReadiedByIndex` already use - so a made spell rides and a rename follows the spell; the press is `hostMagic.js`'s own SetReadySpell with its silence gate, its spell-point refusal and its CasterOnly instant cast, and the model says nothing over any of them; a second press puts the spell away through AbortReadySpell, an unset slot takes the book's first, and a spell the book no longer holds is a ghost that refuses by name. THE HOLD reverses QS2's own law deliberately: a press that acts on its DOWN edge cannot be the start of a hold, so `QuickUse1`, `QuickUse2` and `QuickSpell` join `POLLED_ACTIONS` (the seam ReadyWeapon and SwitchHand already use) and each host's frame drives one machine - past 350ms the press is a hold and steps every 300ms through the book or the pack's distinct consumable kinds, a release that never stepped is a tap, a release that stepped performs nothing, a blocked frame drops every hold silently, and a stalled frame steps once. The spell is drawn on the diamond's CAPTION (four corners are two hands and two consumables; a spell is in none of them), lit when it is the spell actually in hand, and the generic readied chip stands down rather than saying the same word twice. The phone's cells carry the same tap/hold pair, because a phone has no key to hold. **AUDITED BEFORE THE MERGE (AUDIT QS6, the same day, Mac: "Audit everything before merging") - SEVEN FINDINGS, ALL FIXED:** a cite tool rewrote a number INSIDE a pin's pick regex, and five Ledger rows turned out to cite a PAIR whose second half this port had never once read (stale by thousands of lines; seven re-resolved by content, every uncaptured half de-baked and captured); a hold that spanned an open window drank a potion on the way out, because `blocked` CLEARED the holds and the next frame read a key still down as a fresh press (it DISARMS now); the phone's hold left a `setInterval` running for the life of the page after a HUD teardown; the cycling lamp was written below the block's signature guard and so could never go out; the RESPAWN1 pin read `collectWorld` where the memory's publisher is `sharedWorld`, and asserted ADMITTED where it meant CARRIED - the exact shape of the bug it was written for; `scenes/dungeon.js` ticked the hold machine inside its own overlay gate so its `blocked` argument was dead, and two hosts return above their tick while a video holds the frame, so the machine now defends itself on a gap in the wall clock; and TEN mutant records had quietly stopped applying, which is a law checked by nothing while the slice still reports none survived - all ten re-aimed, and `test/mutantdrift.test.js` now sweeps all 879 the way citedrift sweeps the cites. Driven through a real browser keyboard (`tools/qs6HoldProbe.mjs`, 7 scenarios, 24 checks, including the OS's own auto-repeat storm). **HT7 + MWT1 + MWT2 (2026-09-17, Mac: "Take care of both. Also fix morrowind model's torch. It's positioned incorrectly and isnt lit") - THE TORCH, THREE WAYS.** HT6's two recorded-not-departed laws were decisions and Mac made them: (1) Handheld Torches' sheathed arm frees a hand for everything but a bow, on the premise that a sheathed weapon is away - and Daggerfall has NO BACK SHEATH, so that premise is false in this game (the weapon is lowered, still held, drawn the moment you swing). What is WORN takes a hand now, sheathed or drawn; the one stance-bound clause left is the mod's own bare right hand "in use" (0x2d53). (2) The mod ships `OnStow = Drop`, so with the hand law running at the equip moment a shield equipped mid-fight put the lit torch on the dungeon floor - the port DEFAULTS it to Unequip, the mod's value one click away on the dial. MWT1: the Morrowind torch hung at the right bone the wrong way up - `SceneUtil::attach`'s PAT carries an attitude for `isLight` ALONE (actoranimation.cpp:97-103), an extra -90 about X, which this port's own reference notes recorded at `02-Formats/Morrowind-Rules.md:3228` and the code never applied. MWT2: `mwNifMesh` has always RESOLVED the emission (LightMode_Emissive forces the diffuse and ambient to black, so the surface is lit by that term alone) and nothing ever read it - the pack wrote `diffuseAt` alone and the character fragment had no emission at all, so every self-illuminated Morrowind surface drew BLACK. A torch with a black flame is a stick. `emissiveAt` is `diffuseAt`'s mirror, the pack is three floats wider and the program takes a fifth additive attribute, added after every light and ungated by the vertex colour the same law already blacked out. AND THE DEBT AUDIT QS6 F7 CARRIED IS PAID: all twelve stale mutant records re-aimed, the CARRIED list EMPTY - and three of them SURVIVED once they applied again, which is exactly what a stale record hides (the footstep gate had no pin but a source regex; the equip moment's fresh settings read had none that could fail; and one was genuinely equivalent, recorded with the reasoning). | **GetKeyDown AND GetKeyUp, THE READS THE PORT NEVER HAD (MWCROUCH, 2026-09-17)** - `ui/input.js` (the edge ring: `keyEdges`, `noteKeyDown`/`noteKeyUp`, `beginInputFrame`, `pressed`/`released`/`pressedCode`, and a `ring` parameter on `codeDown`), and all four hosts. Mac reported it as a Morrowind bug - "When crouching with the morrowind model. you can't uncrouch" - and the Morrowind rig is innocent: `_heightAction` has no view-mode arm, `get height()` no Morrowind branch, the collider holds world geometry only, `fpArm` reads the SNEAK key by a rule of its own (Rule 32(a)), and a crouch/uncrouch driven against a real `Collider` stands the player up every time. What the Morrowind body does is make FRAMES LONG. **The port had `GetKey` and nothing else**, so every host derived its press edges: `held(keys, act) && !prev`, with `prev` re-sampled at the foot of the same frame. That drops any press whose keydown AND keyup both land between two frames - the key is never in the ring on a frame that looks at it - and at 10 fps a tap often lands in none, so crouching works when it works and standing back up does not. Unity buffers and drains events per frame, so `Input.GetKeyDown` answers true on the frame FOLLOWING the press whatever the key does afterwards (InputManager.cs:1084-1108, one poll a frame in FindKeyboardActions); DFU therefore has no such hole at any frame rate. `motor.js:1155` had already named this bug's twin from the other side ("a render frame that accumulates less than one physics step swallowed the press") - the half that remained was the host's. The fix is the missing BUFFER, not a new rule: the listeners note each edge as the DOM delivers it, the frame rotates the ring ONCE at its head, and `pressed`/`released` read the rotated halves through the same dual-dict fallthrough and the same combo arm `held` takes. Rotating once a frame gives an edge exactly one frame of life - the single frame Unity gives it - so a reader behind a shut overlay still DROPS its edge rather than banking it, which is the paused-InputManager law (:529-563) the old latches were standing in for. `codeDown`'s new `ring` parameter needed no new comment: the one it had already said "the modifier arm reads HELD whatever edge the caller asked for; only the combo'd key takes `method`". FOUR LAWS, FOUR HOSTS, ONE SHAPE - the crouch toggle, ReadyWeapon's press, SwitchHand's RELEASE (WeaponManager.cs:272's ActionComplete) and the E activate are all the edge they always claimed to be; the crouch KEY is still read HELD beside it, because the levitate descent is a held key (AUDIT 26 F031). `world.js` and `exterior.js` own a frame and a ring each, on the `latch` bag whose two fields this retired; `worldModes.js` READS that ring off the same shared bag and never rotates it, because the mode machine owns no frame; `dungeon.js` is standalone. The MOUSE codes ride the ring too - AUDIT 39r put them in `keys` for `GetKey` and an edge read has the same claim on them. Driven in Chromium (`tools/mwcrouchProbe.mjs`): both readers off the same real key events, in a page slowed to ~9 fps, each on its own real `PlayerMotor` - three taps, and the old derivation sees NONE of them while the ring sees all three, the two ending in different stances; at 60 fps they agree exactly. The probe caught its own harness lying first: Playwright's `keyboard.press` awaits the keydown, so against a busy-waiting page it sends the keyup only after the loop has already sampled the key, and the bug would not reproduce - the events go through a raw CDP session back to back now. Record: `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` MWCROUCH. | Mac, 2026-09-17 ("When crouching with the morrowind model. you can't uncrouch") | A8, ROAD-G G3, AUDIT 39r, AUDIT 26 F031/F065, ROAD-Ar, SHEATH/WEAPON-VIS2, QS6 |
| **ONE MODE, ONE DISPATCH - AND A DEBUG SENTINEL OUT OF THE PLAYER'S MOUTH (QS7 + TALK-UNKNOWN, 2026-09-17)** - `scenes/world.js`, `scenes/exterior.js` (the quickslot arm's mode gate) and `systems/talkMacros.js` (`speakable`). **QS7:** the world and exterior hosts each run a keydown ladder AND mount `worldModes` over themselves, which runs its own (U43's one dispatch, `routeKey` over `interiorKeyCtx` indoors and `dungeonCtx` underground) - both on the same window, neither stopping the other. QS2 put the quickslot arm ABOVE the outer mode gate when the modal contexts carried no quickslot doors at all; QS4 gave them the whole set and nothing went back to the arm standing in for them, so from that commit a Digit4 in a tavern pressed the mod's `toggleLightPress` TWICE. Twice is not two, because the toggle is a FLIP: douse, then ignite, both clips, both lines, and a lit torch the player just asked to put out (Mac: "pressing 4 does not actually make it go out, it just goes thru the 'douse' and 'ignite' motions"). WEAPON-VIS2's double-fire at a third door. The arm takes the exterior-mode gate now and the quickslots stay live indoors and underground through the ladder that already routes them; the `SocialInteract` arm beside it stays UNGATED, because AUDIT SOC B4/D1's finding is that the modal contexts carry no `socialInteract` and there is no second answer to collide with. **TALK-UNKNOWN:** `%2com` is CLASSIC's macro and `MacroHelper.cs` has no row for it - 217 rows, diffed cell for cell by `test/macrocoverage.test.js`, carrying `%1com` (:44) and not its sibling - so `GetValue`'s outermost else (:526-527) answers `%2com[undefined]` and TEXT.RSC 7212's Work question says it out loud. DFU does the same with the same data; the port did not drift. What changes is the ONE step between the expansion and the player. E7 moved this walk off the empty string onto the sentinel deliberately and was right to (a 26-row table's empty string deleted ~190 macros DFU renders for real), but its argument - "the table is all 217 rows now, so the shape is safe to speak" - holds only for macros DFU has heard of, and the coverage gate is exactly what makes `[undefined]` mean one thing: classic wrote a macro Daggerfall Unity never implemented. `expandTalkMacros` stays verbatim; `speakable` drops an `[undefined]` from SPOKEN text and collapses the whitespace around it ("possibilities %2com?" reads "possibilities?", not "possibilities ?"). The other three sentinels SPEAK - `[nullMCP]`, `[unhandled]` and `[srcDataUnknown]` each name a context the port could be getting wrong. The symbol is logged (`unknownTalkMacros()`) and warned once, so it is findable rather than readable. IT DOES NOT CLAIM WHAT `%2com` SHOULD RENDER: classic knows, the port cannot recover it from the data it ships, and inventing a word would be a departure wearing a port's clothes - if the text is ever recovered the change is one handler. Record: `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` QS7 + TALK-UNKNOWN. | Mac, 2026-09-17 ("if you go into a tavern with a lit torch, pressing 4 does not actually make it go out"; the talk screenshot) | QS2, QS4, QS6, U43, WEAPON-VIS2, AUDIT SOC B4/D1, E7, MH1 |
| **A BODY IS OPENED NOT EMPTIED, A QUEST NAMES WHAT IT ASKS FOR, TWO WINDOW KEYS, AND A CAMERA THAT CASTS A VOLUME (MAC-A/C/D/E, 2026-09-17)** - `scenes/corpseMarker.js`, `scenes/exteriorFoes.js`, `scenes/cityGuards.js`, `scenes/world.js`, `scenes/exterior.js` (MAC-E); `ui/enhancedInventory.js`, `ui/charSheetDoor.js`, `ui/charsheet.js` and all four hosts (MAC-C); `systems/quest/item.js` (MAC-D); `player/mwCamera.js`, `player/mwView.js` and all four hosts (MAC-A). Five reports in one message; four fixed, the fifth investigated and left alone with its findings written down. **MAC-E** is a RESIDUE this port wrote into corpseMarker.js's own header and left for three waves: "DFU's general arm opens the inventory window with the corpse as the remote LootTarget (:957). The port transfers the lot and reports the count." A recorded residue is a bug with a note on it. The dungeon has opened the body since U26; the two EXTERIOR pools - every body in a street, a road or an encounter - never got it, so a click emptied the body into the pack past every weight the window shows and said "You take 2 items.", a line Daggerfall does not have. `openCorpseLoot` is PlayerActivate's corpse arm whole now, and a caller with NO window warns and takes nothing rather than falling back, because a silent fallback is how this survived its own note. The ONLINE grant landing keeps the bulk take: a peer's body is its owner's to empty and the items are in flight by the time this client sees them. THE CARRY WEIGHT IS NOT A SECOND BUG - neither PlayerSpeedChanger.cs nor PlayerEntity.cs mentions encumbrance at all; DFU draws the figure and never charges for it, and what the window restores is the CHOICE. **MAC-D**: `Item.ExpandMacro` answers `_symbol_` with `GetLongName`, which is `ResolveItemLongName(item, false)` (Item.cs:304-307); the port returned the raw `name` field under a note explaining that it had no long-name maker - D7 built one and nothing came back, so a quest named a Daedric Broadsword "Broadsword" and a book "Book", the one a player read out loud. **MAC-C**: the pack closed on the literal `'F6'` (I2's and FIX-F's bug twice over) and the sheet closed on nothing, because PX27 made it the pause window's Stats page and that screen's capture handler answers Escape alone - correct for the pause face it shares, so the arm belongs on the overlay F5 opened. All three windows read the REGISTRY now, and a window key naming ANOTHER window crosses over: close, then open, in that order, because `showOverlay` replaces the host's one slot. **MAC-A**: the third-person camera's two obstacle guards are `castSphere` in the reference (camera.cpp:186 and :200, the limit as the RADIUS) and were RAYS here with the limit subtracted off the end. A pillar is thin - one ray threads past what a five-unit sphere hits square, so the camera slides through it, and when the ray does catch an edge the distance moves by the pillar's whole width between frames. A line where the reference has a volume. The distance is the swept sphere's own now: OpenMW re-derives the sphere's centre at contact and that centre is what a swept-sphere cast reports, so subtracting again would pay for the clearance twice. **MAC-B IS NOT FIXED AND NOT GUESSED AT**: `AddLight`'s second switch is transcribed cell for cell and was re-read against the live C# for this report - every row matches, including record 8, the "Turkis lamp", which is `Color(0.68, 1.0, 0.94)` in Daggerfall Unity. Turquoise is what that lamp IS. The ambient is PlayerAmbientLight's verbatim pair, the ranges are in the same metres DFU's are, and `withPlayerLights` keeps data and colours paired. What the port has that DFU does not is a fixed light-slot cap with a nearest-N selection, whose swap is instant where Unity's fades - the best candidate for "pops in", and a hardware-shaped departure rather than a defect with a line to point at. Changing DFU's own light table on a hunch is the move `%2com` was refused for. | **A CARD THAT FOLDS, AND A PAGE THAT WAS NEVER DRAWN (MAC-F/MAC-G, 2026-09-17)** - `ui/enhancedChronicle.js` (the fold store and its four laws, the head-as-handle, the tab's own control), `ui/enhancedStyle.js` (`cr-fold`, `cr-caret`, `cr-foldall`, the shut card, `px-src`) for MAC-F; `systems/specialAdvantages.js` (`classSpecials`), `ui/enhancedCharSheet.js` (`sheetModel.specials`, `raceTemplateOf`), `ui/enhancedMenu.js` (the Stats rail's Advantages page) for MAC-G. **MAC-F** is a cost the enhanced window took on and never paid: the classic logbook CANNOT have this problem - it draws into a 320x200 panel and pages four lines at a time - and the enhanced one traded that for a scrolling column, which is better until twelve quests each unroll their whole trail into it. No model changed; what is new is a place to put a reading position. The head is the HANDLE (caret and date are one button, so a card folds by clicking what was already being read) with the note's remove a SIBLING of it, because a button inside a button is not HTML and would make every removal a fold; the BODY is what folds, so a shut card still says which quest and when; the tab folds at once through a control that READS the cards rather than keeping a flag, so shutting the last one by hand flips it; and the fold is a READING POSITION, not a setting - it survives a tab change, it is cleared when the window opens, and nothing about it reaches disk. **MAC-G** is a gap older and wider than the page it was reported on: DFU HAS this list (`GetClassSpecials`, DaggerfallCharacterSheetWindow.cs:459-762, behind the classic sheet's History button at :898-903) and the port had `parseCareerData` - the WRITE onto a career's flags, U20b's whole point - with NO READ anywhere in either skin, so the seven-item balance a player spends the whole of chargen on went invisible the moment the game started. `classSpecials` is that read, beside the write on purpose: the two walk the same bitfields, so a bit that moves breaks both in one file. It is not the pick list read back - that list exists only inside the chargen window and a classic save never had one - so the FLAGS are the source, as they are for DFU, section by section in its order and pairing, the blood appended last and de-duplicated because a Breton mage can carry Resistance To Magic twice. Three departures: the SPLIT into advantages and disadvantages (which Mac asked for, and which is not invented - it is which chargen list the primary belongs to, the division the difficulty table signs); the SOURCE tag, because class and blood are different facts about a re-rollable character; and DFU's copy-paste slip at :744, mapping Athleticism to the ACUTE HEARING string, which is NOT ported - it is unreachable in both (no playable race sets SpecialAbilities, RaceTemplate.cs:172-345) and copying it would put a typo on a screen a player reads. NOT here, and recorded rather than hidden: a vampire's or werewolf's powers, which DFU feeds through an OVERRIDE race template the port does not have - the curses carry them as live effects, and inventing a table is not a port. The classic sheet's History button still opens the history with no specials box in front of it: a second, older parity gap on a different window. Record: `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` MAC-F/MAC-G. | Mac, 2026-09-17 ("In the enhanced chronicle. Quests and their tab's should be able to be minimized" + "The enhanced stat page on the pause menu doesn't have any listing for character advantages/disadvantages") | PX24, PX24b, PX24c, PX27, U20b, U52, MAC-K2 |
| **THE VIEWMODEL TAKES THE ROOM'S LIGHT, AND A NAME IS NOT A BUTTON (MAC-H/I/J/P, 2026-09-17)** - `systems/handheldTorches.js` (the draw's light gate) for MAC-H; `render/renderer.js` (`flatLightAt`, `FLAT_LIGHT_FLOOR`, the viewmodel light borrow), `combat/fpsWeapon.js` (`fpLightingOn`, the Tint), `combat/fpsSpellCasting.js`, `combat/weaponWidget.js`, `combat/weaponRig.js`, `combat/fpArm.js`, `systems/features.js` (the switch) for MAC-I and MAC-P; `ui/chatPanel.js` for MAC-J. **MAC-H**: the torch hand's OnGUI gated on "is there a texture" and `currentTexture` is set ONCE, to torch frame 0, the moment InitializeTextures finishes - so every host drew a torch at SetGuard's rest position, the left middle of the screen, with or without one in the player's hand. The draw asks the LIGHT now, not a latch Update sets, which is what makes the sprite go the same frame the torch does: a window that is open has stopped the frames a latch would be updated on, the trap HT6 already paid for. **MAC-I**: `FPSWeapon.Tint` is DFU's own channel (FPSWeapon.cs:108, handed to the draw at :182) and DFU core never writes it - it is the First-Person Lighting mod's. The port writes it from `flatLightAt`, which is the BILLBOARD program's own composition (ambient plus the moon's Lambert-average half, the sun's half, every point light at the same squared-linear falloff, the indirect term) sampled at the camera. Not in it, deliberately: the cloud shadow (a shader function over a map - sampling it means a read-back) and the lane's decode (a screen quad is drawn by the 2D pass AFTER the lane's composite, so it is tinted in the space that pass paints in - the water's own classic-space read). The FLOOR of 0.25 is the port's: a flat in a black room reads as the room, a HAND in a black room is a hole in the screen. **MAC-P**: the Morrowind arm is rendered LENS-LOCAL at the origin of a camera-local space while the point lights are in WORLD space, so every light in the room missed it by the player's distance from the world origin and the arm had only the ambient and the sun - five of 255 in a dark room, measured. It takes the STUDIO's shape (a key at the eye, which is what makes a viewmodel read) scaled by the same `flatLightAt` answer, so at full daylight it is byte for byte the studio it had and only ever loses light where the room has none; borrowed and returned in a finally, because a leaked studio is permanent. ONE switch for both lanes (Features -> First-person lighting, on, `?fplight=off`). **MAC-J**: the chat roster marked a clickable name with a bare `act`, and `.act` is the ENHANCED SKIN'S BUTTON (12px/20px, a 1px border, 46px floor) - a sheet that is in the document of every online game, because online forces the enhanced lane. Every name a player could click was drawn as a 46-58px button and your own name, which is never a door, sat 16px high beside them. Prefixed now, and the pin is the general law: no class this sheet writes may be a bare selector in the enhanced one. **MAC-Q IS NOT FIXED**: the Morrowind torch has no flame because THIS PORT HAS NEVER DRAWN A MORROWIND PARTICLE SYSTEM - `mwNifFile.js` parses NiAutoNormalParticles and NiParticleSystemController in full and `mwNifMesh.js` drops them, because GEOMETRY_TYPES is NiTriShape and NiTriStrips. It is a slice (emitter, modifier chain, billboarded quads, a sorted additive pass), it cannot be verified in a container with no Morrowind data, and the emission channel is NOT the gap - a flame authored as a triangle has drawn since MWT2. | **THE TORCH'S FIRE - MORROWIND'S PARTICLE SYSTEMS, PORTED (MAC-Q, 2026-09-17)** - `formats/mwNifMesh.js` (the particle geometries to a sink, with the NiBSParticleNode's flags; NiAlphaProperty's blend function and NiZBufferProperty's bits on the material), `formats/mwParticles.js` (the descriptor and the running system), `formats/mwCharacter.js` (bindPart carries a part's systems out), `formats/mwFirstPerson.js` (bindPartsInto places them as the part's rigid shapes), `combat/fpArm.js` (`stepRigEffects`, `effectPlacement`, both rigs' frames, the hide, the release, the textures, the re-lit torch), `render/renderer.js` (the particle program, the effect's buffers, the draw after the body's ranges). The record above closed the flame as investigated and not fixed - no Morrowind particle system had ever been drawn, and none could be verified in a container with no Morrowind data. Mac's answer was "Please take care of the morrowind torch flame", and this is the slice. THE LAW IS OPENMW'S, file by file: particle.cpp's shooter (`Quat(vdir, Y) * Quat(hdir, Z)` on +Z, the speed at half the variation either side, the life plus its variation times the die), Emitter::emitParticles (the counter, the box placer, `transformPositionVelocity(emitterToPs)` with the frame orthonormalised), GrowFade off the DEFAULT size, the colour affector's rgb-as-colour and a-as-alpha, GravityAffector's `magic = 1.6f` for wind and point with their decays, both colliders' reflections with the planar extents "intentionally swapped"; nifloader.cpp's handleParticleSystem, handleParticleInitialState (the file's own live particles, sized by the data's per-code sizes), handleParticleEmitter (the rate as slots over the mean life, or the birth rate under NoAutoAdjust) and handleParticlePrograms; the ParticleSystemController's emit window over ControllerFunction's clock, frozen with no source; osgParticle's Particle::update (judged before it ages, strictly past one) and the quad ParticleSystem draws (half-extent the size, alpha the colour's a times the particle's). THE TWO REFERENCE FRAMES are kept: LocalSpace rides the hand; absolute is kept in the RIG's space, the nearest thing a lens-local arm has to a world, so a swing leaves the fire behind - and that "world" does not include the player's walk, which is the one recorded departure. Not carried: NiBSPArrayController's target emission (refused with a note), NiParticleBomb, NiParticleRotation (unused in the reference), the soft-particle shader. The quad's texture orientation is one constant (`PARTICLE_CORNERS`): the port uploads a DDS top row first where OSG stores it bottom first, so the top corners take v 0. VERIFIED: every law against the reference's numbers, the chain from a hand-built file to PIXELS in a real GL context (`[255,140,26]` where the flame stands, black beside it, black hidden), the seams - and NOT the torch's own file, which nobody here could open. Record: `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` MAC-Q. | Mac, 2026-09-17 ("Please the care of the morrowind torch flame", on the finding that it could not be drawn or verified here) | MW-D51, MWT1, MWT2, MW-TORCH, MW-D11, MW-D20, MAC-P |

CAMP1's roll after the single roll's empty, `_standCampEncounter` - an anchor placed as a single encounter is, the members around the anchor at the group's spacing - and, in the world host, the chunk-load roll on the stream's `entered` event under an exterior gate and `amGroupRollOwner`; WINFOE1: `cityGuards.update(dt`, `exteriorFoes.update(dt` - the enemy pools keep their clock under a window, the civilians still freeze under the talk overlay), `scenes/worldModes.js` (`interiorFoes.update(dt`, `interiorGuards.update(dt`; the 15% chunk-load roll; the wilderness gate; camp vs pack composition off `chooseRandomEnemy`; the deterministic lowest-id group owner within 100m), `systems/deathRespawn.js` (new: `nearestSafeLocation` - a temple, a town or a CEMETERY graveyard by map-pixel distance over the region's mapTable; Mac's two zips, "BetterResting" and "daggerfalljsWildlifeSpawnsRespawn", cut from an older main: each file was diffed against the commit it was cut from so the intended change separated from the staleness (the zips would otherwise have reverted MWCROUCH, MAC-A/C/E and QS7 wholesale), and the intended change was carried by hand. FOUR LAWS. (1) RESTX2 retires RESTX1: online a rest paces on the timer offline always used, the counter ticks down, every sub-tick spends `advanceMinutes`, and a foe that walks up breaks the rest - the shared clock is still refused every write, so what the roll reads online is the session's own sim-minute, seeded from the shared clock once and ten a sub-tick from there; loiter online too. (2) CAMP1 is an ORIGINAL ADDITION, said so in its header: classic spawns one wanderer at a time; this places three to five (a camp, tight, members face the anchor) or two to four (a pack, loose) off the same climate table, wakes the campmates when one notices the player, rolls once per open window and once per pixel entered, and lets exactly one of several players standing together roll. (3) WINFOE1: the four enemy pools no longer take dt 0 under a window; the zip also un-froze the civilians, and that was NOT carried - the talk-window law (nobody walks away mid-talk) is a recorded audit and not what Mac asked for; the record says so. (4) D-ONLINE1: `_deathWasOnline` is snapshotted at the presenter (and at the frame as a backstop for the modal hosts) BEFORE `onlineFrame` leaves the room; `_onlineWorldSession` counts the CELL room the open world stands in, which `_actLive` never did; the reset - Enter, the timer, or F11 - respawns instead of ending the run: any mode is left through `forceExitToExterior` (the zip left a building's interior standing), the landing is a RandomStartMarker as TeleportAway names it (the zip took the tile's centre), the dungeon's door is the pixel already under the player, half health, a line. Pinned by `test/restx2_online_rest.test.js` (8), `test/camp1_groups.test.js` (5), `test/donline1_respawn.test.js` (3), `test/winfoe1_foes_under_windows.test.js` (1); WORLD5, AUDIT WORLD5 C7/C8, OL2 (5), FIX-E, AUDIT 21 F6, AUDIT 62 F15, AUDIT WORLD B6, MWBODY1, ROAD-B, ROAD-G G2, FT0/FT8/FT15 and the interior-foes pins re-aimed; `test/restx1_online_rest.test.js` RETIRED with the lane. `tools/mutants/restx2camp.json`. Records: Online-Arc (RESTX2, D-ONLINE1), Systems-Arc (CAMP1, WINFOE1). NOT VERIFIED IN A BROWSER: no online session and no second player exist in this container; the death respawn's teleport and the camp's placement are pinned by source and by the pure law under them. |
| **FOUR OF MAC'S BUGS - THE RAISED BLADE, THE DOUBLED LINE, THE HAND CELLS, THE HOLD UNDER A REPAINT (MAC-R1/R2/R3/R4, 2026-09-17)** - `combat/fpArm.js` (`frustum`, glFrustum's matrix, kept HERE because world/mat4.js is in the relay's bundle and under RELAY_VERSION's hash law; the draw padded the sprite sub-rect above the screen while a screen transform was set - an off-centre frustum over the same lens, `phFull` rows rendered, the composite extended up by the pad's share. SUPERSEDED by DISC13-C, 2026-09-23: the sides had the same cut, so the pass now renders the pixels of the lens's own grid that the moved rect shows on the screen (`fpFrameWindow`), laid where the rect puts them, and `FP_TOP_PAD` is retired - Morrowind-Rules, DISC13-C), `ui/input.js` (routeKey swallows a quickslot action's auto-repeat), `scenes/world.js` + `scenes/exterior.js` (the non-polled quickslot arm routes the press edge alone and eats the repeat; `quickSwitchHand`; the off hand's `switchHand` door), `systems/quickslots.js` (`offHandQuickslot` presses what the cell SHOWS: a lit light douses, a carried light ignites, the shield, the off-hand weapon and an empty hand with no light switch hands, the swap is the caller's, the light's refusal is said only where no hand can be switched), `scenes/worldModes.js` + `scenes/dungeonContext.js` (the same two doors on their own rigs), `ui/hud.js` (`quickSwitchHand` forwarded), `ui/enhancedHud.js` (the main cell's tap is the hand switch, no hold), `ui/enhancedInventory.js` (the implicit touch capture is given back at the press and on `gotpointercapture`; INV2's `lostpointercapture` end retired; the long-press `contextmenu` refused for the session's life), `ui/enhancedStyle.js` (`-webkit-touch-callout: none` on the tiles and the panels). THE FINDINGS. R1: the Morrowind arm's frame was exactly the screen and the Weapon Widget's channels move the COMPOSITE (a rect the screen's size, shifted by the bob, clamped by `transformRect` to the screen's height minus `weaponOffsetHeight`), so the frame's top edge sat below the screen's and a blade raised through it ended in a straight cut. R2: the two quickslot actions that are NOT polled (the swap, the off hand) were routed on every keydown a held key auto-repeats - two lines and a net nothing; the polled three were already edge-only through `noteKeyDown`, and DFU's ActionStarted is the press edge (InputManager.cs:634-637). R3: QS4 gave the off cell ONE act, the light's toggle, so a cell showing the bow said "You have no light source."; a hand cell's own act is DFU's SwitchHand (WeaponManager.cs:271-273), and the main cell had none. R4: a touch pointer implicitly captures the row it lands on, and a captured row the pane's `render()` detaches raises `lostpointercapture`, which AUDIT INV2 read as the pointer taken back and ended the session; the pane repaints on things a hold cannot see coming (INV2's own list). MEASURED: the diamond's tap path is ONE call per press on every cell under real touch (`tools/macrHudTapProbe.mjs`, 19/19), so the double was not the finger's; the hold arms, carries and releases through two repaints inside it (`tools/macrHoldRepaintProbe.mjs`, 8/8) - AND SO DID THE OLD CODE in this headless Chromium, which dispatched no implicit capture for CDP touch: the repaint premise stands on the Pointer Events spec and on Android Chrome, not on a measurement here. Pinned by `test/macr_fixes.test.js` (5); AUDIT INV2's lost-capture pin, QS3's listener count and main-cell pin, MW-D10/MW-D23's projection pins and drawHud's signature pins re-aimed. `tools/mutants/macr.json`, 18 dead. Records: Morrowind-Rules (MAC-R1), UI-Arc (MAC-R2/R3/R4). |

| **THE TOWN GATE AND THE GUILD HALL, OPEN AT NIGHT ONLINE (OL5, 2026-09-20, Mac: "Town gates online, guild services, should all be open at night time online mode")** - `world/cityGate.js` (`updateCityGate(gate, night, { online })`, defaulting to `sharedClockOn`) and `systems/buildingLocks.js` (`onlineReliefBuilding(type)`, the one home for WHICH buildings the shared world relieves; `buildingHoursState` and the unlock ladder's GuildHall arm read it). OL4's reasoning, applied to the two subjects it deliberately left out and said so in its own pin ("guild access unchanged online"): an online player cannot move the shared clock, so a classic schedule is a real-time lockout - a guild hall shut from 18:00 is a service nobody can buy for two real hours, and a city gate is worse than a shut door because SetOpen swaps the MeshCollider with the mesh (GameObjectHelper.cs:246-250), so a closed gate is a WALL and a walled city is sealed. THE DEPARTURE: online, a guild hall takes the same relief shift a shop has had since OL4, and a town gate never shuts at all. Everything else keeps R1 whole - houses (a residence is not a service), palaces, ships; temples and taverns never closed (0/25). Suns Rest stays a SHOP closure, so the holiday does not start shutting guild halls. TWO THINGS THE SLICE FOUND RATHER THAN SHIPPED: the GuildHall arm called `isBuildingOpen(type, hour)` with no opts, so an explicit `online` handed to `buildingIsUnlocked` was silently ignored by that one arm - harmless while guild halls had no relief, a lie the moment they do, and it is threaded now; and the online gate arm cannot be "the classic machine with night forced false", because `isOpen` is born true whatever model the block laid (DaggerfallCityGate.cs:19) and only a night/day cycle reconciles the flag with the model - remove night and a gate laid CLOSED stands as a wall for the whole session. The online arm is stated on the MODEL instead. The law lives in the component, not in the two hosts that tick it, and both hosts are pinned to carry no rule of their own. Pinned in `test/lockpicking.test.js` (OL4's guild pin INVERTED rather than deleted - the classic answer still held beside the departure - plus a sweep over every building type: a relieved one opens at 3am online, an unrelieved one answers exactly what classic answers, so the membership cannot drift into an enumeration) and `test/audit64_layout.test.js` (+2). `tools/mutants/ol5.json` (3) and 3 more added to `tools/mutants/ol4.json`; 5 mutants, 5 killed, and OL4's four stale records re-aimed BY CONTENT where the guard's predicate was renamed. Record: Online-Arc (OL5). NOT VERIFIED IN A BROWSER: no online session exists in this container. |
| **A STANDING PLAYER IS HEARD BY THE RUNTIME, NOT THE ROOM (RELAY-H1, 2026-09-20, Mac: "cloudflare hit its limit")** - `net/wire.js` (`HEARTBEAT_MS` 5000 -> 20000, `PING_MS = HEARTBEAT_MS / 4`, `RELAY_VERSION` world84) and `net/online.js` (`PEER_TIMEOUT_MS = 4 * HEARTBEAT_MS`, derived; a presence session's liveness ping on its own clock in `tick`). The relay's bill was duration, and the heartbeat pose was why: a pose every five seconds, moved or not, is an event that keeps a Durable Object awake ("incoming requests prevent hibernation"), so a room with anyone in it never slept and the free tier was ~7 player-hours a day. The relay had registered `setWebSocketAutoResponse('{"t":"ping"}', '{"t":"pong"}')` since CHAT1 - the runtime answers it in the object's SLEEP - and only chat used it. Now the socket's liveness rides that ping at PING_MS, the pose goes when it moved or every 20 s as the peers' proof of life, and the peer timeout is derived so the heartbeat can never again move alone (SLAM8's zero-margin blind spot). Four times fewer wakes from a standing player, with gaps a hibernation fits in. The merge drift-deploys the relay (relay-deploy.yml, keyed on the version) and drops every connected player once; the client is whole against the old relay in between. `test/relayh1.test.js` (4); 5 mutants, 5 dead; AUDIT RELAY-H1 paid two findings (the deploy claim, the spelling pin's spelling). See Online-Arc.md RELAY-H1. |

| **NO GROUP ROLL UNDER A REST, AND THE TUTORIAL DUNGEON IS NOT A RESPAWN DOOR (CAMP1-REST / D-ONLINE2, 2026-09-18, Mac's `WildernessRestingHOTHOTFIX` hand-off)** - `scenes/world.js` + `scenes/exterior.js` (`runEncounterTick` takes `isResting`, the CAMP1 group roll stands down while it is set, the rest deps set it; the frame's own tick and the chunk-load roll do not - the latter fires on a pixel crossing, which a resting player never makes), `scenes/world.js` (`isPrivateersHold` off the CONFIGURED start cell, so an online death in the tutorial dungeon takes the ordinary safe-location search instead of D-ONLINE1's door-out). THE DEPARTURES: CAMP1 is an ORIGINAL addition, and gating it under a rest is a DESIGN CALL of Mac's, not a fix - recorded as one because the hand-off's stated reason was wrong. It read the rest interrupt as a distance law (camps 14..26 vs RESTING_DISTANCE 12); `areEnemiesNearby`'s resting arm is SIGHT FIRST (a foe that has seen the player reports at any range, 12 is only the fallback) and the world is NOT frozen while a rest runs (WINFOE1 drives the foe pool under a window, precisely so a foe can break a rest). The real shape is FACING - a lone wanderer is minted `LookAt player` and trips the sight arm, a campmate is minted facing the ANCHOR out past the fallback - so the silent group is the one whose members face away, and the alternative one-line fix (yaw them at the sleeper) is written down rather than taken. ONLINE COST, taken knowingly: the roll is the elected owner's, so while the owner sleeps the cluster rolls none - restoring it needs a rest flag on the wire. D-ONLINE2 departs from D-ONLINE1's own departure: every other dungeon death respawns at its door, the tutorial dungeon may not, or online play skips it for free. REFUSED from the same hand-off, recorded: CAMP2's blanket `suppressInfighting` (a real bug, but justified by a `mobileFactions.js` that does not exist here, the flag also stops a campmate fighting the player's ally, and it is not in `snapshotWorld` so a quickload undoes it), the RemotePlayers `uploadRecordFrame`/`dt`/`eye` arguments (inert - no class-enemy path reads them), and `isOnlineWorldSession()` (no callers anywhere). The zip's base predated JAN1 and would have reverted JAN1's `groundY`, `syncTopics` and the `weaponPose` pair, ECON1's price tilt and WATCH1's `watch` wiring; each feature was lifted by hand instead. | **SOCIAL - FRIENDS, PRESENCE AND THE PARTY (SOC1-SOC7, 2026-09-16, four seats; AUDIT SOC the same day; PARTY8 2026-09-22, eight seats and the quiet-row HUD; AUDIT PARTY8 2026-09-23, `06-Systems/Online-Arc.md`)** - `server/src/index.js` (the hub), `src/net/wire.js` (the law, `world79`), `src/net/social.js`, `src/net/online.js`, `src/scenes/world.js`, `src/ui/socialPanel.js`, `src/ui/partyPanel.js`, `src/net/remotePlayers.js`, `src/ui/chatPanel.js`, `src/ui/socialMenu.js`, `src/player/socialPick.js`, `src/systems/inputActions.js` (the port's own SocialInteract action on F), `src/ui/partyMapMarks.js`, `src/ui/overworldMap.js` (RETIRED in MAP1 2026-09-18; the marks are inked by `src/ui/heldMap.js` now), `src/ui/travelMapWindow.js`. Mac: "A social button next to the chat UI ... friend other users, see if they are online/last online + be able to invite friends or other individuals to the new 4 person party system ... the players name who are in a party together should turn green ... pressing F on their body ... seen on the world map, regardless of their location." Daggerfall Unity has no friends, no parties and no online: the port's own, on the ONLINE row above. The world channel's Durable Object is THE HUB - every player online already holds a socket there - and keeps accounts (an id and a secret beside the tab's peer pair: a friend is a person, a peer is a tab; minted per browser profile in the app's own storage), friends and requests each way, presence and last-seen, four-seat parties (a seat kept five minutes for a dropped member, the seat passed to the longest-standing, the last out dissolves) and fans a member's party pose (map pixel, place, six vitals, the portrait's recipe) to the party alone. Presence rooms are untouched: a name is green because the hub told my client which peer ids are my party's. A refusal is a frame in words, never a close; the relay writes no chat line (notes are codes the client puts words to). Audited the same day under four lenses (AUDIT SOC): a directed act cooled per target, accounts nobody's list names swept on an alarm a page at a time, a pending row without presence, a by-account act for a relation alone, one tab speaking for a seat, the link's inbound gates and frame bound, the host's counted pointer surfaces and F inside. Record: `06-Systems/Social-Party-Arc.md` (its AUDIT SOC section). |

| **THE ASCENSION - THE ENHANCED SKIN'S LEVEL-UP WINDOW (LV1, 2026-09-18, Mac: "the next enhanced UI window... our gold standard is a replication of the skyrim level up UI in our own constellation vision")** - `ui/levelUpView.js` (the reading and the figure), `ui/enhancedLevelUp.js` (the window), `ui/enhancedStyle.js` (the LV1 block), the fork and the overlay in `ui/charSheetDoor.js`, both interior arms in `scenes/worldModes.js`, the font-less escape's new arm in `scenes/dungeonContext.js`, the lab at `levelup.html` + `src/tools/levelUpLab.js`. **AN ORIGINAL WINDOW OVER DFU'S OWN LAW.** DFU has no level-up window at all - `UpdatePlayerValues` mounts a StatsRollout onto the character sheet (DaggerfallCharacterSheetWindow.cs:369-394) - so there is no geometry to cite and THE NATIVE-WINDOW RULE's answer is that nothing native draws: this screen loads no ARENA2, and its words are the port's own (the eight attribute descriptions are annotated in source with the formula each is true of, because TEXT.RSC's records 0..7 are game data the enhanced skin promises not to read). The DEPARTURE is the picture: the eight attributes as one asterism on `ui/pixelGround.js`'s procedural night, a point LIGHTING a star and a line lighting between two raised ones, with the crown, the plate, the skill ribbon and the three vitals in the reference's anatomy. What is NOT borrowed from the reference is its information architecture - Skyrim advances one of three pools, Daggerfall spreads four to six points over eight attributes (a fixed 30 from the Oghma Infinium; a priced purse under ORL1's mod) - so the window offers eight stars and not three choices. **IT HOLDS NO LAW.** a11's rule stands: the Level++ and the health roll are `applyLevelUp`'s, the mod's caps are `commitVirtueLevelUp`'s, and the window is a second FACE on whichever rollout screen the door built, driving it through that screen's own `input` - `canRaise` is `statUp` ASKED whether it moved, never a restatement. **AND IT TEACHES ONE THING DAGGERFALL NEVER SAYS**: the ribbon names, per skill, its part in `SetCurrentLevelUpSkillSum` - every primary counts, the weakest major is dropped, only the best minor counts, a miscellaneous skill counts for nothing - a second reading of one law, held against `levelUpSkillSum` itself over 200 generated careers. **AUDIT LV1 (2026-09-19) ADDED A LAW THE PORT WAS MISSING IN TWO FACES**: CheckIfDoneLeveling's second term, `!PlayerEntity.Stats.IsAllMax()` (:437-443). Without it a character at 100 across the board cannot leave a level-up at all - `statUp` refuses every press, the pool never reaches zero, and both the enhanced window and `ui/charsheet.js`'s LevelUpScreen tested zero alone; the sheet's own rollout had the term since AUDIT 44 but as a PRIVATE method, which is how the other two shipped without it. `DaggerfallStats.IsAllMax` is `ui/chargen.js`'s `allStatsMax` now and all three read it; the unspendable remainder is VOIDED, as DFU voids it, and the window says so. **LV1b (2026-09-19) CLOSED THE TWO THE AUDIT HAD ONLY RECORDED**: the chunk this window is is WARMED at boot in the three hosts that warm the sheet's art (worldModes builds no windows and is named as needing none), and a cold fetch no longer shows a paused game with nothing on it - `paintLevelUpWait` draws, in inline style alone, after 120ms so a warm chunk never flashes it, with the book's own sentence over the book. And `ui/enhancedChronicle.js` closes on `LogBook` off the registry, MAC-C's arm applied to the door it was written for and never given. Recorded rather than hidden: the star NAMES are hidden under 720px wide and 620px tall (a star's box is as wide as the word in it, and the probe caught two of them overlapping on a phone, which is a point spent on the wrong attribute); the figure stretches rather than keeping its aspect on a short stage; and a chunk load that fails leaves the level-up OWED rather than spent, at the cost of one discarded BonusPool draw. Record: `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` LV1. | Mac, 2026-09-18 ("We're going to be working on the next enhanced UI window") | U52, PX27, AUDIT 39, AUDIT 44/a11, ORL1 |

| **THE RISING - THE ENHANCED SKIN ANNOUNCES A LEVEL INSTEAD OF SEIZING THE SCREEN (LV2, 2026-09-19, Mac: "the enhanced level up notification", and the fork he picked: "Notify, then you choose")** - `ui/levelNotice.js` (new: the model, the strip and the three seams), `ui/enhancedStyle.js` (the LV2 block), `ui/hud.js` (ONE enhanced branch, drawn before the HUD), `systems/inputActions.js` (`codeForAction`, the inverse of `actionForCode`, so the standing line names the player's OWN sheet key), `scenes/shared.js` (`raisePlayerSkills`' raise and mastery arms), the `onLevelUp` arm in all four hosts, the `fanfare` option threaded through `ui/charSheetDoor.js` into `ui/charsheet.js` and `ui/virtueLevelUp.js`, the lab's notice lane. **THE DEPARTURE IS WHO OPENS THE WINDOW.** DFU levels you up by PUSHING a screen at you: `RaiseSkills`' tail posts `dfuiOpenCharacterSheetWindow` (PlayerEntity.cs:1413-1414) and the character sheet mounts a StatsRollout, mid-swing, mid-chase, mid-sentence. That is the right law for a 1996 window that paints in a tenth of a second and the wrong one for LV1's full-screen constellation, and it was the one screen in the game a player was GIVEN rather than opening. On the enhanced skin the notice announces, the fanfare plays, and `readyToLevelUp` STAYS SET exactly where DFU leaves it - the window arrives when the player asks the sheet for it, which is where DFU levels you up anyway, since LV1's `charSheetDoor` already returns the Ascension for a pending level to the sheet key, the dial's Stats arm and the pause page alike. **The classic skin is untouched, byte for byte**: the line, the sheet and the mastery's click-anywhere box. **TWO HALVES, KEPT APART ON PURPOSE.** An ANNOUNCEMENT is an EVENT - it holds for `ANNOUNCE_MS` and goes. A REMINDER is a STATE - the level's row folds to a quiet standing line and stays until the points are spent, read LIVE off `entity.readyToLevelUp` and never off a latch, so a level spent by any road this module never watches (the window, the font-less escape, a headless roll, a loaded save) takes the reminder with it, and a level OWED but never announced here still says so. The strip carries all three of the advancement's voices - the level, each skill's rise, and a mastery - because they are one moment to the player and three call sites to the tree; the level row sorts LAST so it is the line left standing. **ONE EVENT, ONE FANFARE**: the notice plays `LevelUp` when it announces, so the window it defers plays none - `fanfareOwed` is the door ASKING whether the sound has already been spent on this exact pending level, not a second latch beside `readyToLevelUp`. Placement is the port's own: Skyrim's notification is top-centre and this HUD's top-centre is taken by the compass strip (and was, until ENH-NOTICE3 moved PopupText's rows to the right-edge notice stack, by the popup column every other line arrived on), so the strip sits above the vitals, pointer-transparent, `role="status"` and `aria-live="polite"`, and hides with the HUD and under the cursor. **AUDIT LV2 (2026-09-19, Mac: "Lets do a comprehensive audit on everything before we merge") FOUND FIVE AND FIXED FOUR**, two of them lessons this tree had already written down and this slice did anyway: a teardown naming a caller that does not exist (AUDIT FONT F12's own struck sentence, one file over, one arc later); AN UNSPENT LEVEL THAT SHOUTED ONCE PER REST, because `checkForLevelUp` stays true while `level` is behind the calculated one and every later pass re-entered the arm - re-OPENING a window is DFU's law (:1413) and the classic lane keeps it, re-ANNOUNCING is a different act and the enhanced arm asks `fanfareOwed` before it speaks now; a TEXT.RSC record read eagerly on the lane that promises to read none, which made this module's own header false until `rows` became a thunk; and THE STRIP SITTING ON THE VITALS - mounted body-level at a flat `bottom: 150px`, while `.hud-bottom` grows upward with its content AND with `--hud-scale`, so with a live block it sat 19px into the vitals at scale 1 on a phone and 154px into them at scale 2, under a probe check that passed because the lab leaves three of that block's four rows empty. It hangs IN the column now, which is QS3's own rule read the right way round, and the probe fills the block and runs a third lane at scale 2. Recorded and then FIXED ON MAC'S WORD ("Yes fucking fix it"): the 4-6 bonus pool re-rolled on every open of the window. That is DFU's shape - `FormulaHelper.BonusPool` is drawn at the rollout's SETUP - and it looked like faithfulness, but it is UNREACHABLE in DFU, whose rollout mounts on the character sheet and commits the level at mount, so there is never an unspent level to re-open on. LV2 is what made it reachable, by design, and "escape and re-open until it says 6" was one keystroke away. The draw is THE LEVEL'S now: `bonusPoolFor` (`systems/advancement.js`) takes it once, remembers it on the entity beside `pendingLevel`, hands it to every screen after, rides the save, and is cleared where `pendingLevel` is cleared - both arms, the Oghma's included. A SECOND deliberate departure, in the opposite direction to AUDIT 23's rule: the port draws FEWER numbers from the stream than DFU, where that rule guards against drawing MORE. Record: `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` LV2 and AUDIT LV2. | Mac, 2026-09-19 ("Next up, I want to implement a new element. The enhanced level up notification"; "Lets do a comprehensive audit on everything before we merge") | LV1, U52, PX27, MENU1, QS3, AUDIT FONT F2/F12, AUDIT 64 F36/F37 |
| **THE SPELLBOOK'S FOUR STANDING DEPARTURES, ROWED (CM3-CM11 cleanup, 2026-09-21)** - `src/ui/spellbookWindow.js` has declared four RECORDED DEPARTURES since its native pass and the only section-A row that named the file was TB1; when CM6 struck TB1 the AUDIT 58 doctrine gate found the four approved by nothing, which is the gate doing its job. They are: (1) no localization LOOKUP - every string is DFU's own en text out of Internal_Strings.csv, held as a constant with its TextManager key named rather than resolved at runtime; (2) DFU's spell VERSION gate (:746-747) has nothing to gate - the port's records carry no bundle version; (3) DFU wires the name label's rename AND the icon panel's picker in BOTH modes (:465, :437; SetupIcons unconditional at :145) and both handlers index the PLAYER's book with the OFFER's index (:929, :962), editing whatever spell sits at that slot - the port gates both to cast mode, and the icon panel's `selectIcon` tooltip with them; (4) buy mode prices the offer with a bare CalculateTotalEffectCosts (:521), no minimumCastingCost, where the LIST rows pass both (:262) - the port has one castCost hook for both, so the offer's price rides the player's live skills either way | DaggerfallSpellBookWindow.cs:145, :262, :437, :465, :521, :746-747, :929, :962 | the departures are the S30/U42 spellbook pass's, unchanged; only the ROW is new - a claim of approval standing in for one, the AUDIT 17m / F7 shape the row above was written to end |
| **THE STATUS READOUT DOES NOT STOP THE WORLD, AND THE PANEL IS AS WIDE AS WHAT IT SAYS (STATUS-LIVE + NOTICE-FIT, 2026-09-22, kurkku through Mac: "would be nice if the info panel that comes up when you press i didn't pause the game, that way you could quickly check your status while walking around" and "the sizing of the boxes to properly adjust for the text instead of always being wide")** - `ui/statusBox.js` (new: the box and the one composer), `systems/statusReadout.js` (new: the live readout, the host's door back out of its slot, and the yield), `ui/actionText.js` (the `pauseWhileOpen` and `noticeHint` fields, and a `dispose` that takes the panel with the box), `ui/input.js` (routeAction's one yield; the binding store pushed down), `ui/enhancedNotice.js` (the box's own caption), `ui/enhancedStyle.js` (`.notice` capped instead of sized), and the four hosts' `showStatus` plus the gates that read the slot where they meant the pause. **THE DEPARTURE IS THAT THE STATUS BOX IS NOT MODAL.** DFU's Status action is a DaggerfallMessageBox (DaggerfallUI.cs:1615-1628) and a window raises `PauseWhileOpen` (UserInterfaceWindow.cs:141), which is right for DFU: the parchment lands in the middle of the screen and there is nothing to see past it. ENH-NOTICE1 moved that box to a panel at the EDGE and kept the modality, so the port ended up with a readout you can see the world behind and still cannot move under - the pause was a leftover of the classic PRESENTATION. A readout is the player's (AUDIT-WH R8), so this ONE box declares DFU's own field false; the field itself is DFU's and every other message box still pauses. **WHAT FOLLOWS, AND IS THE PORT'S**: no AddNextMessageBox chain (nothing routes it a key, so the three pages are one page); no ClickAnywhereToClose (a click while the world runs is a swing, and the panel's caption names the live Status binding rather than promising a gesture it does not honour); a TOGGLE on the Status key, Escape closing it and nothing else, and a YIELD - any action that raises a window takes the slot first, without which a host's own free-slot guard would refuse the character sheet in silence. **AND THE WIDTH**: `.notice` carried a fixed `width`, so "You are healthy." was as wide as a four-paragraph quest box; it is a `max-width` now and the rows set the width. Both skins keep their own face - the classic parchment still auto-sizes as DaggerfallMessageBox does. Record: `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` STATUS-LIVE. | kurkku through Mac, 2026-09-22 ("didn't pause the game, that way you could quickly check your status while walking around") | ENH-NOTICE1 (the panel this is a consequence of), ROAD-B/ROAD-tail (the pause latch and `pauseWhileOpen`), AUDIT-WH R8 (a readout is the player's) |
| **THE NOTICE AT THE EDGE - THE ENHANCED SKIN'S MESSAGE BOX SLIDES IN FROM THE RIGHT (ENH-NOTICE1, 2026-09-21, Mac: "classic DFU has text that shows in the middle of the screen, instead of this, for enhanced I want a panel that slides in from the right side of the screen showing the notification. This should work for any and all mods that utilize this text")** - `ui/enhancedNotice.js` (new: the stack, the keyed panels, the slide, the watchdog, and `noticeDraw`, the one decision), `ui/enhancedStyle.js` (the ENH-NOTICE1 block), `ui/actionText.js` (`ActionTextBox.draw` hands its rows over; `input` releases), `ui/talkWindow.js` (the no-options `ChoiceWindow`, the same two seams), and ENH-NOTICE2's eight classic windows that raise the box from inside themselves (`noticeFrame` in the potion maker, item maker, spell maker, bank, rest, coven, guild service and service flow windows). **THE DEPARTURE IS WHERE THE BOX SITS.** `DaggerfallUI.MessageBox(...)` with ClickAnywhereToClose is a parchment in the MIDDLE of the screen (DaggerfallMessageBox.cs, the popup's VerticalAlignment.Middle default) and the classic skin keeps it there, byte for byte. On the enhanced skin the same box - the same class, the same `done`, the same chain, the same click-anywhere - is a panel that slides in from the RIGHT edge and slides out when dismissed, pointer-transparent so the dismissing click still lands on the canvas. It is still MODAL: the game waits exactly as it waits under the parchment. **WHAT IS NOT A NOTICE STAYS A WINDOW**: a box with buttons, a picker or a text field (`ActionInputBox`, the keyed `ChoiceWindow` menus, the rest window's states) is a decision and keeps its own window on both skins. **WHY EVERY MOD RIDES IT**: the port has two homes for the box and a mod raises one of them; the panel is the homes' face, not a per-caller feature, so no mod is named and none needs to be. **THE WATCHDOG IS THE PORT'S TOO**: a box can leave without a dismissal (a host dropping its overlay on a scene change), and a DOM overlay stays painted unless told otherwise (AUDIT 64 F37), so a panel whose box has not drawn for `NOTICE_WATCHDOG_MS` slides out on its own. Record: `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` ENH-NOTICE1. | Mac, 2026-09-21 ("for enhanced I want a panel that slides in from the right side of the screen showing the notification") | CM1, AUDIT 64 F28/F35/F37, FONT1 (the model-stays-draw-hands shape), LV2 (the strip that set the enhanced-only precedent) |

| **THE ONE DOOR EVERY MESSAGE GOES THROUGH (ENH-NOTICE3, 2026-09-21, Mac: "the new enhanced pop up system isn't working for everything. All mods, including climates and calories need to utilize the enhanced notification popup. This needs a proper detailed audit, maybe a refactor")** - `systems/notify.js` (new: DaggerfallUI's static `MessageBox` written once - the presenter registry, the ladder `mountWindow`, the fallback; `AddHUDText`/`PopupMessage` have a door here too, which the fallback lands on and no shipping producer walks yet: the hosts' own `say`/`hudSay` deps are still AddHUDText's callers - AUDIT ENH-NOTICE3 F4), `scenes/townTalk.js` (the outdoor slot's presenter at priority 0; `showBox` through the door, a push), `scenes/worldModes.js` (the modal modes' presenter at 10 over a hoisted `showQuestOverlay`), `scenes/dungeonContext.js` (the context's presenter at 20, unregistered in `destroy()`; the DaggerfallAction ShowText, the enchant seam), `scenes/world.js` (the Travel Options wiring, the enchant/Azura, quest-machine and holiday seams, the mod-compatibility box), `scenes/exterior.js` (the twins), `scenes/shared.js` (the infection factory without `showText`), `ui/enhancedNotice.js` (`noticeHold`, the held panel for a DOM window), `ui/enhancedTavern.js`, `ui/enhancedInventory.js`, `ui/heldMap.js` (their own notice cards routed to the panel), `ui/huntWindow.js` (the busy page). **THE DEPARTURE IS THE REGISTRY.** DFU's door is a static on `DaggerfallUI` that reaches `uiManager.TopWindow` directly (DaggerfallUI.cs:1328-1362) because DFU has ONE window stack; the port's hosts hold one overlay slot each and no shared stack, so a box has to be OFFERED - each live host registers a presenter (mount, hudText, priority) and the door asks them by priority then recency, the dungeon context before the modal modes before the outdoor slot, which is world.js's showQuestBox ladder written once. A box no presenter takes lands on the HUD line and the handle answers inert (`mounted` false, `addNext` a no-op): FALLBACK, NOT SILENCE (the silent-first-ten-minutes failure U43-ii closed). **WHAT THE AUDIT FOUND AND THE DOOR CLOSED**: six host seams each building a window class by hand (`townTalk.showOverlay(new ChoiceWindow({ lines }))` in the town hosts, `pushDungeonWindow(new ActionTextBox(lines))` underground - the per-host class split `scenes/shared.js`'s infection factory carried as a `showText` dependency, RETIRED); the Travel Options wiring in world.js handing the mod's `DaggerfallUI.MessageBox` lines to `townTalk.say`, so its nine boxes (arrival, the stops, the refusals, the H help) printed as one fading HUD line - THE BUG MAC HIT; three DOM-native windows (the tavern, the inventory, the held map) drawing DFU's click-anywhere box as a card of their own; the hunt window's busy page on the canvas. Every migrated seam is a PUSH, because every `DaggerfallUI.MessageBox` is `PushWindow` (UserInterfaceManager.cs:79-91, ROAD-B B5's law), and the two that REPLACED before (the enchant seam, `showBox`) are fixed by it. **THE LAW**: notify decides WHICH MODEL, the draw decides WHICH FACE - the door never imports the skin, so the classic skin is untouched. **AUDIT ENH-NOTICE3 (2026-09-21, Mac: "Audit before merge")**: the dungeon presenter answers `active` only once its host has ADOPTED the context (`goLive`) and never once dead - the build awaits art for seconds after registering, and DFU has no half-built top window; `pushDungeonWindow` refuses once dead, so worldModes' second door into the same stack refuses too; the quest box rides the door's ladder (`mountWindow`) in both outer hosts instead of a hand copy; the door carries no close callback (two hosts of three had nowhere to put one); the fallback answers an inert handle, never null (the status chains call `addNext` without looking); townTalk's model reports the MODE host's slot beside its own, so an interior window hides the toasts (AUDIT FONT F4's law, held in the fourth host); a box stands ABOVE the toasts in the stack. **SECOND PASS** (three lanes, the same day): the adoption moved BEFORE the mode flip and pinned as exactly one call, the dead latch declared above its first reader (a TDZ throw), the toasts hidden by the previousWindow CHAIN (above) not the pause latch, the stack capped and clipped on a short viewport, the watchdog's release clearing its owner's id set, the interior slot's font-less arm disposing what it drops (the tavern's held panel outlived it), the tavern's click-catcher made the whole screen (a scrim inside the window left the panel's own place dead), a `hint` option so the hunt's busy page, the pack's and the held map's refusals promise no dismissal they do not take, the stack lifted off a z-index tie with the update scrim, the scale setter named for the one label it scales, and six cites the shifter had carried along already wrong re-resolved and anchored. **NOT MOVED, LISTED**: the status-box chains (DisplayStatusInfo, four hosts), the rest/mastery `box:` deps, the exhaustion boxes, the keyed `ChoiceWindow` menus and the court flow (boxes WITH buttons, which the door does not mint), charsheet's own child - each a plain box a later pass moves with its C# in hand. | **THE HUD LINE AS A TOAST - PopupText's ROWS RIDE THE NOTICE STACK (ENH-NOTICE3, 2026-09-21, Mac: "All mods, including climates and calories need to utilize the enhanced notification popup")** - `ui/hudText.js` (a row id, `frame().ids`, the enhanced draw through `drawEnhancedToasts`, `hide`/`dispose` likewise), `ui/enhancedNotice.js` (`drawEnhancedToasts`, `releaseEnhancedToasts`, the `toast` and `hold` frame flags), `ui/enhancedStyle.js` (`.notice.notice-toast`; the FONT1 popup column's rules, tops and chat-peek offsets GONE), `ui/enhancedHudText.js` (the column's draw, release, stack and scale write GONE - the mid-screen label and the online status line stay), `tools/font1Probe.mjs` (measures the toasts now). **THE DEPARTURE**: DFU's PopupText (PopupText.cs, `DaggerfallUI.AddHUDText`) is a column of centred rows at the TOP of the HUD panel that scrolls up as its front row leaves, and the classic skin keeps it byte for byte. FONT1 gave the enhanced skin that same column in the pixel face; ENH-NOTICE1 then put the message box at the right edge, and a player read the column as "not the notification" - Climates & Calories' hunger, thirst and cold, the Ambient Text mod's street lines, the torches' burn-out, every skill-up and loot tally are exactly this kind. So on the enhanced skin each PopupText row is a TOAST in the notice stack: its own panel, no hint (nothing dismisses a toast), the popup's yellow on the box's dark, keyed by the row's id under its model's key, in while PopupText's timer keeps the row and sliding out when the model pops it. **THE MODEL DID NOT MOVE**: the queue, the timer, the rubberband, the maxRows+1 cut, the notebook tail and the classic draw are PopupText's as FONT1 left them; a window that CUTS the previousWindow chain still hides the rows (AUDIT 64 F35's `hudCovered`, asked of this slot and the mode host's since the second audit's C2 - AUDIT FONT F4 had asked whether the slot was OCCUPIED, which hid the toasts under the very box the stack was built to stand beside; DFU paints PopupText under a pushed box, dimmed, DaggerfallPopupWindow.cs:77-85) and the hide door still says so on every gated frame (AUDIT 64 F37). **WHAT FONT1'S AUDIT LOSES**: the column's placement laws (F2's scale, F7's chat-peek step, F8's measured slide, F9's narrow top) had no surface left to hold; their records in `tools/mutants/font1.json` are re-aimed BY CONTENT at the toast laws (26 records, all dead). And where the mod says `"Loot Pile"` the port LISTS what is in the pile, which is PX21c's own departure carried forward rather than dropped: the mod's naming ladder and the port's item rows are two states of one surface, because they answer one ray. The classic skin has neither, byte for byte. **THE LAW BOTH HALVES KEEP**: the plaque and the button can never disagree, and the way it is kept is that there is exactly ONE race - `pickActivatableHit`, the pick the press already walks - so the race decides WHO WON and the model only decides what the winner is CALLED. A key the ladder has no word for draws NOTHING (the mod's own behaviour, an empty `ret` leaving the tooltip down at .cs:169-172), which is also what stops an unported family labelling itself with its key string. **AND IT IS OFF ON A TOUCH DEVICE**, where the activation ray is through the FINGER (`_tapPoint` -> `_tapDir` through `rayDirFromScreen`) rather than the crosshair: a centre-anchored plaque would name what a tap would not open, which is the law above broken on every frame. The tap-anchored variant is a later slice's, not this one wearing a media query. **THREE THINGS PX21c GOT AWAY WITH BY ACCIDENT ARE NOW LAWS**: its guard compared the KEY alone and could not see a list that changed under one (safe only because taking needed a window and the window unmounted the driver) and compares the rendered signature now; its 10 Hz throttle was bought on a guess that a raycast over every pile is not free, where the measured pick is ~3 microseconds of a 16.7 ms frame, so the throttle bought nothing and cost a plaque lagging the crosshair by a tenth of a second; and an open window took the plaque down only because the driver happened not to run, where `cursorActive` - the crosshair's own first statement - now says so. **THE COST SHAPE, MEASURED NOT GUESSED**: the ray is ~3 us and the pick ~21 ns a target, while BUILDING the target list is 95-97% of a hover (a streaming city's door list is ~200 us and ~4,500 allocations a frame - the PERF-TOWN1 shape), so the seam takes a THUNK and a host hands over a list it already holds. Record: `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` WORLD-HOVER. | Mac, 2026-09-21 (the zip, and the two caveats) | PX21c (the plaque), AUDIT 39 (the skin gate above `ensure()`), AUDIT 65 MC-2 (the reach gate), ROAD-E E5 (the reticle moves), AUDIT 17i (one construction seam), AUDIT 64 F37 (a DOM overlay stays painted), ENH-NOTICE1 (the enhanced face precedent) |

THE DEPARTURE, stated plainly: DFU has no quick loot and no mod under `vendor/` carries one, so this is a port of NOTHING and the row says so rather than implying a source. Vanilla Skyrim has none either - what everyone means by the name is the QuickLoot mod (LE's QuickLoot, then QuickLoot RE/EE on SE), whose shape is a list beside the crosshair on look, the wheel moving a highlight through it, the activate key taking the lit row, a key of its own taking the lot, and the cursor never freed. That is what this is, adapted to the plaque Arc A already draws. DFU's OWN precedent for taking with no window is real and narrow - a body holding nothing but arrows is taken whole and no window opens (PlayerActivate.cs:948-952) - and this generalises that one case to any row the player has picked out, which is exactly the size of the departure. WHAT IS UNCHANGED: the take goes through the container's own loot hooks, the very handle a host already gives the inventory window as its remote target, so there is ONE mutable way into a container and not two; the gold rule is `takeOneInto`'s, which is DoTransferItem's first statement, so gold reaches the counter and never the item list; the pool keeps the empty-body refusal and the arrows case, because the take sits INSIDE its `openWindow` callback rather than ahead of it. OFF (`quick-loot`, the player's own, on by default) is Daggerfall's loot exactly: the activate key opens the window it has always opened. Record: `06-Systems/Systems.md` QUICK-LOOT, `10-UI/UI-Arc.md` WORLD-HOVER. **DISC22-C (2026-09-24, Satranath: "no hotbar or quickloot on grimoire either"; Mac's parchment: "a spritesheet to be used for the loot menu (grimoire UI)"): THE CLASSIC SKINS TAKE IT TOO.** The one gate over the whole resolve was the DOM plaque's (`worldPlaqueOn`); `ui/worldPlaque.js` worldHoverFrame now resolves and folds on the classic skins while quick loot is on (`classicPlaqueOn`), leaves the frame in `systems/quickLoot.js` (`classicLootFrame`, held with its frame mark) and `ui/hud.js` draws it through `ui/classicLootPanel.js` - Mac's own parchment under the GrimoireUI pack (`public/art/grimoire-loot-parchment.png`, cut at its two gold rules and grown to the rows), DFU's tooltip box without.
