| **WORLD OF DAGGERFALL, 1:1 (WOD1, 2026-09-23, Mac: "This will be our next mod to implement 1:1")** - `vendor/world-of-daggerfall/`, `tools/worldOfDaggerfallAssets.mjs`, `world/wodLocationData.js`, `world/wodLocationPack.js`. Kamer's World of Daggerfall 2.0, whose bundle ships its whole implementation as C# TextAssets - so the eight sources are vendored byte for byte and every port file cites them - and whose content is 65 prefab layouts (vendored verbatim) and 2,413 instance lists (227,938 instances, 61.0 MB of XML). THE ONE STRUCTURAL CHANGE IS THE INSTANCE LISTS' CARRIAGE: each region folder is one pack of the ported `LoadLocationInstance`'s OUTPUT in the mod's own load order (NTFS name order within a folder, document order within a file), and `locations.json` names every source file with its sha256 and instance count, so the archive re-derives each pack byte for byte - a cache of the author's files, not a re-authoring (the Eye Of The Beholder precedent: 1,035 TextAssets folded to one table). PORTED 1:1: the reader's .NET `TryParse` (an `out` zeroed on failure - 3,000 empty coordinates read 0,0 and 155,398 IDs read 0), the shared tag index, the empty-prefab skip, `ValidateValue`. THE READINGS the arc page records: the culture is the author's (en-US), and the order the loader reads a folder in is NTFS's, which the mod's Windows build gets and which decides 51,355 contested pixels. WOD2 (the same day) STANDS IT in `scenes/world.js` through `world/worldOfDaggerfall.js`, `world/wodLocationLoader.js` and `world/wodLocationObjects.js`: AddLocation's decision before the kernel, the smoothing arms IN the kernel (`world/terrainGen.js`) after the tiles and before the grid and the nature, the objects pixel-local after the location block - models with their colliders and no climate swap or doors, the markers invisible (the billboard shader's alpha test), the mod's OWN AddLight (DaggerfallInterior's but for record 0, a default arm and record 29's lift) lit at every hour on the per-light colour channel, the scaled rocks lit through the inverse transpose (`render/staticBatch.js`). THREE READINGS MORE: the region load comes before the build it feeds (Unity does not order StreamingWorld and PlayerGPS); a folderless region's exception storm is not reproduced; ONLINE THE LIST IS THE ROOM'S - every folder, 17 first then ascending - because the reference's list is a function of one player's travels and the mod levels the ground the room must share. The grass (the lane's own) keeps off a site as DFU's nature keeps off the loader's rect. WOD3 (the same day) SPRINGS THE MARKERS: `world/wodSpawner.js` is `LocationEnemySpawner` as one machine - Start's 300 spends a marker for good, Update's 100 springs it once, and Unity's exclusive `Range` leaves the Barbarian and the Healer unreachable - and `world/groundAlign.js` is GameObjectHelper's two ground aligns, ported once. The foes are the exterior pool's, PLACED: outside the encounter cap and never distance-culled, because DFU's loose enemies stand until a load or a teleport sweeps them and `SerializableEnemy` saves every one. The piles are `GenerateLoot` at dungeon type 3 under LR1 and die with their pixel. Online each client springs its own markers (and, since the AUDIT BRANCH, a placed foe never rides and hunts no peer). WOD4 (the same day) STANDS THE CAMP AT PRIVATEER'S HOLD: `world/wodPrivateersHold.js` is DungeonExterior's search for block CUSTAA30 and PrivateersHold's Start as data, pinned line for line against the vendored C# - 33 models, 17 centred flats, five FireLights burning at every hour, seven foes each on Range(0, 30) > 20. The foes are placed but the BLOCK's, so they die with its pixel. THREE READINGS: the camp stands re-skinned in the location's climate from the start (DFU gives its meshes default textures until the location's next season or city-lights change, a Unity execution-order race that can show only in winter until the next dusk or dawn); a rebuild the reference never makes (a season re-skin, the roads sweep and retry) carries each marker's state and the camp's roll across, and a sweep drops the carry (the AUDIT BRANCH made an unload a POOL: the site rides for as long as DFU's terrain array keeps the slot); and exterior.js, which lays out RMB blocks, stands the camp too, its fires lit beside the lanterns in their own colour. WOD5 (the same day) IS THE AUDIT: an independent pass against the eight sources found four behaviours the port did not yet share, all fixed. The mirrored wall (one model at scaleX -1.57 in 565 instances) is batched with its winding reversed, as Unity reverses the culling. The region is heard on the crossing itself, so a visit shorter than a build no longer goes unheard. A captive stood by a spent marker rides the carry. The marker's loot pile, a LoadID-0 container in the C#, stays out of the save and the scene cache. One C# throw is recorded as a reading and not reproduced: a marker's OnLoad handler outlives its marker. THE FOUR HOSTS: world.js wired; exterior.js flagged for the loader (no terrain) and wired for the camp; worldModes.js and dungeonContext.js flagged. THE AUDIT BRANCH (the same day, Mac: "Lets do a comprehensive audit before we decide to merge. This needs to be a perfect integration and hopefully bug free"): after merging main, five lenses read the whole branch and every finding was reproduced before it was fixed. B1, the blocker: the nine Mountains layouts' rock at a scale of about a million filed each face under millions of 2-unit collider cells and froze the tab; a triangle over 64 cells is filed on a 64-unit grid, or on the bucket's short list, and the same triangles are found. M1: placed foes rode and took every one of a peer's eight puppet slots; they never ride and hunt no peer now. WOD7 (the same day) shares a camp's foes again, tagged with their marker and under a camp allowance of their own: the first player to spring a marker owns its camp, and every reader spends its own copy. M2: one bad or hung region pack stopped the stream; the chain skips it, a fetch stalls out and retries, and the pick cannot fail a pixel. L1-3: DFU pools a terrain that leaves range, so one step back finds the site as it was left; `TerrainSlots` models the array. Four minors (a late pile, stale grass, a Start against a stale position, a sweep mid-rebuild) and the docs' numbers. `test/audit_wod_branch.test.js`, its mutants dead. What it found and left open, taken care of the same day (Mac: "Make the best decisions and take care of the found properly"): TERRAIN-SCALE1 - DFU's game scene streams at TerrainScale 1.25 (the prefab's 1.5 is only its default), so every height consumer reads 1.25; saves, scene caches and anchors carry the scale, and a height written on 1.5 is stood again on today's ground as it lands; the interior cache measures from the building (DFU's localPosition), and the save keeps its torches and camps. BUILD-FAIL1 - a pixel build that throws frees its collider bucket, gates, doors and GPU resources. WOD6 - a region pack that fails every try is tried again in the background and lands in the list's order, the pixels it names built again; an arrival's markers meet Start from the scene origin, as InitWorld holds the player there, and a load raises OnLoad last. `test/terrainscale1.test.js`, `tools/mutants/terrainscale1.json`. Left a reading: DFU saves a treasure marker as a chest and shows it after a load. `03-World/World-Of-Daggerfall.md` | Mac, 2026-09-23; the author's permission confirmed the same day (`vendor/world-of-daggerfall/README.md`) | WOD1 |

(4) the LUT is rebuilt when the shift changes, a time slice a frame with the image shown plain meanwhile - a block at a time, at most 128 a frame, streamed into its texture a z-slab at a time (the second pass's J3/J4) - and freed when retro mode goes off, a failed shift tried again once the shift or retro mode changes (F5), where DFU builds it once a session in one go; (5) the no-mips switch caps the textures already loaded too and lifts with retro off, where DFU's arm latches an instance field (TextureReader.cs:207, :469) - in DFU what loads after retro goes off still has no chain; replacement textures are passed by, as TryImportTexture's are in DFU; (6) a cursor pick maps through the presented rect, where DFU's retro pick divides by Screen.width (PlayerActivate.cs:294) and misses by the pillarbox; (7) the _HUD twin is chosen by the DRAWN bar - the rect's - where DFU asks the settings (RetroRenderer.cs:425), whose docked bar is drawn when they say so (DaggerfallHUD.cs:213-216) once play starts - for the loading frames before, DFU itself pairs the 320x154 texture with a full-screen rect (:330-338), and Shift-F10 hides the bar and keeps its rect (:347-351), as the port does (H3); the enhanced skin draws none (AUDIT RETRO1 A1/C3); (8) DFU's camera-clear quirks are not reproduced - CameraClearManager reads retro once at Start (:37-47), so with retro on at launch interiors keep stale pixels where nothing draws, and ViewportChanger's early return (:73) leaves a stale camera.rect when retro is toggled over a docked bar; the port clears black every frame (AUDIT RETRO1 A5); (9) the -sky test reads the port's depth, which holds the nature billboards (DFU's _CameraDepthTexture takes queues up to 2500, and with NatureBillboardShadows the batches are Transparent, DaggerfallBillboardBatch.shader:23 - DFU leaves trees against the sky untouched) and reads the enhanced far ring, drawn at the far plane, as sky (AUDIT RETRO1 A6/B7); (10) the Morrowind first-person arms, which have no DFU original, are drawn on the canvas after the image, on either lane (the lane's shaders end in `elEncode`, display bytes - the second pass's F8/G4), where retro off puts them, at the canvas's lens - a shot's orb leaves up to ~94 px further inboard of the drawn barrel at 1920x1080 in 4:3 than with retro off, none at 16:10, at the arm's own FieldOfView of 60 (AUDIT RETRO1 C2, I11); (11) Shift-F11 only toggles - in DFU the same press also QUICK-LOADS during play: F11 is QuickLoad's (InputManager.cs:1032), a DialogShortcut chord is no combo that suppresses it (:1683-1685), and GameManager's Update prompts after the HUD's (GameManager.cs:577-584), loading without asking unless mods conflict (SaveLoadManager.cs:489-517); the port's toggle consumes the key (AUDIT RETRO1's second pass, H1). The LUT is DFU's byte for byte: AUDIT RETRO1 modelled .NET's unstable IntrospectiveSort - 4 of 32 k-d leaves come out in another order, and no texel moves at any shift 0..8 | OURS - the eleven above; the rest is DFU's | Mac 2026-09-24 (RETRO1; AUDIT RETRO1, "Audit this", and its second pass, "One more audit") |

| **A DUEL BETWEEN TWO PLAYERS, INSIDE A RING OF LIGHT (DUEL1, 2026-09-24, Mac: "I want to be the foundation of pvp. When inspecting a player, they should be able to send an invite to duel which then traps both players in a surrounding transparent hographic wall that keeps them from going outside of the duel space")** - `net/duelSession.js` (the ask, the answer, the count, the ring, the end and its reasons, the re-ask quiet), `net/duelRecord.js` (the record's read and its words), `combat/duelCombat.js` (a peer's blow and spell as a stub attacker through `calculateAttackDamage`, bounded by the look's own bounds and the ring), `render/duelWall.js` (the wall of light, added onto the frame, no collider), `ui/duelPrompt.js` (the challenge's prompt), over `net/wire.js` (`{t:'duel'}`, `validDuelData`, `world107`), `server/src/index.js` (the duel arm, its own funnel, the account stamp), `player/motor.js` `_keepInArena`, `characters/playerEntity.js` `hurtPlayer`'s `spare` (the 1 HP floor), `systems/effects.js` (a duel's bundle tagged `bundleDuel`), `scenes/hostMagic.js`, `scenes/worldModes.js` `DUEL_DOOR_TEXT`, `scenes/world.js` (the wiring), `ui/profileWindow.js` + `ui/enhancedAccount.js` (the K/D line) and `server-account/` (`0008_duels.sql`, `/v1/duel/loss`, `/v1/duel/record`, `acct8`). **THE DEPARTURE**: Daggerfall Unity has no other players, so nothing here is a port of anything; and the port's own online law (Multiplayer.md) was that players never hurt one another. A duel is the ONE exception: two players who both said yes, outdoors in the world host, inside a ring of `DUEL_RADIUS_M` 12 metres round the point between them. Each side's blows, arrows and spells are resolved by the DEFENDER's own client through DFU's own formulas (the attacker a stub carrying the peer's look, level, stats, skills and weapon), so nobody's client writes another's health. The loser drops to 1 HP instead of dying, both are fully healed when the duel ends, and the loss is written once to the loser's account - the per-account wins and losses shown in the main menu's account row, the profile window and a player's Inspect card. Held while it runs: the ring (the motor's clamp; the wall is only light), doors, fast travel, rest and the journey. Known limits, stated in `06-Systems/Community-Arc.md` DUEL1: no server authority over a blow (a modified client can lie about its own), a quick yield is a free full heal, a pair can farm each other within `DUEL_PAIR_DAY_MAX` a day (AUDIT DUEL1, the same day: only between two registered accounts, a winner counts at most `DUEL_WINNER_DAY_MAX` a day from anyone, and a double knockout is a draw), mounted duels are not special-cased, a reflected spell lands nowhere, and onlookers are not held by the ring. Pinned: `test/duel_session.test.js`, `test/duel_wire.test.js`, `test/duel_combat.test.js`, `test/duel_wall.test.js`, `test/duel_record.test.js`, `test/auditduel1.test.js` (AUDIT DUEL1's fixes), `tools/mutants/duel.json`, `tools/mutants/auditduel1.json`. | OURS - the damage, the hit roll, the spell effects and their resistances are DFU's own formulas; that one player can strike another, the ring, its wall, the floor, the heal and the record are not | Mac, 2026-09-24 |

THE DEPARTURES, three, each the port's answer where DFU's would break: (1) DFU registers a mod's quest action only while the mod is loaded, and a disabled mod raises no ambush - here the switch can go off mid-voyage, or a save carrying the quest can load with the mod off, so "Leave Ship" is registered whatever the switch says; only the mod's own quests say it, so the template is inert without them. (2) `TransportManager.TransportMode = Ship` set from code while the player is inside a building would, in DFU, teleport the streaming world out from under a player PlayerEnterExit still holds inside (UpdateMode never leaves the building); the picker's row is outdoors-only, but "Leave Ship" can run below decks - the port leaves the building first (`forceExitToExterior`, as its every teleport does), then sails. (3) DFU calls nothing on a mod's save interface for a new game, so a mod's statics carry from one character into the next within a session (Warm Ashes' `tempShip` would keep every later voyage peaceful); the port's new game hands every registered mod its NewSaveData. Not departures, and recorded beside them: the world host's quest tick now holds while the world moves and while the HUD fades - QuestMachine.Update's own gate (:310-316) - and the ship's teleport opens black and fades back as UpdateMode's ship arm does (TransportManager.cs:364, :401). `03-World/Warm-Ashes-Ships.md`. | **ILIAC PUDDLE NO MORE: THE COASTLINE BUILT FROM THE PLAYER'S OWN WORLD, THE SEA'S FOG ON EVERY FRAGMENT, THE SWIMMER WITHOUT THE FORGED DUNGEON, THE SCHOOLS CARRIED ACROSS THE RECENTRE (DW-A to DW-E4, 2026-09-25, Mac: "All mods attached are to be compatible and implemented 1:1")** - `vendor/iliac-puddle-no-more/`, `world/deepWatersBake.js`, `world/deepWatersBakeCache.js`, `world/deepWatersClient.js`, `world/deepWatersWorker.js` (DW-A); `world/deepBathymetry.js`, `world/deepWaterFloor.js`, `scenes/deepWatersHost.js` (DW-B); `render/deepWatersRender.js`, `render/fogGlsl.js` (`dwWaterFog`) (DW-C); `scenes/deepWatersPlayer.js`, `scenes/deepWatersSwimMove.js`, `player/motor.js` (`afloatMessageStep`) (DW-D); `world/deepWaterRuntime.js` (the transient reset, the post-transition refresh, the work gates), `world/deepWaterTransients.js` (DW-E1); `world/underwaterDecorations.js`, `scenes/deepWatersDecor.js`, `render/deepWatersRender.js` (`DECOR_VS`, `DECOR_FS`, `COLUMN_GLSL`) (DW-E2); `world/passiveFish.js`, `scenes/deepWatersEncounters.js`, `scenes/deepWatersFish.js`, `systems/deepWatersFishItems.js`, `render/deepWatersRender.js` (`streamDecorations`, the cut-out per group), `ui/nativeInventory.js` (`_remoteTargetIcon`: FishLootIcon), `tools/iliacPuddleAssets.mjs` (DW-E3); `world/underwaterEnemies.js`, `scenes/deepWatersEncounters.js` (`createEnemySpawner`, `trySpawnTreasureGuards`), `scenes/exteriorFoes.js` (`transformY`, `team`, `transient`, `managed`, `waterLevelY`) (DW-E4). THE DEPARTURES, seven, each where the mod leans on something this port does not have: (1) THE COASTLINE IS BUILT, NOT SHIPPED. The mod loads a 356 MB bake from its bundle - `DistanceBakeVanilla` over DFU's own terrain, `DistanceBake` once World of Daggerfall or another terrain sampler is in (DeepWaters.LoadDistanceBake) - and each is a render of WOODS.WLD, MAPS.BSA and BLOCKS.BSA, so neither enters this tree. The port builds the planes on the player's machine from the player's own files by the rules the vanilla file proves (the distance and edge planes byte-exact over the file's own masks, all 32 million cells) and caches them in IndexedDB against the archive and the location rects: ONE bake, of the ground the port stands on, World of Daggerfall's locations included - the carve has to match the ground - and the Perlin row above is the whole of its difference from the author's. (2) THE SEA'S DISTANCE FOG IS APPLIED PER FRAGMENT. UnderwaterDistanceFog is a post effect over the camera's image and depth; the port's classic frame has no depth texture, so the same arithmetic runs after the world fog in every world program (`dwWaterFog`) and in a pass of its own over the sky's pixels (`drawSkyFog` - the fog is affine in the colour under it, and two blends apply it). Each pixel's frontmost surface takes the fog of its own distance, as under the post effect; a blend over it is the fog of the blend where the two share a distance, which the surface and what shows through it do (the ray capped at the sea for both). The port's own air effects that write no depth - the sand, the wisps, the bolts - stand down under the fog, which would close what lies behind them. (3) THE WATER COLUMN'S ALPHA IS SPLIT ACROSS TWO DRAWS. TransparentWaterSurfaceTop reads the depth texture for the column behind the surface - alpha = a + (1 - a) min(behind / vision, 1); the port carries the floor, the one thing a carved sea covers, toward the surface's colour by min(behind / vision, 1) (behind: its own eye depth past the point its view ray crosses the sea) and blends the surface at a - mix(mix(x, s, t), s, a) = mix(x, s, a + (1 - a) t), the same picture. The seafloor's decorations take the same share, each fragment its own (DW-E2, `COLUMN_GLSL` - the mod's batches write the depth texture the top reads); the fish take it in the same program (DW-E3), the deep's foes and the sunken loot as DW-E4 and DW-E5 land them. (4) THE SWIMMER WITHOUT THE FORGED DUNGEON. OutdoorSwimDfuBridge.ForgeDungeonState raises PlayerEnterExit.isPlayerInsideDungeon for the Update window of every forged frame (execution order -32000 to 32000), so DFU's own dungeon arm (PlayerEnterExit.cs:379-412) swims the player, and StreamingWorld.Update stops (the mod's DeepWaterSwimWorldTracker moves the GPS in its place). The port hands the swim, the submersion and the water audio state over directly and never raises the flag. The arm's swim agrees by construction - the forged level is built so the arm's test is the driver's - but for the level's own 2.5 cm grid at the hysteresis edge, where the arm would drop the swim for the frame and the post phase raise it again; its submersion is re-derived in the window (the head under the forged line, 0.75 m over the sea) and the forge's own head test restored after it, so the breath (PlayerEntity.FixedUpdate) and every later reader see the forge's - the port's one reading - and only a reader of the same execution order (AmbientEffectsPlayer's bubbles) could see the arm's, in an order Unity leaves undefined. The arm's afloat line ("You are carrying too much to stay afloat.", :395-404) is ported - at sea while forged, and in the dungeons that never had it. Not reproduced, the flag's other readers in the window: PlayerEntity.SpawnCityGuards (the watch held while forged), PlayerAmbientLight's target (held), EnablePlayerTorch's settings-off arm (itself not ported - `systems/playerTorch.js`), and the readers at orders 1-4 - DaggerfallUI's windows, QuestMachine's Place and CreateFoe, a save taken in the window - that would take the sea for a dungeon for that frame (the mod's own "Outdoor Spell Parent" exists to keep GetBestParent from throwing there). (5) THE TERRAIN COLLIDER GATE. The mod turns the terrain's collider off over the sea (UpdateWaterTerrainColliderGate) so a swimmer can pass under the vanilla ground; the port's carved `heightAt` already is the floor, on the floor mesh's own triangles, and the floor's walls stand as collider meshes the shore probes skip (`host.wallBuckets`, IsShoreGround). (6) THE PERIPHERAL-LOCATION SKIP. While the player is in or over deep water, or any streamed terrain is ocean-connected, the mod has StreamingWorld skip building every location but the player's own pixel's (and an owned ship's) and builds them once the player is clear of the sea (SkipPeripheralLocationUpdates, PumpDeferredLocationRestore) - a cost measure for DFU's streamed world; the port builds its locations in its own pipeline (`world/deepWaterRuntime.js` says so at the load grace), so a coast's neighbouring towns stand as they do without the mod. (7) THE RECENTRE CARRIES THE SCHOOLS. DFU's FloatingOrigin moves the world a map pixel's width when the player crosses into a new pixel (FloatingOrigin.FixedUpdate: the player, StreamingTarget's children, the OnPositionUpdate event), and a fish, parented to its column's terrain (SpawnPassiveFish, the re-parent in ClampToWater), moves with it; the mod answers no such event, so what it keeps in world space stays where the world was - a school's centre (PassiveFishSchool.center), which its fish steer for (`school.Center + schoolOffset`), and a fish's last safe place (PassiveFishBehaviour.lastSafePosition), where a fish that meets shallow water is put back - and after the crossing a school's fish swim for a point 819.2 m off and a stranded fish jumps back to the old world's coordinates. The port moves both with the world on its recentre (`offsetAll`): the school keeps its water and the fish its retreat, as the author's play inside one pixel shows them. Not departures, and recorded beside them: the mod lowers Unity's Time.maximumDeltaTime to 0.1 s at its install, which is the hosts' own frame clamp already (`Math.min(0.1, ...)`), so under it GuardSwimMotorFrameSpike (LevitateMotor disabled for a swimmer's frame past 0.1 s) can only fire when a raised time scale - Travel Options' accelerated journey - lifts the frame past the bar, and it is ported so (`levitateMotorEnabled`); PlayerShipWaterlineFix is a no-op here, measured - the two ship pixels (2,2) and (5,5) sample flat at the ocean elevation, so the ship's location already stands at the sea; and a heightfield STEP (the carve's edge) is no slope to the collider (restFloor and groundNormal take the gentler one-sided grade), a correction of the port's own collider, not of DFU. DW-E3, also not departures: the fish items draw their pictures from archives of their own (the template's index, record 0) where the mod's templates name TEXTURE.216 records 41-47 and its icon cache (ItemHelper's, CacheInventoryIcons) answers those keys with the fish - no classic template draws from those records (all 288 measured), a dropped pile never takes an item's picture (DaggerfallInventoryWindow's drop icons), and the cache reaches no world billboard, so every picture the player sees is the mod's; the fish's pictures are the vendored files, fetched as the world starts, and a species spawns once its picture is in (BuildSpawnableCache's rule for a picture that is not); and a fish's obstacle probe (Physics.Raycast, every layer, triggers ignored) meets what the port's world is made of - the collider's meshes, the ground walked in 5 cm steps under a probe of a metre or less, the player's capsule and every standing foe's and guard's. DW-E4, not departures either: a deep foe is the exterior pool's own and stands as its foes do, a few frames after its spawn is pumped (its career and its picture load first) - the tracker's entry and the live count are the spawn's at once, and one that never stands gives its count back as the mod's failed SpawnEnemy does (only the treasure guards' own tally, which nothing reads, cannot see a late failure); the exterior pool's own relevance cull (120 m, the encounter pool's allocation guard - DFU has none) passes them by (`managed`): they stand, as DFU's loose enemies do, until the mod's spawner releases them, and its cap bounds them; and a foe's WaterMove reads the level the swim driver leaves (`waterLevelY`), as every EnemyMotor reads the forged blockWaterLevel - an aquatic foe swims while the player is in the sea's water context and freezes once the driver lets go, the mod's own behaviour, and the forged dungeon flag reaches no enemy code (EnemySenses reads only the castle's, which the mod does not forge). `03-World/Deep-Waters.md`. Pinned: `test/dwa_bake.test.js`, `test/dwb_world.test.js`, `test/dwc_fog.test.js`, `test/dwd_swim.test.js`, `test/dwe_runtime.test.js`, `test/dwe_decorations.test.js`, `test/dwe_fish.test.js`, `test/dwe_enemies.test.js`. | Approved (DW-A to DW-E4) |
| **THE OBLIVION GATE'S OMEN - A WORLD BOSS ON THE SHARED CLOCK, FIRST SLICE (WB1, 2026-09-25, Mac: "at any point in the world, on the timer, a large area would be shown on the map, also in chat like you suggested. A gate model would be spawned with a timer that leads to a completely different area, a gate of oblivion")** - `net/gateLaw.js` (the schedule: `gateTimes`, `gateAt`, `gatePhase`, `gateCountdown`, `countdownText`; the room `gate:<day>` - `gateRoomKey`, `isGateRoom`, `gateAdmits`, `gateHolds`; the rolls - `gateHash`, the provinces' shuffle bag `pickGateRegion`, `pickGatePixel`, `gateSpotLocal`, `omenRing`; `GATE_BOSSES`; the chat's words), `systems/gateSite.js` (`scanGatePixels`, `findGateSite` - land, no location on or beside it, a fast-travel town two to four pixels off, no spawned dungeon), `systems/gateOmen.js` (`createGateOmen` - each moment's line once, the map's mark, the compass's), `ui/gateMapMark.js` (the mark's law for both maps), `ui/inkMap.js` `paintGateRing`, `ui/heldMap.js` and `ui/travelMapWindow.js` (the ring on each), `ui/enhancedHud.js` `drawGateMark`, `scenes/world.js` (`gateOmen`, `gateFrame`, `gateCompassMark`, the travel map's `gate`). Daggerfall has no world events and no other players: the whole system is the port's own, online alone. A gate a game day - the omen at 17:00 (a ring two map pixels wide on both maps, a line on every chat tab), the gate at 19:00, open 20:00-22:00, the wrath at midnight - HH:32:30 UTC every even hour, off the shared clock (WORLD5), so it costs the relay no frame; the site a hash of the day over the world's own data, spawned dungeons' law. The design and the four slices after this one: `11-Multiplayer/World-Bosses.md`. No relay change in this slice (the law imports wire.js alone and the relay does not import it yet). | Mac, 2026-09-25 (Option B, world bosses first) |

| **HIS VOICE AND HIS MUSIC - THE BURNING COURT HEARD (WB7, 2026-09-25, Mac: "Proper boss audio during the boss fight")** - `systems/gateScore.js` (THE WARDEN'S SCORE, WRITTEN AS NOTES: Daggerfall has no fight music, so four songs in the shape an HMI song decodes to, on the FM bank's own articulations - GATEWAR1 at 132 BPM (i-VI-iv-V under a pizzicato ostinato, timpani, brass stabs, the Warden's theme, the choir), GATEWAR2 at 138 (the choir from the first bar, the kit rolling, the theme dotted and high), GATEWAR3 at 150 (the Neapolitan Eb, the kick on every beat, the tritone tolled), GATEFELL (a timpani roll into D major, fanfare, choir, bells); and `courtScoreFor` - the war song of his phase, the third in the Wrath's last minute, the fanfare for 12.5 s after his fall, then quiet, nothing over the landed Wrath), `systems/music.js` (`registerSong` - a song made in code answers for its own name before MIDI.BSA is asked, and is then any song: the same player, fades, volume, and replaceable by a music pack), `world/gateBoss.js` + `scenes/gateCourt.js` (HIS BODY: a step each stride of his walk or charge, a growl between his attacks and never during one, a grunt when a share of his health goes, the ground's shock under a slam, a charge, a nova and the Wrath, thunder over his roar at a phase, his body meeting the floor 1.5 s into his fall - Daggerfall's own clips pitched for his size), `scenes/world.js` (`gateScoreFrame` - the court holds the music while it stands, ahead of the music director, and lets it go stopped the frame it is gone), `tools/gateScoreProbe.mjs` (the songs played through the real song player offline and measured). Not a DFU member: the score is the port's own composition; the voice is Daggerfall's clips. | Mac, 2026-09-25 |
| **GUARD THE GUILD, ONLINE: THE WATCH IS THE ARRIVAL'S (GUARD-ONLINE, 2026-09-26, Mac: "Guard the guild quest (mages guild) - wait time for this should be alot shorter"; asked, the window "Starts ~1 min after you arrive")** - `systems/quest/onlineGuard.js` (`ONLINE_GUARD_WINDOWS`, `guardWindowStep`), `systems/quest/actions.js` (`DailyFrom.checkTrigger`), the `sharedClock` quest seam (`scenes/questBridge.js`, `systems/quest/machine.js`, `scenes/world.js`, `scenes/exterior.js`). N0B10Y03 watches the hall `daily from 00:00 to 03:00` and pays once that window is shut again. DailyFrom.cs reads the world clock's hour, and offline that stands: the player loiters to midnight. On the SHARED clock a game day is two real hours and a rest moves no world time (RESTX2), so the window came round once in two hours. Online, for the quests the table names (this one), the window is the player's ARRIVAL's: ten game minutes after they are first in the watched Place (the script's own `_magesguild_`) it opens, and it stands one game hour; leaving after it shuts and coming back opens it again, staying keeps it shut so the questor can pay. The arrival rides the action's save beside C#'s two fields (`guardAnchor`, `guardAway`; an older save restores as "no watch yet"). Every other `daily from` - seventeen quests - and every quest offline keeps DFU's window. | Mac, 2026-09-26 |
## A-note (H1): the houses-for-sale seed

DFU rolls the town's house market with

    Random.InitState(buildingDict.GetHashCode() + Now.Month)

and `GetHashCode()` on a Dictionary is the CLR's OBJECT-IDENTITY hash -
a different number every time the process runs. So the `+ Month` term,
which plainly intends "the same houses are for sale all month", is
defeated by the term it is added to: in DFU the market reshuffles on
every load. A JS port cannot reproduce a CLR identity hash and should
not want to, so `banking.housesForSale` seeds on the LOCATION and the
month - what that expression was reaching for. Pinned both ways in
`test/houses.test.js`: stable inside a month, turned over between them,
and different per town.

## B. Verbatim quirks preserved (real-data reality)

World:
- THE GREEN "MARKER / START" PLACARD OUTSIDE PRIVATEER'S HOLD - **RESOLVED
  AGAINST THE SOURCE, 2026-09-08: a DEFECT, not a preserved quirk** (AUDIT
  64 F12; the fix and the full reading are in bible/03-World/World-Arc.md).
  This row read that `CUSTAA30.RMB`'s one misc flat is archive **199 record
  10** and that DFU draws it too, because `RMBLayout.AddMiscBlockFlats`
  (:340-379) skips only the LIGHTS archive and
  `GameObjectHelper.CreateDaggerfallBillboardGameObject` (:313) "has no
  archive-199 branch either" - the 199 filter sitting in
  `AddExteriorBlockFlats` (:409, the building SUBRECORD loop) alone. It
  closed with its own escape clause: "what has NOT been checked is DFU
  actually rendering it on screen, which needs a Unity build nobody here
  can run." No Unity build was needed. The branch it never reached is
  `DaggerfallBillboard.Start` (:77-84): archive 199 is `FlatTypes.Editor`
  (MaterialReader.cs:980-981) and Start disables such a billboard's mesh
  renderer - "Just disable mesh renderer as actual object can be part of
  action chain" - under `StartGameBehaviour.cs:45`'s `ShowEditorFlats =
  false`. That is WHY AddMiscBlockFlats has no 199 branch: layout stands
  the marker, the COMPONENT hides it. `collectBlockFlats` stamps `editor:
  true` now and both exterior hosts refuse to batch it.

Chargen:
- `characterDocument.isCustom` is assigned in exactly ONE place in the whole
  DFU codebase (`= true` on the Custom row,
  DaggerfallStartNewGameWizard.cs:358) and is never cleared. So opening the
  custom builder, cancelling out of it
  and then picking a standard class still ships a document marked custom: it
  gets the custom starting kit (ItemHelper.cs:1310) and the Spellsword spell
  set (StartGameBehaviour.cs:804-809), so a Mage picked that way starts with
  the Spellsword spells. The five reputation fields ride the same rule
  (:385-389, written only in the builder's accept arm). Reproduced
  bug-for-bug in ui/chargen.js `_acceptStandardClass`.
  (Added at AUDIT 18: that function's comment already said "recorded in the
  Ledger" and no such row existed - the 17m shape exactly.)

Characters:
- ENEMY BOOTS never reduce ArmorValues[Feet]. EnemyEntity.SetEnemyEquipment's
  armour pass is `for (int i = (int)EquipSlots.Head; i < (int)EquipSlots.Feet;
  i++)` (EnemyEntity.cs:414) - a STRICT `<` against Feet, and Head = 12 /
  Feet = 26, so slot 26 is never visited. DFU still rolls and equips the
  boots (ItemHelper.cs:1452-1457) and they still drop as corpse loot; only
  the armour-value pass skips them, leaving ArmorValues[Feet] at 100 before
  the class/monster clamp. To preserve it the port must skip Boots in the
  value loop ONLY - never in the roll, which consumes a Dice100 and a
  material draw from the stream. (Added at AUDIT 18, which found
  combat/enemyEquipment.js:137-145 subtracting boots there while its header
  called the pass verbatim: at plate steel that is Feet 55 against DFU's 60,
  and at daedric -5 against 60, a 65-point swing straight into
  calculateSuccessfulHit. The code fix is routed to the Combat lane of the
  same audit.)
- RandomEncounters.cs:580-599 carries a commented-out second Cemetery table
  ("Cemetery - DF Unity version"); the generated encounterTables.js is
  comment-stripped, so the port ships the 45 LIVE tables and not that dead
  block. (Added at AUDIT 18 - encounterTables.js:5 cited "Ledger B" for it
  and there was no row.)
- SENT7.RMB carries one archive-210 lamp flat with factionID -4080 (Int16): DFU's faction != 0 rule makes the lamp a StaticNPC; reproduced verbatim (C2 corpus pin).
- NameGen.txt ships with two lenient-JSON constructs inside Monster3 (a missing comma, a trailing comma); FullSerializer treats commas as optional, so the committed normalized nameGen.json equals what DFU loads.

Readers:
- BSA: junk record 669 "FOO" in BLOCKS.BSA; structural closure invariant.
- BLOCKS vs the C#, MEASURED at AUDIT 18 by running DaggerfallConnect's own
  BlocksFile under Mono over the whole corpus: 5,411,170 values compared,
  NINE differ, and all nine are inert and accounted for. (a) Record 669 (the
  junk "FOO") - DFU's ReadBlock falls through its else and returns a
  populated-but-empty DFBlock; the port returns null. Every call site guards
  it (world.js, locationLayout.js). (b) FixRdbData's three SYNTHESIZED
  objects in blocks 1025/1034/1036 - C# leaves ActionResource.
  PreviousObjectOffset and NextObjectIndex at the struct default 0 where the
  port's defaultActionResource() uses -1. Dead data: those objects carry
  Flags = 0, so HasAction is false and RDBLayout.AddActionModelHelper - the
  only reader of PreviousObjectOffset - never runs for them. Before AUDIT 18
  fixed the fixed-length ReadCString this count was 262,589.
- Palettes: MAP.PAL filename triggers x4 six-bit expansion.
- TEXTURE: archives 215/217/436 unsupported (as DFU); wild compression values
  0x900/0x101/0x100 fall through to the uncompressed default; getColor32
  vertical flip and double alpha assignment kept.
- IMG: 6 palettized files read their embedded palette; FMAP0I00/01/16
  unsupported; ImgFile reads raw w*h regardless of the compression field.
- CIF: WEAPON09 (bow) has no wield record; FACES.CIF routes as RCI 64x64.
- ARCH3D: UV unpack only first 3 points AND recordId < 905 with the -7168 ->
  1024 exception; 905-entry patch table applied to a working copy; fixed
  buffers throw on overflow exactly like the C# arrays; min/max seeded at 0
  so Size spans the origin.
- BLOCKS: RMB subrecords step by blockDataSizes, not bytes read; RDB unknown
  list sized by the FIRST node's index; object root must start at the header
  offset; flat factionOrMobileId re-reads two bytes as LE u16; "REF_CUBE"
  and 0xff model ids parse to 0 via strict TryParse semantics; FixRdbData
  repairs blocks 994, 945/946, 958, 975, 1025, 1034, 1036; 8 RDBs carry
  0xff padding instead of the DAGR signature.
- MAPS: 17 regions ship empty records and are rejected (45 populated, as
  classic); two 32-byte names truncate exactly (Porcupine Hostel/Bhoriane,
  Feather and Barbarian/Kambria); letter2 = LETTER2[((2*char)&0xff)>>6], the
  8-bit truncation is load-bearing; LocationType decode uses uint
  wraparound; Orsinium (locationId 50015) border block moved to Z=-2.
- SND: record index 5 is zero-length; synthesized 44-byte RIFF header is
  byte-exact.
- WOODS: GetHeightMapValue clamps both axes with `>= dim - 1`;
  GetLargeHeightMapValuesRange strips the 5x5 to the interior 3x3 with
  INVERTED sample Y (src[ix][4 - iy]) and walks source pixels with a
  DESCENDING map Y (mapPixelY - y).

World layout:
- Exterior subrecord FLATS use the unrotated (subX, 0, -subZ) offset - DFU
  does not rotate flats with the building subrecord.
- Climate-swapped nature flats get `billboardPosition.z = natureFlatsOffsetY`
  (raw -2): suspected upstream y/z typo in RMBLayout.cs, kept verbatim.
  SETTLED: a corpus scan (pinned in world.test.js) shows zero exterior
  subrecord flats in archives 500-511 across all 920 RMB blocks - the branch
  is a dead code path on classic data and only fires for mod-injected
  buildings. Verbatim is provably identical to any fix.
- Ground tiles read [x][15 - y]; scenery skips records < 1; tile records
  >= 56 reset to grass (DFU's tilemap index 8 == texture record 2);
  offsets propsOffsetY -4, blockFlatsOffsetY -6,
  natureFlatsOffsetY -2; classic data never sets model scale.
- City LIGHTS (archive 210) place at (-Y + size.y) * GlobalScale where
  size.y is GetScaledBillboardSize's NATIVE height (MeshReader.cs:549-568
  applies no GlobalScale), and the light Y differs from the billboard's -6
  offset by design.
- ModelDoor extraction: door Index resets per submesh (DFU's doorCount
  scope); archive 156 (Scourg exterior) only exempts the base-archive
  reduction and never becomes a door; ruin-enter 331 record 0 is plain
  stone, skipped.
- On-terrain locations take NO RMB ground plane: StreamingWorld creates
  city blocks with addGroundPlane = false, so the stamped terrain tilemap
  is the ground and marker tiles (record >= 56) fall through to generated
  terrain tiles. Isolated-block scenes keep the ground plane (DFU default
  true).
- RDB flat positions are billboard CENTERS: AddFlat performs no
  AlignToBase, unlike the RMB exterior (AlignToBase) and interior
  (+size.y/2) paths which are base-anchored. Renderer-side base batches
  shift down half the scaled height.
- RDB model matrix composes T * Rz * Rx * Ry - a different rotation order
  than RMB's TRS (Ry * Rx * Rz). Red-brick doors (72100) carry the DOR tag
  but are never action doors. Flat actions pass the flat's MAGNITUDE as
  the raw axis. TRP objects with raw axis 13 rotate NegativeX at 400 (DFU
  bumps classic's 392 so the player cannot stick). LID/WHE force fixed
  rotations. RemoveOverlappingDoors seeds the exit-door centre WITHOUT the
  block origin (benign: start blocks sit at grid 0,0).
- Interiors: prop models (ObjectType 3) keep +Y UNNEGATED then anchor to
  the model's lowest vertex Y; IsBadInteriorModel repairs classic data by
  filtering misplaced model 31000 at 60 block/record combos across 27
  blocks (kept verbatim, like FixRdbData); action doors pin to the 9000
  model range via DoorModelIndex % 5 (900x..980x duplicates have differing
  origins); editor flats (199) are spawned as data but hidden from render - in ALL FOUR hosts since AUDIT 64 F12 (the two exterior ones had drawn them).
- Rest (16f audit): the sub-tick interval is waitTimePerHour /
  minutesPerTick - DFU divides by the CONSTANT 10, not the 6 ticks an
  hour takes, so a rested hour passes in 0.45 real seconds (loiter
  0.75). Preserved verbatim (restSession.js).
  AUDIT 18 deleted this row's second sentence, which claimed a 0-hour
  timed/loiter request rests ONE full hour in DFU "because hoursRemaining
  < 1 is only tested after an hour completes". That is backwards:
  DaggerfallRestWindow.Update (:215-227) is an else-if ladder whose
  `(currentRestMode != RestModes.FullRest) && hoursRemaining < 1` arm sits
  AHEAD of `TickRest()`, so DFU ends a 0-hour rest on the very next Update
  with the clock unmoved. The row was recording a port divergence as a
  preserved quirk, which is what kept it from being audited; the code fix
  is routed to the Systems lane of the same audit (restSession.tick must
  test `mode !== 'full' && hoursRemaining < 1` before the sub-tick loop).
- Falling into deep water keeps the fall LIVE (P14): swimming never
  grounds, so wading out can bill the whole drop as fall damage -
  nothing in DFU clears it (AcrobatMotor.Falling untouched by
  LevitateMotor); preserved bug-for-bug.
- CONJURED ITEMS ARE A DFU RESTORATION, NOT A CLASSIC BEHAVIOUR (X11b).
  ItemCollection.RemoveExpiredItems carries its own note: "Reverse-
  engineering suggests this was intended behavior in classic, but
  classic does not correctly set the item flags so magically-created
  items never disappear." So in the original game a Create Item spell
  gives you permanent gear, and in DFU it does not. The port follows
  DFU, as the doctrine says - the whole conjured-item family (the
  lifetime, the sweep, the stacking split, the transfer refusal, the
  bow's shortest-life-first pick) exists because DFU restored an
  intent classic shipped broken. Recorded here so nobody "fixes" the
  port back toward classic's bug.
- SPELL REFLECTION REFLECTS THE BUNDLE BUT LANDS THE REST OF IT ANYWAY
  (X11a). `TryReflection` refuses a bundle whose `ReflectedCount > 0`
  (EntityEffectManager.cs:1210-1212), and `IncrementReflectionCount`
  runs INSIDE the per-effect loop of `AssignBundle` (:1228). So on a
  multi-effect spell only the FIRST effect can bounce: the whole bundle
  is reassigned at the caster - who takes ALL of its effects - and
  effects 2..n continue down the target's own loop and land on the
  target as well. A Damage Health + Paralyze spell reflected by its
  target therefore hits the CASTER with both and still paralyses the
  TARGET. It reads like a bug and may be one, but it is what the source
  does and there is no comment claiming otherwise, so it is ported
  verbatim rather than "fixed" toward a guess. Pinned by name in
  `test/x11.test.js` so nobody tidies it away by accident. (The refusal
  itself is deliberate and DFU says so: its own comment offers to raise
  the limit later for "limited reflect volleys", which is exactly why
  the port does not raise it now.)

| USING A DRUG DOES NOTHING (U25). DaggerfallInventoryWindow's drug arm is `InflictPoison(player, player, (Poisons)item.TemplateIndex + 66, true)` under DFU's own comment "Drug poison IDs are 136 through 139. Template indexes are 78 through 81, so add to that" - which needs 58, not 66. 78 + 66 = 144, outside the Poisons enum, so GetClassicPoisonEffectKey formats "Poison-144", no effect is registered under that key and AssignBundle instantiates nothing. The drug is consumed and nothing happens, silently. Ported verbatim and pinned so it cannot be quietly "fixed"; startPoison refuses an unregistered type, which is the same outcome by the same reasoning rather than a guard bolted on | DaggerfallInventoryWindow.UseItem :1766-1770 | Preserved (Ledger B) |

## C. DFU features not yet ported (routed)

**DT1 ADDED THAT ROW AND ID1 CLOSED IT THE SAME DAY (2026-08-29).**
The interior host mounts its own `droppedLoot` pool now - the drop at
`FindGroundPosition` on the interior collider with NO pixel key
(`TrackLooseObject` is the outdoor arm), the E-ray target and its
activation, the draw, the CacheScene/RestoreCachedScene halves, both
teardowns, and the Detect feed's `piles`. AUDIT 58 CLOSED THE HALF THAT
CLAIM WAS FALSE ABOUT: the producer built `droppedPiles` and the
consumer read it, but `cacheScene` destructured two keys and wrote two,
so the field was always `undefined` on the way back - and
`restorePiles(undefined)` kills every live pile and then iterates
`saved ?? []`, i.e. it CLEARS. The store, the save envelope
(`snapshotSceneCache`) and its restore carry the third array now,
deep-copied because a pile's record nests where DFU's flat
LootContainerData_v1 does not; a pile is not a CorpseMarker, so it also
survives the permanent-scene clear. DFU needs no third field only
because its `GetLootContainerData` (SerializableStateManager.cs:343-354)
already walks player-dropped `DaggerfallLoot` as one more loot
container. `mintRewardPile` took the same
arm, because GivePc.cs:168 mints through the same member. Every
inventory window this host opens goes through one door
(`interiorInventory`) so the next call site cannot miss it. Kept for the
trail - the original row:

~~interior DROPPED PILES. The interior
host mints no ground pile of its own - `mintRewardPile` falls interior
drops through to the world host, whose `dropFeet` reads the EXTERIOR
player and collider - so an item dropped inside a building is not a

scan cannot see it. Found while wiring DT1's loot pools and deliberately
not taken there: the fix is an interior `droppedLoot` pool, and when it
lands it joins the feed's `piles` with no other change. Recorded at the
seam in `worldModes.js` with this row named.~~ | GameObjectHelper
.CreateDroppedLootContainer (:716-775) | CLOSED ID1 2026-08-29


SWEPT 2026-08-19, RE-SWEPT 2026-08-24 (L1). The re-sweep judged all 60
unstruck rows against the tree - ten readers over six rows each, every
STALE verdict then put to a skeptic whose brief was to REFUTE it,
because striking a row that is still real work is the more damaging
error. The skeptic pushed three back to OPEN (the politeness idle, the
PlaySong action, the ResetBonusPool control), and every surviving
verdict was re-checked by hand against its cited lines before the row
was touched. (PlaySong was struck for real on 2026-08-25 - the skeptic
kept a row whose REASON was already false, which is the other half of
this preamble's warning: a row can survive a refutation on its subject
and still be wrong about why.)

TWENTY rows had drifted. Four were struck outright (TalkToStaticNPC,
the enemy loot tail, the title screen, the talk window's four pages);
the rest were narrowed, several with their NUMBERS wrong rather than
their substance: "seventeen unbuilt guild service windows" is two,
"thirty unported effects" is six, "the only trace of banking is
goldPieceWeightInKg" is a 419-line module. Two of the twenty drifted
because of the same session that swept them, which is the failure this
preamble warns about.

The 2026-08-19 sweep's own note, still true: every unstruck row
re-verified against its arc doc and against `src/`. A row here is a CLAIM that something is unported, and a
stale one is worse than a missing one - it sends the next slice to build
what already ships. Four were wrong: breath/drowning + crouch (P12
shipped it, never struck), transition/activation sounds (closed as
verbatim N/A in Audio.md 2026-08-17, never struck here), interior
containers (S2b + E2 shipped two thirds of that row), and the U20b
career flags (S23 and S24 moved five picks from INERT to LIVE). The rest
were confirmed genuinely open, including the four this ledger is most
often read for: lockpicking's ATTEMPT (SHIPPED at R1 2026-08-23 - both
arms, see the struck row below), smaller-dungeon generation (SHIPPED at
AUDIT 28 W4 2026-08-30, struck below), the interior people gates, and
AddSpawnPoints. Re-sweep whenever a slice closes something routed here.

**LR1 RE-SWEEP (2026-08-29): THREE OF THOSE FOUR ARE NO LONGER OPEN, and the
sentence above outlived them.** Lockpicking was already struck in its own line
when it was written; the interior people gates SHIPPED at P1 2026-08-25

The paragraph is kept rather than rewritten because it is a dated record of

### Derived figures (GATED - `test/ledger.test.js`)

Four of the twenty rows the 2026-08-24 re-sweep corrected were wrong in
their NUMBERS rather than their substance - "seventeen guild service
windows" when the answer was two, "thirty unported effects" when it was
six. Prose cannot notice that; a reader plans a very different week off
the wrong figure. Every number below is DERIVABLE from a module, so it
is stated once here and pinned, and the pin fails if either side moves
without the other. Rows above may quote these figures but must not
restate them independently.

| ~~The outdoor RAIN LOOP keeps playing indoors (`src/systems/ambientEffects.js`)~~ **STRUCK 2026-08-26 - it is NOT DFU, see the AUDIT 26 F088 row below.** The WeatherManager half of this row is right and survives in that row: Update (:146-155) opens with "Do nothing if player inside" and RETURNS, so SetAmbientEffects never runs and `Presets` stays frozen at Rain. What it missed is the COMPONENT: WeatherAmbientEffects is a child of the scene's Exterior object, and every interior/dungeon transition calls DisableAllParents -> `ExteriorParent.SetActive(false)` (PlayerEnterExit.cs:1037-1050), which stops its AudioSources and clears the loop handles in OnDisable - so DFU falls silent indoors with its Presets field still saying Rain. The `IsCemeteryNearby && !playerEnterExit.IsPlayerInside` guard at :154 this row read as proof the component keeps running proves only that the guard is reached on EXTERIOR frames. Recorded so nobody re-files the correction either | Struck (AUDIT 26 F088) |
|---|---|---|
| VidFile: a MID-STREAM PALETTE desyncs the stream (U22) | ReadBlock does `streamPosition += 768` for a palette block and then OVERWRITES it with `streamPosition = stream.Position` at its tail, so the skip is lost. Ported as-is. No ARENA2 .VID contains a mid-stream palette, so it never fires on real data |
| VidFile: frameDelay is recomputed for EVERY block from audioBuffer (U22) | The recompute sits after the switch, not inside the audio arm, so a video block inherits the last audio block's delay - and a video block before ANY audio block dereferences null. Ported as a real throw. Every ARENA2 .VID opens with Audio_StartFrame, so it never fires there |
| VidFile: a swallowed exception does NOT advance the stream (U22) | The catch leaves streamPosition where it was, so a bad block type, a short read or a frame-buffer overrun parks the reader on the same block forever rather than erroring. Preserved; the corpus sweep asserts the stream advances each block so a stall fails a pin in under a second instead of hanging |
| VidFile: Audio_StartFrame consumes its data BEFORE rejecting a bad PlaybackRate (U22) | The read happens first, so a block that 'fails' has still replaced audioBuffer. Ported in that order |

| ~~Interior people visibility gates (house ownership, shop hours, building-open rules, GuildHall anytime access, TG/DB House2 membership)~~ **SHIPPED (P1 2026-08-25).** `characters/interiorPeople.js`'s `peopleAreVisible` is AddPeople's tail (:1206-1226) whole. It was routed at C1 for banking, and H1's house registry was the last dependency. It reads the same primitives as the door ladder in `systems/buildingLocks.js` and INVERTS one: a house you own is always unlocked to you and always empty of its previous occupants. The shop arm reads an entry-time LATCH (PlayerActivate.cs:1120) rather than the clock, so a shopkeeper does not blink out around a player still inside at closing time; the non-shop arm stops at House4, which puts Temple/Tavern/Palace under it and leaves House5/House6/Ship ungated. Two host changes went with it: the building identity is now resolved BEFORE `buildInteriorContext` (it used to come after, so the gate would have read null - DFU hands AddPeople a resolved `buildingData`), and the quest hook is DFU's ELSE branch, so a person the gate removed is never handed to the quest machine. | ~~VAMPIRISM / LYCANTHROPY, and the one number it already owes (U24)~~ CORRECTED AND CLOSED (V1 2026-08-24). This row said the temple's cure-disease count is short by one for a player who is turning, and that the arc "lands by filling one parameter". It does not, and the row was wrong in both halves. GetDiseaseCount (EntityEffectManager.cs:1489-1499) counts every bundle of type Disease, and a DFU-NATIVE infection IS one - the DiseaseData is all zeroes at 0xFF days precisely so the disease machinery carries it - so it was ALREADY counted the moment it existed, and V1's pins drive that both ways (an infection counts beside an ordinary disease; EndDisease is the turn's last line, so a cure a minute before the turn works and a minute after has nothing left to cure). PlayerEntity.TimeToBecomeVampireOrWerebeast is read from a CLASSIC .SAV character record (CharacterRecord.cs:242) and is set by nothing else in DFU: its `+1` prices an IMPORTED character who carries the timer without carrying the effect. This port has no classic-save reader, so cureDiseaseOffer's parameter stays false and the count is correct THROUGH THE DISEASE IT ALREADY IS. The parameter is kept, not deleted - a save importer would need it | PlayerEntity.TimeToBecomeVampireOrWerebeast | V1 |

The third, lawless path is GONE: `questMacros.expandMacroValues` is the ONE walk for window text - maximal-munch `%\w+` with EXACT symbol lookup in the caller's value map (its MCP: a null value leaves the token verbatim, the legacy if-guards as a law), falling to the CONTEXT HANDLERS when a questLike context (machine.macroContext()) rides in, else verbatim (these windows never printed DFU's [undefined] shape). talkSession's greeting/answer sets, guildServiceActions' eight service symbols and arrestFlow's court records all ride it now - the replaceAll chains are deleted, and the walk's exact-match RETIRES their latent corruption (replaceAll('%a') fired inside %adj/%arm). By the census that prompted this slice the leak had grown to 46 replaceAlls across 7 files against the table's 127 handlers; what deliberately REMAINS is not leakage: itemInfo/useItem are the ITEM MCP surface (IM1's recorded design over DFU's item template tokens), buildingNames is BuildingNames.cs's OWN expander (not MacroHelper's table), and townTalk's three are the talk pipeline's lazy SIDE-EFFECT slots (%hnt rolls the reveal, %loc marks the map - values that cannot be precomputed into a map without firing the effect). The two REAL tables stand as they were: questMacros' HANDLERS (GetValue's error-shape ladder, the context table) and talkMacros' MCP (ExpandRandomTextRecord's talk source). A new window now passes a value map to the one walk instead of growing a chain - and can hand machine.macroContext() to get the whole context table for free. 6 mutations, 6 killed. **AND NOW THERE IS ONE TABLE (E-group E7, 2026-09-02)** - the row's own ask, "the table is one file in DFU and wants one slice here", finished. questMacros' HANDLERS carries all 217 rows of `macroHandlers` (M-X's ELSEWHERE map of thirty-seven rows recorded at their consuming windows is gone: the item/painting/service families as ItemName-style call-throughs, the talk MCP's %hnt/%hnt2/%pqn/%pqp, the talk GLOBALS %1com/%key/%loc/%fcn/%hnr, %gii/%map and the court four, and the 217th row - the bare `%`, which prints a percent sign and which the port had been DELETING out of talk text). `systems/talkMacros.js` carries NO table at all now: it is the MCP C# supplies - TalkManagerDataSource's thirteen overrides plus the GameManager the global rows read (the host hands the quest machine's macro hooks straight in, one GameManager as DFU has one) - and the shared ladder is exported as `getMacroValue`. Two shipped corrections fell out of the consolidation: %lev and %pct are ONE C# row (`{"%lev", GuildTitle}`), so both fall through GuildTitle's mcp-null arm to the player's name where %lev used to answer a sentinel; and Date (%dat) is a GLOBAL off WorldTime, not the quest's own clock, so it answers in every context. 7 mutations, 7 killed. Original text (U24; NARROWED). `systems/quest/questMacros.js` ports GetValue's error-shape ladder plus ~40 handlers for the quest path (Q4-i) and `systems/talkMacros.js` ports ExpandRandomTextRecord's talk MCP, so two REAL handler tables now exist where the row assumed none. | ~~GOLD IS A BAG STACK WHERE CLASSIC KEEPS A COUNTER (`src/systems/inventory.js:50`)~~ **SHIPPED (E-group, 2026-09-02): gold is `PlayerEntity.GoldPieces` now, and `PlayerEntity.Items` can never hold Currency.** The seam is DFU's own: `DoTransferItem` (`DaggerfallInventoryWindow.cs:1562-1571`) intercepts a `Currency.Gold_pieces` item whose destination is `PlayerEntity.Items`, does `GoldPieces += item.stackCount`, `from.RemoveItem(item)`, plays `SoundClips.GoldPieces` and RETURNS - so the pile is spent into the counter at the door and no later code has to exclude it. `systems/itemTransfer.js`'s `applyTransfer` carries that arm for every screen that transfers (`toPlayer` is DFU's `PlayerEntity.Items == to`), and it ANSWERS NULL, because the `return` also skips `EquipItem` (:1580) and the choose-one close (:1585-1591) - so a reward pile of gold is taken without closing the picker, DFU's quirk and not the port's. Both doors run it: `ui/nativeInventory.js:_pickRemote` and `ui/enhancedInventory.js:take`. Every other producer writes the counter as DFU's does - `startingGear` (`ItemHelper.cs:1354`), biography's GP command (`BiogFile.cs:283-289`, its recorded minus-arm departure intact), the pickpocket (`PlayerActivate.cs:1628`), `classicSave`'s physicalGold (`StartGameBehaviour.cs:603`), the quest machine's `GetItem` gold arm (`GetItem.cs:73-78`) - and the four readers go through `court.js` (`goldAmount`/`addGold`/`deductGold`/`deductGoldPieces`), which is now `PlayerEntity.GetGoldAmount`/`DeductGoldAmount` on the counter rather than on a stack. WEIGHT came back as DFU writes it: `inventory.carriedWeight` IS `PlayerEntity.CarriedWeight` (`:184`), `Items.GetWeight() + goldPieces * goldPieceWeightInKg`, and its four readers are DFU's four - the sheet's encumbrance line, `GetCarriedWeight()` inside `CanCarryAmount`, `DaggerfallBankManager.cs:370`'s withdrawal test and `LevitateMotor.cs:83`'s over-encumbered walk (all three motor hosts feed it). The wagon KEEPS its stack, because DFU's does (`DaggerfallBankManager.cs:343` reads it back), and so does every loot pile and quest gold resource - `ItemBuilder.CreateGoldPieces`, `value = 1` and all. SAVE: `snap.goldPieces` beside the collections (`SerializablePlayer.cs:133`/`:302`); a PRE-E4 save's Currency stack is absorbed into the counter on restore, BELOW the index-keyed `lightSourceIndex` relink (removing a row above it would light the wrong item) and ONLY when the envelope carries no counter - because `GivePc.cs:179/:186` can legitimately put a Currency stack in `PlayerEntity.Items` in DFU too, unspendable there as here. Pinned across `audit18_systems_items` (the door, both directions), `littlelaws` (the carry gate over `CarriedWeight`, the split-take into the counter), `nativeinventory`/`wagon` (drop-gold reads and writes the counter), `save` (the envelope, the migration's order and its condition), `audit17f` (the producers and the invariant), `audit26_movement` (the member and all three motor hosts) | DaggerfallInventoryWindow.DoTransferItem (:1562-1571); PlayerEntity.cs :64/:158/:184/:1313-1354 | Systems arc |

| ~~The PlaySong QUEST ACTION~~ **SHIPPED 2026-08-25.** The row's stated blocker - "the QUEST SYSTEM is not ported... no quest runs" - stopped being true arcs ago: the machine, the parser, the task loop and sixty-one of the eighty-two actions have been live since Q2b/Q4, and PlaySong sat as a `PendingTrigger` GUARD in the registry rather than as an unwritten action. It is now the real template at its verbatim RegisterActionTemplates slot (`systems/quest/actions.js`, between ChangeFoeTeam and SetPlayerCrime), and the row's own prediction held - the music side WAS a one-liner. What the row did not predict is the piece that needed writing: `systems/songFiles.js`, which is SongFiles.cs's 133 member names plus DaggerfallSongPlayer.EnumToFilename (:275-279) in the port's spelling - `song_gday___d` -> `GDAY___D.HMI` instead of C#'s `gday___d.mid`, because DFU loads pre-converted .mid out of Unity Resources and this port asks the user's MIDI.BSA. That is the ONE door between the enum spelling and the archive spelling every other music module already uses, and a pin proves the two are one set (every songManager playlist entry is reachable from some enum member). TWO THINGS ARE VERBATIM AND LOOK WRONG: the unknown-song arm THROWS rather than pending its line - PlaySong.cs:47-52 has no try/catch where PlaySound's create does, so the two neighbouring actions disagree in C# and ParseQuest's catch drops the whole quest for the loud one; and C#'s `SetComplete()` on that path runs against the TEMPLATE, not the action being built. The playback seam is the machine's new `playSong` hook, wired in `world.js` to `music.playSong`. ONE CONSEQUENCE, and it belongs to an existing row rather than being a new departure: DFU's quest song plays ONCE and SongManager's `!songPlayer.IsPlaying` arm then restores the context track, but the port's songs LOOP (section A, "THE SONG SCHEDULER"), so a quest song holds until the music context changes | PlaySong.cs + SongFiles.cs + DaggerfallSongPlayer.EnumToFilename | CLOSED |

| FaceUVTool's 1,803-UV residual at matched precision **NARROWED (E-group, 2026-09-02) - the surface is fenced and the harness is reproducible; the number itself still needs ARENA2.** Three things are now RULED OUT by reading DFU rather than by measuring it. (a) `API/Vector3.cs` is DOUBLE throughout - x/y/z (:35-45), Magnitude (:151-160), DotProduct (:508-520) and Normalize's `double inverse = 1 / Magnitude` (:583-597) - so P0..P2, V0, V1, the orthogonalisation, both magnitudes, both normalizes and all six basis dot products were ALREADY matched on both sides and cannot carry this. (b) Points 0, 1 and 2 of every face are pure Int32 delta sums (FaceUVTool.cs:172-186) with no floating point on the path, so the whole residual lives at point index >= 3 - only on faces with MORE THAN THREE corners. (c) `Math.trunc` and `(Int32)` agree on every in-range value, negatives included. What is RULED IN is the one genuinely single-precision half of the routine: `df3duvparams_lt` (float[] X/Y/Z/U/V, :55-63), `df3duvmatrix_t` (float UA..VD, :66-76) and `l_ComputeDFUVMatrixXY`'s `float determinant` + `float[] Xi/Yi/Zi` (:222-229), ending in `(Int32)((pn.x * UA) + (pn.y * UB) + UD)` - a float expression truncated. `test/faceuvresidue.test.js` measures that seam without ARENA2: over 400,000 random Daggerfall-scale four-point faces, 76 (0.019%) land on opposite sides of the final truncation under the two precisions, and one of them is pinned (point 3's v is 11009 in the port's doubles, 11010 under DFU's declared floats). The flip needs BOTH halves at once - rounding only the matrix coefficients, or only the final accumulate, leaves that face at 11009 - which is why the pin's mutation is the whole chain and a half-mutation proves nothing. AND THE MEASUREMENT WAS NOT REPRODUCIBLE: the float->double widening this row credits was never one of the five committed harness patches, so nobody with ARENA2 and this repo could re-run it. `tools/parity/patches/FaceUVTool.cs.patch` is that edit, wired into `tools/parity/prepare.sh` and marked as the one patch that is NOT behaviour-neutral. Re-running is `bash tools/parity/prepare.sh` then `ARENA2_PATH=... bash tools/parity/run.sh`; the faceuv line's second column is the number. **FU1 (2026-09-11) READ BOTH SIDES TERM FOR TERM AND FOUND THE HARNESS DISAGREEING WITH ITSELF, NOT THE PORT WITH DFU:** every expression of `ComputeFaceUVCoordinates` and `l_ComputeDFUVMatrixXY` (FaceUVTool.cs:89-266, at the patch's matched precision) and of `Vector3` (`operator -`, `operator *`, `DotProduct` :508-516, `Magnitude` = `Math.Sqrt(SumComponentSqrs())` :151-157, `Normalize` :583-597's `1 / Magnitude` multiply) is the same operation in the same order as `formats/faceUVTool.js`, and the corpus reaches both sides from one file - but on a SINGULAR face (`determinant == 0`, `return false`) the C# dump prints every point of its fresh, zeroed struct array while `tools/parity/js/faceuv.mjs` printed nothing, so each such face counted 2n differing lines that no port arithmetic produced. The JS side prints what the C# side prints now (the point, or zero). What remains of the 1,803 after that is a number only a re-run with ARENA2 and mono can give; nothing read differs. | Readers arc |

| **THE DAGGERFALL -> MORROWIND WEAPON MAPPING** (`src/formats/mwFirstPerson.js`, `DF_TO_MW_WEAPON`, MW-D9 2026-08-29). Daggerfall's weapon taxonomy is not Morrowind's, and the rules doc had already ruled on this shape before any code existed: any mapping between them "is a PORT DECISION, not a ported rule, and belongs in the recorded divergences with its reasoning visible - not inferred inside a lookup table, where the last attempt hid it wrong twice." So the table is EXPORTED, pinned row by row, and keyed by the port's own WEAPONS **template index** rather than by the sprite layer's `WEAPON_TYPES` - which folds a Claymore and a Longsword into one class and therefore cannot tell a one-hander from a two-hander. Eighteen rows, one per wieldable template (Arrow is ammunition and is the only omission). MOST ROWS ARE NEAR-TRANSLATIONS: the short blades, the long blades, the axes (Daggerfall's War Axe / Battle Axe split maps cleanly onto AxeOneHand / AxeTwoHand), and both bows onto MarksmanBow. **FOUR ROWS ARE JUDGEMENT AND ARE MAC'S TO OVERRULE:** (1) **FLAIL** - Morrowind has no flail at all; BluntOneHand is the nearest thing that exists and nothing here is right. (2) **STAFF** -> BluntTwoWide, matching where Morrowind's own staves sit. (3) **CLAYMORE and DAI-KATANA** -> LongBladeTwoHand, which is a BEHAVIOURAL change rather than a mesh swap: it costs them Daggerfall's own one-handed animation. (4) **MATERIAL** - `DF_TO_MW_MATERIAL` folds Daggerfall's ten materials onto the seven Morrowind ships, so Mithril and Elven both read as glass, and Adamantium / Orcish / Ebony all read as ebony. A type the player's archives do not carry draws NOTHING and says so on the card, because a longsword standing in for a bow would hang on the wrong bone, in the wrong hand, and look entirely deliberate | weapontype.cpp's eleven short groups vs `WEAPONS` (18 templates) | DECLARED MW-D9 2026-08-29, awaiting Mac's read - the four judgement rows are the ones to argue with |
| **THE UNCHARGED DAGGERFALL SWING** (`src/combat/fpArm.js`, MW-D12 2026-08-30). Morrowind's blow is CHARGED: the wind-up section plays while the button is held, and `calculateWindUp` reads how far into the `min attack` -> `max attack` window the clip got when it was released. That fraction picks how much of the release is skipped and which of the small/medium/large follow keys the blow ends on. **Daggerfall has no charge at all** - the swing fires the instant the drag crosses the gesture threshold and its damage does not vary with how long you held the button. So the port releases the wind-up the moment its section ENDS, which puts the playhead exactly on `max attack` and makes calculateWindUp answer 1: a Daggerfall swing is a Morrowind swing released at full charge. That is the reference's own arithmetic on the port's own clock, not a constant somebody chose, and it is why every blow takes the LARGE follow keys. THE ONE PLACE A HOLD WOULD BE RIGHT is the bow, and Morrowind's draw-and-hold is wired to the port's own machine (`isBow && state === 'StrikeUp'`) rather than to "is it a bow": the in-game bow path is DFU's BowDrawback-OFF instant shot (`playerWeapon.gesture`), so today the arm never holds, and coupling the hold to the weapon TYPE would have parked it at full draw waiting for a release the game path never sends. It goes live the moment DFU's drawback arm does, with nothing in the arm to change | `CharacterController::calculateWindUp` + `prepareHit` (character.cpp:1235-1259) vs `PlayerWeapon.gesture` | DECLARED MW-D12 2026-08-30 - the note to argue with is whether a Daggerfall swing should read as a FULL-strength Morrowind blow or a random one (the reference's no-window fallback is `min(1, 0.1 + rnd)`) |

| **THE SETTINGS SCREEN'S PLAYER-FACING TAXONOMY** (`src/ui/settingsMap.js`, MENU-slice). The store's 13 ini sections are where DFU WRITES a value; they are not how a person looks for one - two of the three settings a casual player most wants (Sound Volume, Music Volume) live in `[Controls]`, and Field Of View sits in `[Video]` beside twenty-four keys about shadow-map resolution. So the screen carries its own 7-category map over the same 171 keys. It stays close to DFU's own UI grouping (`DaggerfallAdvancedSettingsWindow.cs:234-238`: gamePlay / interface / enhancements / video / accessibility) with TWO DELIBERATE DEPARTURES: **AUDIO is its own category** where DFU buries it as a section on the gamePlay page (`:264`), because volume is the most-reached-for setting in any game; and **MODS is its own category** rather than a section under enhancements (`:327`), so the mod manager has a home to grow into rather than a corner to be added to. The map is TOTAL and DISJOINT over all 171 keys and pinned both ways, so a DFU re-bake that adds or renames a key fails the build instead of dropping a row off the screen | DaggerfallAdvancedSettingsWindow's page/section split | Approved 2026-08-21 (Mac: "game settings need a home, audio needs a home, graphics needs a home, the future mod manager needs a home") |
