The keyed art-less path survives as the fallback. **c2/S6 2026-09-01 then landed the shader's SECOND HALF**: the six presentations (below-slice colour/grayscale unchanged, above-slice TRANSPARENT at alpha 0.75, above-slice WIREFRAME at (0.9,0.9,0.7,0.6) / (0.25,0.25,0.25,0.6)) with Cutout as the ABSENCE of the above-slice group, the inverted clip that makes the two passes a partition, the `min(y, slice)` that leaves the above-slice dim IDENTICALLY ZERO (DFU never dims it - a port that does is wrong), the water tint both passes carry fed per block from S1's `automapWaterLevel` - whose COLOUR is `UnderwaterFog.waterMapColor` (0.1,0.3,0.25,0.4), the value Automap.cs:2589-2590 injects into the ONE automap material before AutomapModel.cs:83 Instantiates it onto every submesh, NOT the `.shader` Properties default (0,0.3,0.5,0.4) DFU overwrites before a fragment is ever shaded; c2 flight 2's review found S6 had pinned the dead default, and the constant now comes from `render/underwaterFog.js` by import so the two cannot drift again, the blend flip owned by `setAutomapMode` (the mode IS the queue) with ZWrite still ON and no sorting, the enum's own Cutout->Wireframe->Transparent cycle with its four hotkeys where DFU polls them, and the lazily built per-mesh line index buffer - two recorded substitutions, both rowed above. **c2/S7 2026-09-01 then landed the BEACONS AND THE PICKER**: SetupBeacons' five objects as procedural meshReader-shaped models whose sub-meshes name 1x1 solids in the `amap` archive, so `drawMesh` carries every one with NO new renderer entry point - the red position cylinder (0.3,50,0.3), the blue pivot axis (0.15,50.2,0.15) yawed to the MAP camera each update with its two curved arrows as CHILDREN (so the axis's own scale multiplies their +/-(2,-0.02,-2) offsets down to 0.3 units and their (0.15,0.0005,0.15) down to (0.0225,0.0251,0.0225)), and the green entrance ray + 0.8 cube hidden until the LOS tick discovers them; the beacons draw OPAQUE because Unity's Standard material ignores the 0.5/0.75 alphas the C# writes, and are never sliced, dimmed or grayed - but they ARE lit, which c2 flight 2's review found missing: CreateLightsForAutomapGeometry (Automap.cs:2025-2076) builds three DIRECTIONAL lights whose `cullingMask = 1 << layerAutomap` reaches the Standard-material beacons and nothing else (the geometry shader has no light term at all), at -forward of Euler(50, 270/126/0, 0) and intensities 0.9/0.7/0.5 in a dungeon or castle, 1.0/0.6/0.2 in a building, over ambient ZERO; the mesh program grew a THIRD directional term for the back light rather than collapse two of the three into one, so this is fidelity and not a substitution. Plus `systems/automapPick.js`: the panel-relative unprojection through the pass's OWN mirrored projection, an AABB broadphase over the c2/S1 model - which now carries each entry's CPU triangles and placement matrix by REFERENCE - a triangle-precise narrowphase in the row's own space, DFU's `MeshRenderer.enabled` discovery filter, nearest-wins with ties to the marker, and GetMouseHoverOverText's seven Internal_Strings rows on the status label, cached on the mouse position so a still pointer re-traverses nothing. Two recorded departures, both rowed above: the curved arrow is procedural (DFU's is an authored Unity .obj, not game data) and the marker proxies are boxes where DFU raycasts capsule/box/mesh colliders. **c2/S8 2026-09-01 then landed the USER-DATA HALF AND EVERY CLICK VERB**: user note markers (the 24-vertex orange diamond CreateDiamondShapePrimitive lists by hand, at `hit.point + normal*0.7` with the strict 1.0-unit refusal that burns no id, ids from SortedListExtensions.AddNext's lowest-free-key REUSE, 50-character notes edited in a DaggerfallInputMessageBox that owns the keyboard while it is up, and the note itself as its own hover text); teleporter connections (a new `onTeleportPortal` producer on DaggerfallAction's Teleport relay, firing BEFORE the warp as :596 does and installed on the shared dungeon context so both hosts get it; the two violet portal discs at localScale (2,0.1,1) / localRotation Euler(0,90,90) under a parent that takes the ENTRANCE's rotation yawed +90 AT BOTH ENDS; the hover connection cylinder created once and kept when sliding portal-to-portal, as the C# does); and the five gestures - the left double-click's portal jump (a 1.0 s easeInOutSine camera translate by the connection's own delta, from either end, during which Update returns before base.Update() and NOTHING else acts), the note add/edit behind it with Ctrl skipping the prompt, the whole left arm gated off inside a building, the right double-click's delete-then-rotation-pivot fallthrough (ungated), the middle click's centring at the camera's distance to the PLAYER, and map_revealall / map_hideall / map_teleportmode with the Ctrl+Shift debug-teleport click on the standalone host's probe surface. HideAll turning MeshRenderer.enabled off WITHOUT touching the grayscale keyword is what forced `revealed` to become the draw gate and the tier a choice inside it. One recorded departure, rowed above (the key's yaw-built quaternion); one live defect fixed on the way (`ui/automapChrome.js` threw on any MIDDLE-button release - `held` has no middle set - which is the slice drag's own release). **c2/S9 2026-09-01 then landed the INTERIOR-BUILDING ARM**, with DFU's own quirks reproduced and recorded at the site so a later reviewer cannot fix them out of parity: `interiorContext` mints per-model keys (`int:<placement index>`, outside the dungeon's `${bi}:${position}` space by construction) and world AABBs at the ONE push site every building entry runs, builds the reveal model over them and runs the same 5 Hz probe set indoors (CheckForNewlyDiscoveredMeshes covers IsPlayerInsideBuilding at :1155); M opens the window inside a building in BOTH interior hosts, with all three pointer phases routed to the interior overlay slot so the map's press-hold buttons and drags work there. The interior differences are DFU's: geometry is ALWAYS presented in COLOUR (AutomapModel.cs:46-72 passes `visitedInThisEntering = playerIsInsideBuilding`, so RENDER_IN_GRAYSCALE is disabled on every interior material and never enabled again - a LAW in `_partitionDraws`, not an accident of the two sets agreeing); SetupBeacons places the entrance beacon at the entered door and sets it DISCOVERED (:1450-1457) and RestoreStateAutomapDungeon(true)'s opening HideAll() takes it straight back out (:2355, :2460), so a building's map opens FULLY HIDDEN with an undiscovered entrance that re-lights on the next LOS tick - pinned as a SEQUENCE, including the tail at :2379 where a dungeon record sharing the town's "RegionName/Name" lights the shop's beacon anyway; interior discovery is PER-VISIT (the locationName mismatch at :2381 ends the restore); there is NO micro-map indoors (:1722-1748's null arm); the reset default is CUTOUT; and S8's note/teleporter click arm stays gated off. `RestoreStateAutomapInterior` is DEAD CODE in the reference - written, and called from nowhere - and is deliberately not ported. One recorded departure, rowed above (the save-while-inside-a-building arm that would put a shop's map in the dungeon dictionary). the residence-quest plate arm alone stayed FLAGGED - and then ROAD-C c2/S10 SHIPPED THE EXTERIOR WINDOW WHOLE (2026-09-01), in two commits. HALF A - the composition: the native AMAP00I0 + TOWN00I0 chrome through the shared `ui/automapChrome.js` tables, the HUDCompass strip on the MAP camera's yaw, real ROTATION (the orthographic straight-down camera of ExteriorAutomap.cs:539-543 as `screen = R(yaw)*(p - centre)*k`), DFU's four-layer PAINT ORDER made explicit - circle y=-20, stamp y=-10, layout y=+0.01, arrow y=+0.1 - so the circle and the dark stamp show THROUGH the layout's transparent street pixels, the real mesh-99900 arrow and stamp rasterised once by the new pure `ui/meshStamp.js`, the player marker's own map-pixel modulo with the -64/+3 custom-location offsets, the four border jumps, drag pan at dragSpeed 0.00345 x orthoSize and drag rotate at 5.0, max/min zoom, and the four backgrounds. THE ARC'S WHOLE RENDERER SURFACE, stated in full because an earlier draft of this clause claimed "one new GL call": c2/S6 added `setAutomapWater`, `drawMeshWire` with its private `_ensureWireMesh` and the exported pure `buildWireIndices` - the arc's ONLY new GL primitive, `gl.LINES` over an edge index buffer - and c2/S10 HALF A added `drawScreenQuad`'s optional `rotate` plus the renderer-owned `screenScissor` bracket, which introduces no new GL call (setScreenScissor/clearScreenScissor predate the arc). S5, S7, S8, S9 and HALF B touched renderer.js not at all. HALF B - THE PLATE ANCHOR MOVED and EVERY PLATE IN EVERY TOWN MOVED WITH IT: the anchor is now the building subrecord's own Position (ExteriorAutomap.cs:664-665) over ALL buildings of ALL blocks via the new `world/buildingSummaries.js`, retiring the exterior-DOOR substitution; plus `customUserDisplayName` on the discovery record with the double-click rename (drawn in the label's place while the tooltip keeps the canonical name) and revealUndiscoveredBuildings. THE CORNERS STAY AXIS-ALIGNED: RotateBuildingNameplates' corner rotation is DEAD CODE in DFU (UpdateAutomapView recomputes all four corners axis-aligned before ComputeNameplateOffsets reads them) and porting it would itself be the departure. **ROAD-C c2 flight-2 REVIEW then corrected ten things in that window and rowed them here** (2026-09-02): the ROTATION SIGN (`ui/automapCamera.js`'s two exterior rotate laws moved the yaw the WRONG WAY - `AngleAxis(A, -up) == AngleAxis(-A, +up)`, so ActionRotate's `RotateAround(pos, -up, -amount*dt)` RAISES the camera's euler-y, which is exactly the basis `toPanelScreen`/`exteriorDragPan` are written in; rotate-around-player also turned its centre one way and its basis the other, so the marker it pivots on swung across the panel instead of staying pinned); THE HOTKEYS ARE NOW DFU'S OWN - the window reads all thirty `ExtAutomap*` rows of `systems/dialogShortcuts.js` (DialogShortcuts.txt:267-296) through the same ShortcutOrFallback door `ui/automapWindow.js` reads the `Automap*` half with, in DFU's two poll classes, replacing an invented WASD/QE/digit table that matched classic at no key at all; with it come the three DIRECT view-mode verbs (F2/F3/F4, :1235-1262) that had no arm anywhere, the two rotate-around-player rows that were mouse-only, keyboard zoom-OUT (A2's `case 'minus'` could never fire - ui/input.js returns 'char:-' for the hyphen), and the two-phase `isCloseWindowDeferred` toggle-close; the two panel DRAGS now scale their native delta back to REAL SCREEN pixels, which is the space `InputManager.MousePosition` and DFU's two speed constants live in (the dungeon window already read the identical idiom that way), and the right drag carries `ActionRotate`'s own `Time.unscaledDeltaTime` instead of folding it in as 1; the hover seam's (-1,-1) SENTINEL no longer reaches the chrome's drag machine (a fabricated coordinate, not a position - it teleported the map ~165 world units on any pointer excursion off the letterboxed panel); the chrome's `sound` output reaches `audio.playOneShot(SOUND.ButtonClick)`, which is what makes `ActionClickSoundOnly` mean anything; and the camera ROTATION is remembered across close/reopen beside the zoom, as `ExteriorAutomap.cs:88`'s `cameraTransformRotationSaved` is. The same review's second lane corrected the PLATES AND CHROME: the double-click rename prompt now opens on the name the player is LOOKING AT, because `mb.TextBox.Text = renamingLabelRef.Text` (ExteriorAutomap.cs:888) is the label's drawn text - the custom name whenever one is set - while the canonical stays TextBox.Name/DefaultText (:887, :889), the fallback for an emptied box; before this, re-editing a renamed building opened on the canonical and a player who confirmed unchanged silently reverted their own rename; that rename push no longer LATCHES the panel drag - the double click is a press on the render panel, so it arms the pan flag, and its release is routed to townTalk's slot, which by then holds the input box, so the window gained `onReturn` (windowStack's RemoveWindow tells the uncovered window) to release the chrome the way DFU's polled `PanelAutomap_OnMouseUp` does on the first frame after the box pops (BaseScreenComponent.cs:643-648); the compass is now hud.js's ONE `drawCompassStrip` - the dummy panel gives the POSITION alone (:456) and HUDCompass sizes the frame from the ART (HUDCompass.cs:132-135), so it is COMPBOX.IMG's 69x17 with a 65px strip window, not the dummy panel's 76x17 stretched, and the second copy of the scroll/inset arithmetic is gone; and the plate tooltip is toolTip.js's ONE `drawToolTipBox` at the CURSOR (ToolTip.cs:158-179) in the two GUI colour settings DFU assigns it by hand (:874-875), replacing a bespoke black box at the plate's own x - with `ignoreEnableSetting`, the one exception DFU itself carries, because `nameplateToolTip` is a bare `new ToolTip()` (:870-871) drawn unconditionally (:571-572) rather than the window's EnableToolTips-gated `defaultToolTip`. DEPARTURES THAT STAND: the exterior window's HELD keyboard arms are edge-driven - one frame of DFU's per-second speed per press, because townTalk's overlay seam delivers keydown and no keyup, with the browser's key repeat supplying the hold; it covers the IsPressedWith class alone, since DFU's focus/reset/view-mode/background arms are IsDownWith (:599-641) and so are parity here (the MOUSE arms, chrome press-hold and both drags, are frame-exact); the plate label is the port's FNT text at half TextScale rather than DFU's yellow-reloaded DaggerfallFont Texture2D. **ROAD-D D5 (2026-09-02) THEN CLOSED TWO OF THE THREE RESIDUE ITEMS, both because the blocker each was written against had gone stale.** THE RESIDENCE-WITH-ACTIVE-QUEST PLATE ARM: the port DOES expose locationWasMarkedOnMapByNPC - `systems/topicTree.js:394-419` is IsBuildingQuestResource verbatim, flag and override name both, pinned since TK-ii - it simply had no production consumer, so `questName` had no producer anywhere in src/ and the arm could never fire; a quest-marked residence was silently plateless where ExteriorAutomap.cs:705-707 names it. `residenceQuestName` + `stampResidenceQuestNames` (`ui/exteriorAutomapWindow.js`) are now that C#'s own DOUBLE LOOP over it, with its three quirks kept and named at the site - there is NO break, so the LAST marked match wins; the `&&` SHORT-CIRCUITS, so IsBuildingQuestResource is never asked about a place whose buildingKey does not match; and the name that lands is the OUTER place's `SiteDetails.buildingName`, NOT the inner call's `overrideBuildingName`, which parts from it exactly when the key-only outer guard matches a place in another town (buildingKeys are location-relative and really do collide) while the inner mapID+key test matches one here. The stamp happens where DFU's does - once per nameplate build, at window open (:273), never per frame - in the M-outside door of `scenes/world.js`, the host that has a quest machine; `scenes/exterior.js` mounts none, so its every residence takes DFU's own empty-set answer and says so at the site. THE TEN BUTTON TOOLTIPS: Internal_Strings.csv:908-917 now sits beside the dungeon block in `ui/automapText.js` byte for byte - including the two misprinted parentheses (`exteriorAutomapToolTipUpstairsButton` closes one it never opens, `...DownstairsButton` opens one it never closes) and the double space on the rotate-left row - formatted by THE SAME two laws the dungeon half uses (ShortcutOrFallback onto the same KeyCode.Home, String.Format over HotkeySequence.ToString) with the `ExtAutomap*` names, in DFU's own argument order rather than the sentences' (the stair rows take ZoomIn/ZoomOut, not the Upstairs/Downstairs rows their BUTTONS are named after); they are driven by the chrome's rest clock, which `tick()` was already computing and this window was throwing away, and unlike the plate tooltip they DO honour EnableToolTips because they ride the shared `defaultToolTip`. ~~FLAGGED residue, now ONE item, at `src/ui/exteriorAutomapWindow.js`'s header and nowhere else: map_revealbuildings / map_hidebuildings (the flag they set, revealUndiscoveredBuildings, is live and pinned; the port has no console for them, exactly as ui/travelMapWindow.js records for map_reveallocations)~~ **SHIPPED (E-group, 2026-09-02): the port has a console command database now** - `src/systems/consoleCommands.js` is Wenzil.Console's ConsoleCommandsDatabase/ConsoleCommand/HelpCommand/ConsoleInputHistory and ConsoleController's submit law, and all EIGHT registered map commands hang off it: this window's pair (registered from both exterior hosts, its flag moved to module scope where DFU's persistent component keeps it), the dungeon automap's trio (registered from `systems/automap.js` where the laws already were, with `Automap.instance` now latched at bindAutomapLayout and dropped by both exits - the standalone host's `window.__automapCommand` probe runs the database instead of repeating its three answers), and the travel map's trio including `map_reveallocation`, whose PlayerGPS.DiscoverLocation throw is reported and whose short-argument arm answers with HELP's own details block. NO Ledger-A departure here, unlike U38's crosshair, because all four files are ARENA2's own. Eleven panels, eight of them pure hit rectangles over painted art; they POST ACTIONS through a routeAction pulled out of routeKey, so a click and a keypress reach ONE door; docked and undocked layouts both; the head follows the race through the paperdoll archives. It is an ALTERNATIVE HUD - the vitals, compass and mode icon go off while it is up and the crosshair stays. It closed TWO DEAD SEAMS on the way: GUI/LargeHUD, written by the pause window since I3 and read by nothing, and Actions.ActivateCursor, bound to Enter since I1 with no consumer at all - which is PlayerMouseLook.cursorActive, the flag IsLargeHUDInteractable IS, so without it the panels would have been unreachable). ~~HUDActiveSpells~~ SHIPPED TOO (U46 2026-08-25: ui/hudActiveSpells.js - the eight layout schemes, the shared 24-slot pool, the caster split, the `< 2` expiry and its 4Hz blink that an ITEM never joins, the large-HUD lift that only goes upward, and the tooltip. It reads the bundles X10 already built, through the SAME liveBundles the Dispel picker uses, so ShowIcon has one home. Two more fields joined the bundle stamp - the spell's icon and whether the player cast it - because neither is derivable once the entries are on the list. ONE RECORDED DEPARTURE: DFU loads face textures synchronously inside RefreshFaces and THROWS on a missing one; the port's art is async and data-gated like the rest of the HUD - a face draws from the frame its art lands, a missing record costs that face (warned once), never the session. QUIRK KEPT: DaggerfallHUD.cs:207 wires ShowEscortingFaces to EnableBorder, a no-op, so the faces are unconditional). ~~the two large-HUD settings still read by nothing - LargeHUDOffsetHorse and LargeHUDUndockedOffsetWeapon~~ **BOTH LIVE at ROAD-D D10**: `ui/hudLarge.js` is the one home for `horseOffsetHeight` (TransportManager.cs:304-309 - the drawn bar's height when LargeHUD and LargeHUDOffsetHorse are on, and it never asks about docking) and `weaponOffsetHeight` (FPSWeapon.cs:146-155 - the same height, but a DOCKED bar FORCES it whatever the setting says, DFU's own anti-misconfiguration arm); `scenes/world.js` feeds the first into `ridingRect` and `combat/fpsWeapon.js` takes the second as its default `offsetHeight`, so the viewmodel is no longer drawn underneath a docked bar. Both keys are tiered live. ~~AND THE DOCKED BAR STILL OCCLUDED THE WORLD INSTEAD OF SHRINKING IT (AUDIT 39 F135, flagged at `ui/hudLarge.js:75`)~~ **SHIPPED (ROAD-E E5, 2026-09-02)**: `Utility/ViewportChanger.cs:52-67` and `HUDCrosshair.cs:43-52` are both live and they had to land together. `ui/hudLarge.js` is the one home for all three terms - `dockedLargeHudHeight` (`LargeHUD.ScreenHeight` gated on LargeHUD AND LargeHUDDocked, which is NOT the horse arm's gate), `largeHudViewportRect` (DFU's `new Rect(0, hudHeight, 1, 1 - hudHeight)` digit for digit; Unity's camera rect is normalized bottom-left, which is `gl.viewport`'s own space, so it crosses over without a flip) and `largeHudWorldAspect` (Unity derives a camera's aspect from its viewport, so a rect shrunk without its denominator would STRETCH the world into the strip rather than crop the lens). The rect is RENDERER-OWNED FRAME STATE - `Renderer.setWorldViewport` is consumed by the next `beginFrame` exactly as ViewportChanger recomputes it every frame, so no menu, video or map scene can inherit a world frame's strip; the 2D passes take the whole canvas back at the first `drawScreenQuad`, the port's one screen-space primitive, so no host has to remember it; and both the character-sprite RT (which runs mid-world-pass) and the panel bracket now BORROW AND RETURN the rect, the EV6 shape the clear colour has had since F034. All four hosts that draw the bar carry it - `scenes/world.js`, `scenes/exterior.js`, `scenes/worldModes.js` (both modal arms) and `scenes/dungeon.js` - with the sky and EV8's far province ring sharing the lens; `scenes/interior.js` draws no HUD at all, so there is no surface there. The crosshair re-centres through `ui/hudCrosshair.js`'s `crosshairCentreY`: DFU's `(Screen.height - ScreenHeight - crosshairSize.y) / 2` is the reticle's TOP, so its CENTRE is `(Screen.height - ScreenHeight) / 2` with the component's own size cancelled out, which is the form this port draws about. RECORDED, not ported: `SetRetroAspectViewport` (:98-146), the pillarboxed 4:3 / 16:10 rect that reads the same hudHeight term - it is RetroModeAspectCorrection, a render-to-texture presenter this port has no equivalent of, and the docked-bar term inside it is the one this file now carries. D10's withdrawal held under the ship: the activation ray is the camera's forward vector, and not one activation site changed. 20 mutations, 20 killed at `test/hudlarge.test.js`. WHAT IS LEFT OF THIS ROW: the TextBox single-line edit model. NARROWED TWICE BEFORE THAT: U37 shipped the ToolTip whole AND the MouseEnter/Move model it needed (ui/toolTip.js + overlayHover in both channels and all four hosts), so the inventory's hover info panel now waits only on its own hover(vx,vy); U38 shipped HUDCrosshair and HUDInteractionModeIcon, both riding drawHud - the ONE host-agnostic call - with DFU's placement, the cursor-active suppression and the xhair-replaces-the-crosshair branch verbatim, and their two settings live. The 67 bug-severity ones are FIXED and pinned (`git log`: "AUDIT 26 fixes wave 1/2/3", closed green 2026-08-26); the 151 that remain are the rows below - 89 PARITY, where the port and DFU differ somewhere a player can reach, and 62 marked **NIT**, which are technically divergent with negligible impact and are NOT a work queue: a NIT row exists so nobody re-finds it and so a slice touching that seam knows the exact shape of the difference. Every row carries its finding ids so the register can be cross-referenced, and the per-finding evidence - both sides, with the C# and the port site read line by line - lives in `.audit26/remainder.json`. Findings are GROUPED where they are one law seen from two sides; the ids in a row's tag are that row's whole membership. | ~~**INTERIOR WINDOWS KEEP THE EXTERIOR GLOW** (F001, F002)~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 render batch 2026-08-27: WINDOW_STYLES gains the `disabled` entry - Color.black OUTRIGHT (MaterialReader.cs:933-935), not a colour times an intensity - and EVERY non-exterior host now writes its own style, which is what the tint being one renderer global demands. All three interior mesh paths are laid out Disabled in DFU (DaggerfallInterior.cs :473 individual models, :517 the combined batch, :1270 action doors) and nothing re-lights them mid-visit, since PlayerEnterExit.cs:713 creates the interior as a ROOT GameObject that DaggerfallLocation's Day/Night re-apply never reaches. The missing entry was worse than absent: `windowEmissionRGB('disabled')` fell through the `|| WINDOW_STYLES.day` guard and answered DAY. WIDER THAN THE ROW, and deliberately: DUNGEONS had the same defect and fixing only interiors would have REGRESSED them, since a dungeon inherited whatever the last host wrote and would have started inheriting `disabled`. SetDungeonTextures (DaggerfallMesh.cs :153-169) calls GetMaterial with no window style, and a window material is BORN DayWindowColor * DayWindowIntensity (MaterialReader.cs:456-461), so both dungeon hosts write `day` explicitly - which also fixes a dungeon entered at night having glowed amber. 7-mutation checked). Original finding:  DaggerfallInterior lays out every interior model with `SetClimate(..., WindowStyle.Disabled)` (:473, :517, :1270), and Disabled means EmissionColor BLACK (MaterialReader.cs:934-936). The port's window emission is a single renderer global (`renderer.js:1294-1295`, bound at :1578) that is only ever day/night/fog: `main.js:98` boots it to `day` before routing, only exterior frames write it (`world.js:15687`, `exterior.js:5083`), the interior block at `worldModes.js:3274-3291` never touches it, and `WINDOW_STYLES` (`windowEmission.js:8-13`) has no disabled entry to write. So inside every shop, tavern and house the glass glows pale blue by day and amber at night where DFU and classic show unlit panes - and the port contradicts its own bible (the Rendering-Arc R2 note) and its own comment one import away (`interior.js:34-36`, the `?interior` dev scene - the NIT half, F002, since only the dev route draws it) | DaggerfallInterior.SetClimate + MaterialReader.SetWindowStyle | Rendering arc |

| ~~**THE JUMP GATE HAS NO WasClimbing BYPASS, AND THE FLAG IS WRITTEN FOR NOBODY** (F026)~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 movement batch 2026-08-27: the gate is `!this.slowFalling && (this.climb?.wasClimbing || groundedTime >= 0.1)` - AcrobatMotor.cs:76 verbatim, and HandleJumpInput sits inside `if (grounded)` (PlayerMotor.cs:337-346) so the port's grounded term was already right. Original finding:  LevitateMotor.Update computes `overEncumbered = CarriedWeight * 4 > 250 && !levitating && !GodMode` and, for a swimmer who is neither climbing nor water-walking, adds `Vector3.down` INSTEAD of reading the float keys (:83-85) - the sink replaces the input, so a loaded swimmer cannot surface. SLOW FALLING DESCENDS AT 1.75 m/s WHERE DFU FALLS AT 2.1~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 movement batch 2026-08-27: SLOWFALL_VELOCITY = 105 * UNITY_FIXED_DT (0.02) = a flat 2.1 m/s, written straight to velY - the constant is coupled to UNITY's fixed step, since ApplyGravity runs inside FixedUpdate (:187, PlayerMotor.cs:275/:357) on a value spent as a velocity (`Move(moveDirection * dt)`, PlayerGroundMotor.cs:86). PlayerGPS.UpdateNearbyObjects walks EVERY active DaggerfallLoot with no scene gate (PlayerGPS.cs:747, :766-776), so in DFU the cast marks piles and corpses outdoors. In the port the same cast that works in a dungeon shows nothing while the player stands on their own dropped pile. **FX1 fed both hosts**: the world piles (droppedLoot._piles) and the lootable corpse containers (cityGuards + exteriorFoes in world.js, the guards in exterior.js) ride the loot thunk in the dungeonContext shape, and the two "no loot piles above ground" comments that justified the gap are gone | PlayerGPS.UpdateNearbyObjects (:747-777) | ~~Systems arc~~ CLOSED FX1 2026-08-28 |

| ~~NIT (F213, F214, F054)~~ **FIXED (NT1 2026-08-28)** (all three: destroy() latches `_ctxDead` FIRST and spawnCorpse's warm window reads it; the pile frees mark `dead` per droppedLoot's own removal protocol; hostMagic grew `destroy()` - retireMissile per flight, candle.clear(), the batches sweep - and dungeonContext.destroy calls it; the no-landing throw frees the built context before propagating. 9-mutation checked at test/resourcesafety.test.js (with F215)) Original finding: THREE TEARDOWN PATHS DROP ALLOCATIONS THE 17e OWNERSHIP RULE ASSIGNS THEM. (1) `dungeonContext.destroy` frees batches and clears `_piles` WITHOUT setting `dead` first (:3066-3069), which is the documented removal protocol (`droppedLoot.js:53-69`), so a pile dropped just before exit whose texture is still warming mints a batch onto an orphan; `spawnCorpse` has the same shape (:1449-1473, guarding only `!f.dead`). (2) `tryEnter` builds the whole interior context - GPU billboard batches plus voxelfolk meshes - before computing the landing and throws "no interior landing" before `interiorCtx` is assigned (`worldModes.js:2553`, :2391), and both hosts only log it, so the build leaks on every E-press at such a door. (3) `destroy()` never reaches the player magic engine's own missiles/batches lists (`hostMagic.js:84-127`, minted at `dungeonContext.js:1395`), which expose no destroy surface, so exiting a dungeon with a spell in flight leaks that missile's VAO and buffers. All three are narrow windows with no symptom short of long-session GPU growth; the rule they break is the port's own | (port-internal: DFU relies on Unity scene teardown) | Systems arc (nit) |
| ~~NIT (F215)~~ **FIXED (NT1 2026-08-28)** (the flag IS the promise - `ensure` returns `this._booted ??= this._boot(fetchBytes)`, music.js's own AUDIT 19 law, with the context-creation sync prefix preserved inside _boot; a concurrent second caller now awaits the whole archive load. Behaviorally pinned: two callers one promise, and the second caller provably unresolved until init finishes) Original finding: `AudioEngine.ensure` GUARDS ITS AWAIT WITH A BOOLEAN. `audio.js:88-107` sets `_booted = true` and returns before `await this.init(fetchBytes)` completes, so a concurrent second caller's await resolves while `enabled` is still false and its immediate one-shots drop. The sibling MusicService diagnosed and fixed exactly this at AUDIT 19 ("a guard set before its own async work is not idempotence, it is a race with a flag on it", `music.js:39-73`) and the sound engine kept the boolean. Per-frame callers self-heal, so the cost is one-shots lost inside the boot window | (port-internal: DFU's audio source is a Unity singleton) | Audio arc (nit) |

| ~~**QUEST ITEMS ARE STOOD BY THE NPC PLACEMENT LAW** (F068, F069)~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 quest/item batch 2026-08-27: standQuestFlatIn takes an `isItem` arm and the two resources are stood by DIFFERENT laws. AddQuestItem (GameObjectHelper.cs:1128-1141) NEVER rays: its dungeon shift is the CONSTANT -randomTreasureMarkerDim/2 * GlobalScale = -0.5, nothing to do with the sprite's height, so an item on a table, cage or ledge STAYS UP where the port's ray snapped it to the floor. Only AddQuestNPC aligns (:1040), and F069 moved its ray ORIGIN to the billboard's CENTRE + 0.2 (:339) from the base + 0.2 - half a sprite lower - so a tall dungeon NPC no longer starts its ray below a surface DFU clears. The questflatanchor header and two of its pins asserted the false 'AddQuestNPC and AddQuestItem both call' and were rewritten. 5-mutation checked). Original finding:  `standQuestFlatIn` applies one law to both resources - the item's own half-height anchor plus AlignBillboardToGround (`worldModes.js:557-559`) - and its comment asserts AddQuestNPC and AddQuestItem "both call" the align. AddQuestItem never rays (GameObjectHelper.cs:1116-1160): its dungeon shift is the CONSTANT `-randomTreasureMarkerDim/2 * GlobalScale` = -0.5 (:1135-1136) plus Size.y/2, and the item stands where the marker is. Only AddQuestNPC rays (:1040). So a dungeon quest item whose sprite is not 1.0 unit tall sits at the wrong height, and an item on an elevated marker - a table, a cage, a ledge - is snapped down to the floor below where DFU leaves it up. NIT half (F069): the port's ray also ORIGINATES at the bottom anchor + 0.2 where DFU rays from the CENTRE + 0.2 (:336-346), half a sprite lower, so a tall dungeon NPC sprite can miss the snap DFU performs and stand embedded | GameObjectHelper.AddQuestItem (:1116-1160) vs AddQuestNPC (:1034-1040) | Quest arc |

| ~~**THE ARMOUR MODIFIER CHANNEL IS ADDITIVE WHERE DFU'S SETTERS ARE NON-STACKING MIN-SETS** (F122, F123)~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 magic batch 2026-08-27: the one `+=` channel is now DFU's TWO min-set channels. Both setters are written as DaggerfallEntity.cs:400-416 writes them - `if (amount < standing) standing = amount` - and both are zeroed at the top of every fold, as :841-842 zeroes them. So repeats within a channel floor at -5 while the two channels stay independent, and a Strengthens item beside a BadReactionsFrom source still reaches -10 between them. F123's inert WeakensArmor is preserved bug-for-bug: `SetDecreasedArmorValueModifier(5)` cannot pass a `< 0` test from a zeroed channel, so the -700 drawback still costs the player nothing. Two consumers, not one: FormulaHelper.cs:1158 ADDS both channels into the struck part's armour value (enchantArmorMod), and RefreshArmourValues (PaperDoll.cs:161) SUBTRACTS them the other way for the paperdoll's labels (enchantArmorDisplayMod) - which retires nativeInventory's own FLAGGED note, since armorLabelValue had carried the parameter for that number since AUDIT 17e F36 with nothing to feed it. 11-mutation checked). Original finding:  DaggerfallEntity's SetIncreasedArmorValueModifier and SetDecreasedArmorValueModifier assign only when the new value beats the standing one and the modifiers are cleared every constant-effects pass (:400-416, :842), which has two consequences the port does not reproduce. (a) Any number of StrengthensArmor items yields exactly -5 in DFU and multiple BadReactionsFrom items likewise floor at -5 (their ChanceToHit term IS additive, :418-421); the port folds each row into `mods.armorMod` with `+=` (`enchantments.js:444`, :542), so two Strengthens items give -10 and the player is measurably harder to hit than DFU allows. (b) WeakensArmor calls `SetDecreasedArmorValueModifier(5)` and `5 < 0` is never true, so the enchantment is INERT in DFU - the port adds +5 into the same channel each round and `combat/formulas.js:313` hands it to the attacker's hit chance, so the drawback the player took for -700 enchantment budget costs defence only here | DaggerfallEntity armour modifier setters (:400-416) | Systems arc |

LayoutNature expands the location rect by natureClearance 4 only when the un-expanded rect has `x > 0 && y > 0` (TerrainNature.cs:60-66), and then RE-EVALUATES `rect.x > 0 && rect.y > 0 && rect.Contains(tilePos)` on the EXPANDED rect inside the tile loop (:124) - so when the expansion pushes a min to <= 0, DFU disables the containment test entirely and scatters nature across the town footprint. | ~~**THE EXTERIOR AMBIENCE LOOP FOLLOWS THE PLAYER INDOORS - AND THAT IS NOT DFU** (F088)~~ **REFUTED 2026-08-27 (AUDIT 26 audio batch) - the carry-over IS DFU and stands.** The row's case rests on a SCENE-HIERARCHY claim (that AmbientEffectsPlayer hangs off PlayerEnterExit.ExteriorParent, so DisableAllParents silences it). That claim cannot be read from DFU's source at all - the .unity scenes are not in the tree - and the CODE refutes it: AmbientEffectsPlayer.Update's water arm (:165-175) acts only when `playerEnterExit.blockWaterLevel != 10000`, and blockWaterLevel is assigned in exactly one place, inside `if (dungeon && isPlayerInsideDungeon)` (PlayerEnterExit.cs:328-343). Were the component deactivated on entering a dungeon, that whole arm - its DFRandom rolls, its surface positioning, its classic cadence - could never run; the same goes for the cemetery guard's `!IsPlayerInside` (:154). Update's only early return is IsMuted (:109-110). So nothing stops the loop and the port is right to carry it. The row's attack on the ORIGINAL comment's weak argument was fair - the cemetery guard proved little - so ambientEffects.js now carries the decisive water-arm evidence instead, and this is the SECOND challenge to the same decision, recorded so there is not a third. Original finding:  This CORRECTS the struck rain-loop row above, which read the WeatherManager half correctly and stopped one component short. WeatherAmbientEffects is a child of the scene's Exterior object (PlayerEnterExit.ExteriorParent), and TransitionInterior/TransitionDungeonInterior call DisableAllParents, which does `ExteriorParent.SetActive(false)` (:797, :931, :999, :1037-1050), stopping its AudioSources - OnDisable then clears the loop handles (:96-100). So DFU goes SILENT indoors even though its Presets field still says Rain. The port never stops the loop: `world.js:14623-14666` and `exterior.js:4514-4552` return on modal frames before the ambience update (`world.js:15602`, `exterior.js:5025`), and `ambientEffects.js:155-182` defends the carry-over as bug-for-bug fidelity. Enter a tavern or a dungeon in the rain, or at night with the crickets up, and the outdoor loop plays for the whole visit. **DISC6-C (2026-09-23): for a BUILDING the port now departs on purpose - the loop is the street heard through the walls, at a Ledger A gain (THE STREET'S AMBIENCE INDOORS); underground this row's verdict stands unchanged.** | PlayerEnterExit.DisableAllParents (:1037-1050) | Audio arc |

Armed and disarmed from the LOCATION RECT edge in world.js - the very transition pair AUDIT 26 F062 built this session for the crime clear, which is where DFU's OnEnterLocationRect/OnExitLocationRect fire. 5-mutation checked, including one that made the pin watch a single clip where the rng drew another). The port has the flag comment and nothing else (`ambientEffects.js:34`): grep finds no reference to clip 113 or 14, no cemetery wait and no rect arming anywhere in `src/`. Graveyards sound like ordinary wilderness | AmbientEffectsPlayer cemetery channel (:45-50, :518-529) | Audio arc |

The count gate the old arm kept in front of the bar is gone with it - DFU's bar is a panel child whether or not it painted, so a press on a list that FITS falls into the `>` arm and SetScrollIndex clamps it away. The SAME slice closed the two other consumers still short of the seam: `ui/spellIconPickerWindow.js`'s scroller (SpellIconPickerWindow.cs:91-94) had the clamp alone - a dead rail, no drag, and a grey trough with a grey block that DFU never draws, since Draw returns before DrawScrollBar when the content fits and that scroller sets no BackgroundColor - and `ui/chargen.js`'s picker bar dropped its latch on the NEXT MOVE (`releasePickBar`, forwarded by `systems/chargenSession.js`). One host was short the seam's other half and is fixed here too: `scenes/interior.js` routed its overlay's hover WITHOUT the DOM event, so a window mounted there could latch a thumb and never move it. Pinned by `test/roadg_g4_dragrelease.test.js` (12, 19 mutations), including the hosts' (-1,-1) sentinel kept out of ALL FIVE drag machines (the wizard's bar was left unguarded when this row was written and the ROAD-G G4 review found it - `ui/chargen.js`'s `hover` carries the same `vy >= 0` arm as the spellbook's now, the icon picker's being the two-part `vx >= 0 && vy >= 0`; AUDIT 62 F24/F25 then found the shared `ui/listPicker.js` - the very shape those machines cite as their model - and `ui/bankPurchaseWindow.js` still unguarded, and G4-12 drives all five). 8-mutation checked, including the per-frame re-pin, the count-gate drop, and the self-referential-constant trap). | ~~**THE SPELL MAKER IGNORES THE CHOSEN EFFECTS' ALLOWED TARGETS AND ELEMENTS** (F181)~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 spell-maker batch 2026-08-27: UpdateAllowedButtons ported whole, by the two OPPOSITE rules this row's own text ran together and was corrected in place for earlier today. Targets INTERSECT from TargetFlags_All (:585), so one caster-only effect narrows the whole spell; ELEMENTS UNION from ElementFlags_MagicOnly (:588), so magic is always offered and each effect only adds. The window holds both sets, recomputes on every slot change as DFU does at :226/:338/:396/:462/:1005, and EnforceSelectedButtons moves an outlawed selection to the FIRST legal value in declaration order. The default arm FORCES CasterOnly + Magic rather than merely enforcing (:564-570), so deleting the last effect snaps an Area At Range selection back even though the default set still allows it. Where DFU has five buttons whose SetSpell* returns early on an illegal bit, the port has one cycling row, so the cycle STEPS OVER a disallowed value - the player can no more land on one here than select one there. The per-effect target flags are a new table read off every effect class; the ELEMENT half needed no table at all, since MAGIC_ONLY_KEYS already is that set for the GetElementType law - the two derivations were cross-checked key for key, 72 of 72, and agree exactly. 18-mutation checked, all caught first pass. spellmakerwindow's cycle pin was rewritten: it read the empty sheet's element wrap as freedom, where DFU's default flags leave Magic the only legal element). Original finding:  DFU restricts both: SetSpellTarget returns early when the target bit is not in allowedTargets (:499-501), SetSpellElement likewise (:527-530), and UpdateAllowedButtons forces the selection back to the first legal value (:563-601). CORRECTED 2026-08-27 while the rest of this cluster was fixed: the two flag sets are combined by OPPOSITE rules, which this row ran together. Targets are the INTERSECTION of each chosen effect's AllowedTargets, seeded TargetFlags_All (:585); ELEMENTS are the UNION, seeded ElementFlags_MagicOnly, so magic is always offered and each effect only ADDS to what is legal (:588). With no effects chosen at all the window falls back to defaultTargetFlags/defaultElementFlags and forces CasterOnly + Magic (:564-570). `_inputMain` cycles rangeType and element freely through all five values (`spellMakerWindow.js:187-190`) and no allowedTargets or allowedElements state exists anywhere in `src/`. The port will price and inscribe spells DFU cannot mint - an "Area at Range" built from a caster-only effect - with the target semantics and costs that go with them | DaggerfallSpellMakerWindow.UpdateAllowedButtons (:499-601) | UI arc |
| ~~**TWO HUD VITALS SETTINGS ARE SURFACED AND CONSUMED BY NOTHING** (F148, F149)~~ FIXED (VB1 2026-08-28: ui/hudVitals.js carries all three members whole - HUDVitals' nine-bar rig, VerticalProgressSmoother's 0.4s lerp with its two entry delays (-0.5s cold, -0.25s re-armed), and VitalsChangeDetector's previous-value snapshot with its max-change reset and pause gate. EnableVitalsIndicators ships TRUE, so the damage trail is now the DEFAULT look: on a loss the main bar drops by LostPercent AT ONCE and the dark loss bar lags shut behind it; on a gain the loss bar jumps by GainPercent (-1 * LostPercent) and the main bar climbs; the bright gain bars never smooth-change. Draw order is Components.Add's - losses behind, gains between, art mains on top. TWO RIGS, ONE DETECTOR, which is DFU's own shape (HUDLarge.cs:66 owns a second HUDVitals): both rigs take every change event, only the enabled one cycles, and a HUD-mode flip unprimes the detector exactly as DaggerfallHUD_OnLargeHUDToggle resets it - the idle rig's trail would otherwise stand stale. The SWAP (F149) exchanges the health/fatigue ART and their loss/gain colours with magicka untouched, applied as a draw-time re-mapping (vitalsSkin) so both HUDs swap together; DFU reads it once at construction, the port per frame - the largeHudEnabled posture, recorded in the module header. The detector's IsGamePaused gate rides the active cursor, its nearest port seam; DFU's other reset triggers (world init, load, court) need no seam because those paths boot a host afresh. Both keys are LIVE-tiered with hudVitals.js as consumer. 18 mutations, 18 killed - including the toggle reset, whose absence is masked by the both-rigs law until the enabled trail has closed, and the large HUD's draw arm, which a source regex alone could not kill). Original finding:  HUDVitals adds six indicator bars when EnableVitalsIndicators is true - smoothed loss trails and gain bars driven by the change detector (:99-115, :276-312) - and takes SynchronizeImmediately only when it is FALSE (:211-214); DFU ships it TRUE, so the port's three plain bars (`hud.js:302-312`) are the setting-false path and the damage/heal trail never appears. LoadAssets also swaps the health and fatigue bar art (MAIN03I0/MAIN04I0) and their indicator colours when SwapHealthAndFatigueColors is set (:185-202); `loadHud` maps them unconditionally (`hud.js:197`) and `hudLarge.js:357-360` reuses the same unswapped art. Both keys are surfaced on the settings screen and read by no file in `src/`, so two toggles a player can turn on do nothing - and neither was recorded anywhere | HUDVitals (:99-115, :182-199, :212-215) | UI arc |

## D. THE BOARD (next up, in order)

Logged 2026-08-25, at the end of the X11c/T2/T3/P1/H3 run. Section C is
the standing GAP list and answers "what is not ported"; this is the
much shorter WORKING list and answers "what should be picked up next".
Every entry below was re-verified against the tree the day it was
written, in the L1 discipline - a stale board sends the next slice to
build what already ships.

**The rule that keeps this honest: close a row here by striking it and
naming the slice, the same way section C rows are closed. If an entry
is still open after a slice that touched its area, say why.**

### 1. ~~The bank purchase window's 3D model preview~~ SHIPPED (H4 2026-08-27)

The 104x91 panel shows the selected building's own ARCH3D model live,
rotating -1 degree per 0.02s (the window's clock, real-time
accumulated). The split: the WINDOW owns the rotation and the camera
law (houses from (0,3,-20), near 0.7 far 100, Unity's default
60-degree lens; the ships arm's (0,12,SHIP_CAMERA_DIST) pairs sit in
banking.js beside the model ids for the day a ships list exists) and
worldModes owns drawBankModelPreview - the automap's second-beginFrame
precedent cut down to a panel: scissor BEFORE beginFrame so the clear
touches only the display rect, viewport AFTER it (beginFrame sets the
full one), the ONE mirror on the projection, the mesh through the
pipeline's own async getGpuMesh, clear color borrowed and returned.
RECORDED: DFU climate-swaps the preview's textures and lights it with
a 0.4 directional + hard shadows; the port draws base textures under
the collapsed-ambient idiom the automap records. Pins in
`test/bankpreview.test.js`.

### 2. ~~The sixteen `.FLC` summoning videos~~ SHIPPED (G7b 2026-08-27)

`ui/daedraSummonedWindow.js` is DaggerfallDaedraSummonedWindow: the
prince's own film fullscreen and LOOPING (FLCPlayer.Loop's default)
over the F1/F2a reader the constellations built, the quest offer read
over it in FOUR-LINE chunks (a click or any key turns the page), the
last chunk answering to Yes/No through the offer flow's REAL respond -
so accept runs startQuestImmediate and refuse runs the rumor/topic
sweeps - and the answer's own message reading through the same chunks
before the last click closes. ~~The refusal's 3-5 daedra ride a
spawnRefusalFoes door FLAGGED unmounted~~ (the IF slice mounted the
door with its interior pool; CV1 2026-08-27 verified both punishment
arms' numbers against the C# and closed the F194 row with them). The host mounts ONE consumer
of the offer step: the film window when the FLC loads, the box chain
when it cannot - never traps.

### 3. ~~Morph Self, the last inert effect~~ TAKEN (V2a 2026-08-27)

Whoever takes it takes lycanthropy - and V2a did exactly that: the
racial override shipped (`systems/lycanthropy.js`, the row above
narrows in full) and Morph Self's arm rides the ONE cast engine. The
gated inert figure reads ZERO.

### 5. ~~Save screenshots - the capture half~~ SHIPPED (SS1 2026-08-27)

The save ARMS a request (`requestScreenshot`, on `saveSlot` success in
both composers) and the host frame loop DELIVERS it after its last
draw (`capturePendingScreenshot` in both tails of world.js's and
dungeon.js's loops - preserveDrawingBuffer false makes the buffer
same-task-only), with a two-frame countdown standing in for SaveGame's
two WaitForEndOfFrame yields so the save window has popped, HUD in
shot as the C# leaves it. The WINDOW loads the shot itself
(GetSaveScreenshot -> the panel texture, `_shotTexture`'s (key, url)
cache released on change); `hooks.screenshotTexture` stays the
override seam. RECORDED departure: DFU stores the full-resolution
JPEG on disk, the port downscales to 320x200 for the localStorage
quota. 13 pins mutation-run and killed
(`test/savescreenshot.test.js`).

### 9. `no-unused-vars` is OFF for `src/**`, and 134 imports are dead (AUDIT-WH L4, 2026-09-21)

`eslint.config.js` runs `no-undef`, `no-dupe-keys`,
`no-dupe-class-members` and `no-unsafe-negation` over `src/**` and
does NOT run `no-unused-vars` - the server lane does. The world-hover
audit found the consequence the way these things are always found:
`world.js` imported `destroyWorldPlaque` and never called it, so the
plaque outlived its host, and nothing in the tree could see the dead
import. It is fixed; the RULE is not.

Measured on the tree the day this was written: turning the rule on for
`src/**` reports **215 sites, of which 134 are unused IMPORTS** across
about sixty files. The imports are the half that matters - a dead
import is a claim about what a module depends on - and they are not
safe to delete mechanically: a file whose ONLY import from a module is
unused loses that module's load, and a handful of this port's modules
do work at load (a registry that registers itself, a style that
injects). Each one needs a look.

The 81 unused LOCALS are a different judgement again: some are
deliberate (a destructure that names a field to say it exists), and
mass-deleting them is how a subtle behaviour goes quietly.

So this is a slice, not a flag: turn the rule on, walk the 134 imports
with the load-order question asked of each, and decide the locals one
at a time. It is bounded, it is mechanical, and it closes a whole class
the tree currently cannot see.

## Ledger A (continued) - the browser tab's own hazards

Added at the END of this file on purpose: every identifier and line
number above is cited from the tree, and a row inserted among them
renumbers three gates' worth of bookkeeping for nothing. A row's place
in the file is not a claim about its importance.

| What | Ours | Approved via |

| THE BOOT'S PARAMS ARE THE URL (MAC-N3, 2026-09-16, Mac: "Chat UI not visable with classic in online mode") | Daggerfall Unity has no URL; the port's front door decides six keys per choice (`load`, `online`, `loadkey`, `test`, `classic`, `classicload`) on a `URLSearchParams` copy of `location.search` and hands the copy to the world host - while the online lane (`systems/onlineLane.js isOnlinePage`, the read under `uiSkin`, `getPref` and `modSetting`) reads `location.search` itself. Two copies of one page's parameters, and the lane read the one nobody wrote: OL1 was dead for the Play Online button. `publishBootParams` writes the decided copy to the URL (`history.replaceState`, the menu's own bare-value spelling) before either world boot the door reaches, and the door clears the six keys off both copies before the menu that decides them runs. A refusing history is warned about and not fatal. | Mac's call, 2026-09-16 (OL1's own contract, kept) |
| THE BROWSER TAB'S OWN DOORS (MAC-L3, 2026-09-16, Mac relaying Orion: "right click in general seems to cause either a new window to open, or for the page to refresh to the menu, causing unsaved progress to be lost") | Daggerfall Unity is not in a browser tab, so two of its hazards have no DFU member at all and are the port's own. **The context menu**: the right button is a WEAPON control here (classic swings by dragging it), and four hosts suppressed `contextmenu` on their CANVAS while thirteen surfaces are appended to `document.body` and two of those shut it themselves - so the browser's menu opened over the pause screen, the pack, the spellbook and the sheet. One capture-phase listener on the document now (`ui/input.js`'s `installContextMenuGuard`), with text entry excepted so the chat and online-name fields keep their paste. **The unload**: `src/systems/unloadGuard.js`, because the port had no `beforeunload` anywhere and every gesture a browser reads as Back - a right-drag or a rocker, the Back button, a trackpad swipe, Ctrl-W - took a running session in silence. It ASKS and does not save: `beforeunload` gives no time for async work and a corrupt slot is worse than a stale one. Many arms and one answer (THE FOUR HOSTS are not one at a time - the world host hands its interiors to the mode machine), and `exitToTitleMenu` stands them all down before it navigates, because prompting a player for the door they just pressed trains them to click through the prompt that matters. | Mac's call, 2026-09-16 |
| **OBLIVION REMASTER LIKE LEVELING, 1:1 WITH PERMISSION (ORL1, 2026-09-17)** - `vendor/oblivion-remaster-leveling/`, `systems/oblivionLeveling.js`, `ui/virtueLevelUp.js`, `ui/levelingChoice.js`, the branch in `systems/advancement.js`, the door in `ui/charSheetDoor.js`, the stand-down in `ui/charsheet.js`, the question in `systems/chargenSession.js`. **THE FIRST MORROWIND MOD IN THE TREE** - every other vendored MOD is a Daggerfall Unity mod, all but one of them a `.dfmod` bundle; this is OpenMW Lua, so the port is read off the author's own shipped source line by line rather than off a decompile, and its whole data layer is ONE GMST in a 487-byte `.omwaddon` (`iLevelupTotal` = 100), parsed by the pin rather than trusted. AN ALTERNATIVE LEVELING SYSTEM, CHOSEN PER CHARACTER AT CREATION (Mac: "On new game, I want the player to receive a notification on which leveling system they would like to use"): every skill raise fills a 100-point bar by its tier, the overflow carries into the next level, and levelling up hands a PURSE OF VIRTUES - twelve points across at most three attributes, five apiece, Luck priced at four - in place of Daggerfall's 4..6 bonus pool. `entity.levelingSystem` rides the SAVE, not the mod store: it is a property of the character, and a save written before this slice has no field and reads as classic, so no existing character changes. SIX DEPARTURES, all in `06-Systems/Oblivion-Remaster-Leveling.md`: (1) **Daggerfall's own hit-points-per-level** in place of the mod's `Endurance * fLevelUpHealthEndMult`, which is MORROWIND'S ENGINE rule the mod restates because it took the dialog over, not a thing the mod invented; (2) a fourth impact knob (`primarySkillsImpact`) because Daggerfall has a tier of chosen skills Morrowind does not - primary and major both ship at the mod's 8, minor at its 6, misc at its 2, the tiers matched by COUNT; (3) **every maximum in the settings is the port's** (OpenMW declares only minima and the port's store needs both bounds or a numeric key reads as a boolean); (4) the mod's LOOK is not ported - its Morrowind icons, level-up pictures, GMST tooltips and triumph music have no Daggerfall twin, so both screens are the text idiom `LevelUpScreen` already ships in and neither claims native geometry it has no source to cite - **NARROWED (LV1, 2026-09-18):** in the ENHANCED skin the mod's level-up is drawn by the port's own constellation window (`ui/enhancedLevelUp.js`) instead. That is still not the mod's LOOK, which stays in Morrowind: it is the port's own, on the enhanced skin's procedural sky, and it drives `VirtueLevelUpScreen` through that screen's own `input` rather than restating a line of the mod's law. The text idiom is what the CLASSIC skin draws, and the leveling QUESTION is text in both; (5) the Oghma Infinium stays on Daggerfall's own rollout in both lanes - that artefact is DAGGERFALL'S OWN, a book the mod has never heard of, so its pool is DFU's law whichever system the character levels by. **RE-SWEPT (the deep audit):** this row, the page and two comments all justified it a second way, that ~~thirty points could not be spent under a three-attribute cap anyway~~ - which is FALSE at the mod's shipped defaults, where three rows at five apiece with Luck priced at four is exactly thirty and `virtueSpendPlan` plans it. The departure never needed that argument; (6) **A SECOND CLAMP ON THE PURSE, because the mod's own can hand out points it will not let you spend**: `calculateAttributepoints` never looks at `maxUpdatableAttribute`, so a late-game character can be minted a purse three rows cannot absorb and the window - which refuses to close on an unspent point - becomes a wall. `modVirtuePurse` is the mod's arithmetic kept whole; `virtuePurse` clamps it to the most a legal spend can pay for, solved exactly (`virtueSpendPlan`). **THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT CLAMP WAS A WORSE BUG THAN THE ONE IT FIXED** and the adversarial review found it: it measured the purse with a lowest-value-first greedy, which at the ceiling spends the row budget on the rows with the least headroom - eight attributes at 98, at the shipped defaults, mints 12, can legally take all 12, and was handed 6. Half a level-up destroyed. The pin then asked only that the purse be spendable to zero, which 6 satisfies, so it could not see it; it asks for the maximum now, against an independent exhaustive search. **THEN A DEEP AUDIT (2026-09-17, eight lenses, against a FROZEN tree this time) found the fixes had left three things worth a fraction of what they looked like.** The question PAINTED from the canvas origin and HIT-TESTED in letterboxed native units, so through townTalk - world.js's and exterior.js's own pointer route - a click on the painted Oblivion option selected DAGGERFALL on 27% of its area at 1366x768, 29% at 1024x768 and 59% at 800x600 (where the Daggerfall option answered 3% of its own clicks): the wrong leveling system, silently, on the one screen a character cannot come back to. The exact solver maximised COST and kept the fewest-rows plan, so twelve of purse bought LUCK +3 - three attribute points where twelve were affordable - on every headless path. And `checkForVirtueLevelUp` refused to re-offer an owed level, where DFU's own check re-offers on every pass precisely because the hosts' one overlay slot may be busy: measured over six skill passes, six offers on the classic lane against one here. The level-up window also had no pointer seam at all, so in the classic skin a mouse-only player could open it and never close it. Both screens draw through `nativeMetrics` now, the DP carries its point total, the check re-offers, and the window has three labelled presses. ELEVEN RECORD ERRORS with them, two of them arguments rather than typos: departure 5's "thirty points cannot be spent anyway" is false at the mod's own defaults, and "OpenMW's number renderer takes a min and no max" is contradicted by OpenMW 0.49's own renderers.lua. The root of nearly all of it was one pin-shaped hole: NEITHER NEW SCREEN'S `draw` HAD EVER BEEN CALLED, so thirteen draw mutations survived, `draw() { return; }` among them. RECORDED, NOT FIXED: the port's `1.04^level` skill-advancement term means the two systems do not pace skills identically at the same level, and the online lane forces every mod's `Enabled` true, so an online character is always ASKED the question (they may still answer Daggerfall). Both are Mac's to rule on. The seam is gone; a row CLICK selects, which is the one rule the window's own comment always stated. And removing `checkForVirtueLevelUp`'s guard also removed what kept it off the OGHMA INFINIUM, whose pool is Daggerfall's own: with a full bar under a pending book the headless arm took a Level++ and a health roll the book forbids and left `oghmaLevelUp` latched. The exclusion the door, the sheet and both interior arms already carry is the fourth place it belongs. The pin that was supposed to hold the pointer TELEPORTED between points, which is why it saw neither; it walks the line a mouse walks now | Mac, 2026-09-17 ("So I've been analyzing morrowind mods to integrate and the first one I'd like to add is this"), recorded in `06-Systems/Oblivion-Remaster-Leveling.md`; **the permission record AND THE AUTHOR'S NAME are open** in `vendor/oblivion-remaster-leveling/README.md` - the archive states no licence and names nobody | ORL1 |

| **THE LANTERNS' GLOW THROUGH THEIR SHADOWS (VOL1, 2026-09-23, Mac: "Continue" - the second arc's last step; BOUNCE1 beside it, withdrawn by AUDIT REACH the same day)** - `volFs` / `volToneFs` / `VOLBLUR_FS` / `_volumetrics` / `_blank` / `fresh` in `render/airPass.js`, `pointShadowOne` in the shadow block, `EL_TONEMAP_GLSL` / `EL_SCATTER_GLSL` (the chord) and the door in `render/enhancedLighting.js`, `setVolumetrics` / `_airGlows` in `render/renderer.js` | Daggerfall Unity has no volumetric light: its lanterns are Unity point lights with no scattering | THE DEPARTURE, the lane only: the lanterns' in-scatter, which EL1 added per fragment unshadowed, is marched at the bloom's size through each lantern's own cube map over the ray's chord (the closed form's integrand, jittered, linear light blurred by depth in a float target and tonemapped once, added at the resolve and seen by the eye and the bloom) - a wall throws its shadow into the air. A per-lantern bounce was tried and withdrawn: without visibility from the lit floor it leaks or fills nothing; real bounce is a reflective shadow map, its own step. Seen on SwiftShader by `tools/volumetricProbe.mjs` (the glow as a difference from a no-glow baseline: the sky, a dark far floor within a fifth, a shadowed front under a third). `test/vol1_glow.test.js` (3) |
